djinterp math: the lane's decisions, S1 to S504

Every decision the math lane has taken, in the words of the record that took it, with the alternative it set aside. The numbers run across the lane's records from S1 (slice 3, 2026.10.08); each record's section of decisions is here as its README has it, its own first lines among it. A decision was the lane's to take within the owner's rulings, and stands once his next word on its delivery, his "continue", has accepted it: S1 to S499 stand, his own rulings of 2026.10.27 on complex numbers and quaternions, S265 to S278, the design of steps 22 to 24, and his choice of 2026.11.07, S383, among them. His rulings of 2026.11.03 and 2026.11.04 on the flagged decisions are in the reviews they rule, each "Ruled 2026.11.04", two of them, S336 and S340, his own choice of an alternative the review had set aside, made in chunk 46 (S384, S385). His rulings of 2026.11.16 on the decisions of 2026.11.15 are in chunk 51's opening lines and, for S391, S403 and S413, in their reviews, each "Ruled 2026.11.16", and each done in chunk 53; chunk 51's decisions, the first of the double family's rewrite as he ruled it, stand by his "continue for now" of 2026.11.20, and chunk 52's, the second, log_c correctly rounded, by his "continue" of the same day, and chunk 53's, step 29 (5a) with 0.8's items as he ruled them, by his "continue" of 2026.11.21, and chunk 54's, log_c 1.7 to 1.85 times as fast, by his "continue" of the same day. Chunks 55's to 58's stand by his "continue" of 2026.11.23 at 19:54: chunk 55's, math's largest files split, as he ruled on 2026.11.21, each old name kept - the eight largest generated headers, the language's matrix kind, its face test and the generator - and the delivery over his main of 2026.11.21 with chunk 52 laid over it, without the records; chunk 56's, step 30 (5b), function.hpp, functional.hpp and values.hpp from C++11, with two of their own failures fixed; chunk 57's, chunks 55's and 56's zips cut again on his push of 2026.11.23, the lane's prose and the generator's strings with one hyphen for a dash; and chunk 58's, step 31 (5c), the calculus headers from C++11, and with them step 33 (5e), math.hpp at every rung; and chunk 59's, step 32 (5d), reify, a compile-time expression made a term of the run-time language, by his "continue" of 21:32 the same day; and chunk 60's, step 34 (5f), the opt-in lazy type of linear algebra, by his "continue" of 2026.11.24 at 08:13; and chunk 61's, step 35 (5g), the compile-time headers' style debt, which closes Phase 5, by his "continue" of 12:34 the same day, S473 as his word of 10:30 ruled it, its first alternative; and chunk 62's, the banners' HOW TO USE, OPTIONS and CONFIGURATION in every header of math, by his rulings of 14:20 and 14:37, S476 as he ruled it, its alternative; and chunk 63's, the plain double exp correctly rounded, as his ruling 12 has it, and chunk 62's objects compared, by his rulings of 21:16, S482 as he ruled it, its alternative; and chunk 64's, the scalar helpers of ruling 13 and the terps' needs numbered as Phase 9, steps 52 to 62, by his "continue" of 2026.11.25; and chunk 65's, step 53, exp's speed step and the fast exp his note on S478 asked for, and S359's precedent corrected, by his rulings of 08:38 the same day, each as recommended; and chunk 66's, step 54, log_c's speed step and the plain double log correctly rounded, as ruling 12 has it, by his rulings of 14:42 the same day, each as recommended. His rulings 12 to 15 of 2026.11.24 are in chunk 63's opening lines. S500 to S504 are chunk 67's, open until his word on them: d_math_d_log_fast, a fast log beside log_c, as his decision 71 of 14:42 asked, before step 55. His word of 2026.11.14 on the language's names, which kept them flat in djinterp::math, is S397's. Where a later decision changes an earlier one, the later one says so; the earlier one stays here as it was taken.

A decision names files as they were when it was taken. Chunk 48 merged the two quaternion classes (S398) and renamed or split five of the language's headers (S400, S401): expr/complex.hpp is expr/complex_kind.hpp, expr/quaternion.hpp expr/quaternion_kind.hpp, expr/series.hpp expr/summation.hpp; expr/matrix.hpp became expr/matrix_kind.hpp and expr/matrix_function.hpp, and expr/function.hpp expr/function_table.hpp, expr/arithmetic.hpp and expr/logic.hpp; linalg::quaternion's to_matrix is to_matrix3 in both names. Chunk 55 split the largest files (S436 to S439): each old name is still a header, which includes its parts. A record's text is here as the record has it, but for the merge window's rewordings of two of chunk 51's lines and the lane-name check's of a line each of chunks 52's and 53's (S440), and the owner's push of 2026.11.23, which gave every dash one hyphen and reworded a line of chunk 52's (S447). expr/expr_common.hpp's banner maps the language's headers as they are.

Each decision is followed by its review, written from chunk 34 on at the owner's asking, each chunk reviewing its own: what the path taken gains and what it costs, beside the alternatives, and the path the review recommends, judged on accuracy and functionality, the best solution rather than the cheapest. A review ends "Taken" where the path taken is the one it recommends, or "Ruled" where the owner ruled it; it ends in a flag where the path taken is not the one it recommends, or has a significant drawback. The 21 flagged decisions, the outstanding ones, come first, in five groups by what they concern, the most urgent first in each (section 1); then every decision, by record (section 2).

The questions put to the owner before the S numbers began, M0 to M3, C1 to C6 and N1 to N6, are in the consolidation's record (_handoffs/consolidate_2026.10.07/README.md, section 5) and the notes that followed it; his rulings on them are the roadmap's principles and items (roadmap.html). From chunk 34 on, each chunk's delivery brings this file, its own decisions added at the end. This file is made by _handoffs/math98_chunk67_2026.11.25/tools/decisions_c67.py.

1. Outstanding decisions

The 21 flagged decisions: those whose review recommends what is not yet done, or names a significant drawback of what was. Each gives when it is due, by the roadmap's steps, its flag and its review's recommendation; the decision itself and its whole review are in its record's part of section 2. The groups and the steps are decisions_outstanding_c67.md's, in chunk 67's record.

1.1 The expression language

  • S190. When: before step 25 (4a): decided on 2026.11.06 with lane F, against expression core v2. Not the recommendation; a growing cost. Recommended: the alternative, before Phase 4's rewrites and any further operator make the layer larger. Ruled 2026.11.04: decided on 2026.11.06 with lane F against expression core v2's design: core v2 if its parser holds postfix operators and calls, S209 and S252 then following it; otherwise the language's own precedence parser, as S190's review recommends.
  • S209. When: with S190. Follows from S190. Recommended: S190's alternative would make it unnecessary. Ruled 2026.11.04: decided on 2026.11.06 with lane F against expression core v2's design: core v2 if its parser holds postfix operators and calls, S209 and S252 then following it; otherwise the language's own precedence parser, as S190's review recommends.
  • S252. When: with S190. Follows from S190. Recommended: as taken within S190's shared climber, whose own alternative, a precedence parser of the language's, would hold postfix operators with operands as it holds calls. Ruled 2026.11.04: decided on 2026.11.06 with lane F against expression core v2's design: core v2 if its parser holds postfix operators and calls, S209 and S252 then following it; otherwise the language's own precedence parser, as S190's review recommends.
  • S203. When: step 36 (6a): gamma, lgamma, erf and erfc with their kernels and the correctly rounded forms (S379). Functionality gap, now gamma, erf and the rest alone. Recommended: those five in the table in step 23 (S271), and min, max and sign with them (exact, no kernel needed), sign(z) being z / |z| of a complex z; gamma, erf and the rest with their kernels; chunk 40 placed the eight in step 23's second part, chunk 41 (S324), which put them in the table (S338), each with its complex value but the next chunk's two (S335); gamma, erf and the rest wait for their kernels, which no step has yet. Ruled 2026.11.04: gamma, erf and the rest wait for their kernels, the lane to place them: at step 36 (6a), gamma, lgamma, erf and erfc, with the correctly rounded forms (S379).
  • S220. When: at any step that touches the folds' logic; minor. Not the recommendation; a minor point, changeable later, as it makes an error a value. Recommended: the alternative, the identities, with piecewise kept at one argument at least. Ruled 2026.11.04: the alternative, as recommended, at any step that touches the folds' logic.
  • S272. When: the option on every evaluator since chunk 46 (S385); a documented drawback, nothing due. Significant drawback, documented: a stray negative goes complex silently unless the option is set. Recommended: as ruled, the option given with the kind's functions, in step 23's second part, chunk 41 (S324), which gave it, expr_context's real_results (S340). Ruled 2026.11.04: S272 follows S340's ruling, the option on each function's evaluator, made in chunk 46 (S385).

1.2 Simplification and variables

  • S251. When: step 25 (4a), with declared domains (S274), as ruled. Significant drawback for variables bound to matrices. Recommended: declared domains (S274), declared matrix variables among them, as SymPy's MatrixSymbol and its non-commutative symbols are: a binding's or the syntax's mark that a name is a matrix, of a shape where known, or a quaternion, which simplification then treats as a literal is treated now, its powers' identities included; in step 25 (4a), or sooner where a user simplifies terms of matrix variables. Ruled 2026.11.04: declared domains, matrix variables among them, at step 25 (4a); S376's variables beside quaternions with them.
  • S376. When: step 25 (4a), with S251's declared domains. Significant drawback until 4a: a variable bound to a number that does not commute with a quaternion's factors gets another value from a simplified term. Recommended: as taken, with S251's declared domains at step 25 (4a), a variable declared complex or a quaternion then keeping its place.

1.3 Precision and one answer

  • S15. When: step 36 (6a), where one answer becomes bit-identity; sooner at no cost. Not the recommendation; two answers across levels. Recommended: the library's functions wherever <math.h> declares them, the _c forms only where it does not. Ruled 2026.11.04 (the owner's, settled): as recommended, one answer per platform, at step 36 (6a).
  • S460. When: as ruling 12 (made 2026.11.24) switches each plain function: a reified term's sin, cos, tan and atan the plain functions, one ulp from the C core's correctly rounded kernels at 3 to 56 of 20,000 arguments each; exp the kernel since chunk 63 (S478), log since chunk 66 (S496). Until each plain function switches, a reified term's sin, cos, tan and atan can differ from the expression's in the last bit, the expression's being the right one. Recommended: as taken, the plain functions made the correctly rounded kernels as ruling 12 decides. Taken in part: ruling 12, made 2026.11.24, switched the plain exp in chunk 63, and a reified term's exp is now the expression's, bit for bit (S478, S480); the plain log switched in chunk 66, and a reified term's log is the expression's too (S496, S498).
  • S9. When: step 39 (6d), with MSVC. Significant drawback on compilers without the builtin. Recommended: as taken, plus std::bit_cast from C++20 where the builtin is missing, so every C++20 compiler gets it. Taken in part. Ruled 2026.11.04: as recommended, at step 39 (6d).
  • S37. When: step 39 (6d), where clang on the x87 is taken up; documented until then. Significant drawback on clang's x87, documented. Recommended: as taken, given that C allows no fix inside a constant expression; keep the SSE advice prominent. Taken. Ruled 2026.11.04: as taken, the SSE advice kept prominent.
  • S39. When: step 39 (6d), with the x87. Not the recommendation. Recommended: the alternative, as S38 does for the roots. Ruled 2026.11.04: the alternative, as recommended, at step 39 (6d).
  • S370. When: step 40 (6e): chunk 42's whole power, in the family's own type, a _fast form where measured demand asks for one. Significant drawback: the plain forms' whole powers cost 2.0 to 3.5 times chunk 44's time for double and 3.3 to 6.2 for float, n from 2 to 100. Recommended: as taken, every part within its bound before a power is fast, as the roadmap's seventh principle has it; chunk 42's squaring in the family's own type, the faster, kept for a _fast form at step 40 (6e), where measured demand asks for one.
  • S20. When: at any step that touches power.h; minor. Not the recommendation (minor). Recommended: the alternative, at its small cost. Ruled 2026.11.04: the alternative, as recommended, at any step that touches power.h.

1.4 Linear algebra and eigenvalues

  • S392. When: step 40 (6e), or sooner on demand: math::quaternion<T> a number type, both faces' matrices taking it. Not the earlier review's recommendation in full: neither face's matrix holds quaternions; the language's does. Recommended: the first alternative, with step 40 (6e) or sooner on demand, both faces' matrices at once; as taken until then.
  • S306. When: at the next step that touches the Jacobi functions; minor. Minor: no scaling before the Jacobi sweeps. Recommended: as taken, with the matrix scaled by a power of two first, as the driver is, at the next step that touches the Jacobi functions, the bits in range unchanged. Ruled 2026.11.04: as recommended, at the next step that touches the Jacobi functions.
  • S311. When: at the next step that touches the balancing; minor. Minor: the balancing exact only where no entry passes below the least normal. Recommended: the first alternative, the factors applied once at the end, at the next step that touches the balancing: exact wherever representable, the same bits wherever no entry underflows. Ruled 2026.11.04: the first alternative, the factors applied once at the end, as recommended.
  • S360. When: at the next step that touches quat.h's kernels; minor. Minor: the accurate rotation costs some 900 ns, near two hundred times the _fast form's. Recommended: as taken, and the rotation and its matrices in double's pairs for float and double, as the product's (S358), at the next step that touches quat.h's kernels.
  • S364. When: at the next step that touches quat.h's kernels; minor. Minor: the plain exp's and pow's error grows with |v| and p. Recommended: as taken for the _c forms, which the faces take; the plain forms carrying |v| as a pair, as the product's double pairs do, at the next step that touches quat.h's kernels.

1.5 Number types and the value's kinds

  • S130. When: step 45 (P3), big rationals, as planned. Significant drawback until P3. Recommended: as taken as an interim, big rationals in P3, as planned. Ruled 2026.11.04: the interim stands; big rationals at P3, step 45.

2. Every decision, by record

S1 to S3: slice 3, 2026.10.08

The record: _handoffs/math98_slice3_2026.10.08/README.md.

New with this slice; the earlier ones (M1 to M3, C1 to C4, N1 to N5) are in the consolidation record's section 5 and stand.

  • S1. The constants' spelling below C++14. Taken: constants::of<Type>::name(), numeric_limits' form, constexpr from C++11. The alternatives: gate the named constants at C++14, as re_std's numbers.hpp does ("a struct-of-static-members would be a different API"), leaving C++98 users without them; or one struct per constant, pi_c<Type>::value. The name of is the session's.

Review. Taken: for, one spelling from C++98, constexpr from C++11, in the numeric_limits idiom C++ readers know; against, a call where C++20's std::numbers::pi_v<T> is a variable, so it is not the standard's spelling. The alternatives: gating at C++14 leaves C++98 and C++11 without constants; one struct per constant cannot hold a floating-point value in class before C++11. Recommended: as taken, with std::numbers-named aliases of the same values from C++14, so that code moving to <numbers> finds them. Taken.

  • S2. A non-static member counts as absent in is_rational, has_number_base and is_using_base, at every level, as in interval's traits; a radix that is not static cannot serve as a base. The alternative, counting it, as decltype did from C++14, would need a probe that takes a pointer to member as present at C++98, where Clang does not find the name.

Review. Taken: for, one answer at every level, which a C++98 probe can give; against, a type whose radix is a non-static member is read as having none. The alternative counts it only where decltype can, so the same type answers two ways by level. Recommended: as taken; one answer across levels is the framework's principle (4). Taken.

  • S3. rational stays at C++14, where expression.hpp is: N1 decides where both go.
  • N3, sharpened. exp_c and log_c are correctly rounded for float and double (but 3.9 double exponentials in a million) and glibc's exp and log are not (one in 1,600 and one in 14,000 differ by an ulp). A face that takes the library at run time and the _c form in constant evaluation, as linalg_elementary does for acos, sin and cos, gives two answers at those points; one that takes the _c form everywhere gives one answer and pays about 30 times the library's cost.

Review. Taken: a holding decision until N1, which the owner ruled; 1a (chunk 16) then wrote the exact rationals in C. Superseded; nothing to recommend now.

S4 to S7: slice 4, 2026.10.09

The record: _handoffs/math98_slice4_2026.10.09/README.md.

New with this slice; the earlier ones (M0 to M3, C1 to C4, N1 to N5 in the consolidation record's section 5; S1 to S3 in slice 3's) stand.

  • S4. The sine past 2^20: exact everywhere. Taken: Payne and Hanek's reduction in pure arithmetic, so sin_c, cos_c and tan_c are right and constant expressions at every finite argument, as the library's functions are. The alternative was a stated bound - NaN, or a documented loss of digits, past 2^20 quarter turns - which would leave _c and the library disagreeing there and the C++ faces unable to promise one answer (N3). The cost is 2,112 bytes of literal and about a microsecond an argument past the bound.

Review. Taken: for, every finite argument right, a constant expression, and the same bits as a correct library; against, 2 KB of digits and about a microsecond past 2^20. The alternative's bound would give wrong or NaN results there and break one answer (N3). Recommended: as taken. Taken.

  • S5. sin_c and cos_c rounded once. Taken, so that all four of this slice's functions, like exp_c and log_c, are correctly rounded for float and double but at a few arguments in a million. It changes, below 2^20, 234 double sines and 261 cosines in a million (231 and 257 to the correctly rounded value, 3 and 4 from it) and some 7.5% of long double ones (section 2.4). The alternative keeps every result below 2^20 as it was and only adds the far reduction.

Review. Taken: for, correctly rounded but at a few arguments in a million; against, not proven, so "nearly" remains. The alternative keeps known double roundings. Recommended: as taken, then proven correct rounding (step 33, 6a, CORE-MATH's method). Taken.

  • S6. 2/pi as a string literal. A static const long array at file scope is what fdlibm has, but GCC emits it, 5.6 KB, into every translation unit that includes the header at -O0 (C and C++); from C++11 a constant expression can read it only if it is constexpr, so it needs a spelling per level; and below C++17 an internal-linkage table read by an external-linkage inline function is a technical ODR question. An array inside the function avoids those, but is 5.6 KB of stack that GCC copies on every call. The literal has none of these costs. Its alternative is the array, with a math_common.h qualifier for its spelling.

Review. Taken: for, no per-unit copy, no stack copy, one text for C99 to C++23; against, digits read from a string are slower to reach than an array's and an unusual idiom. The alternative's array costs 5.6 KB a unit at -O0 and a spelling per level. Recommended: as taken while one text serves every level; an inline constexpr array from C++17 would be faster at run time without changing a bit. Taken.

  • S7. tangent.h, and the shared kernels in elementary.h. tan and atan have a header of their own, as exp and log do; the exact arithmetic and round_pair moved down into elementary.h, which the other two include, rather than into a new header beneath all three.
  • N3, sharpened again. sin_c, cos_c, tan_c and atan_c are now correctly rounded for float and nearly so for double; glibc's double sin, cos and tan differ from them by an ulp at about 1 argument in 650, 780 and 400, atan at 1 in 4,300 - and where they differ the _c result is the correctly rounded one, but at the few counted above. linalg_elementary's rule - the library at run time, _c in constant evaluation - gives two answers at those points.

Review. Taken: for, no new header; against, elementary.h grows into the kernels' catch-all. The alternative's header beneath all three is the cleaner layering in the long run. Recommended: either; a shared kernel header is slightly better once more families need the exact arithmetic. Taken, a minor point.

S8 to S10: slice 5, 2026.10.09

The record: _handoffs/math98_slice5_2026.10.09/README.md.

New with this slice; the earlier ones (M0 to M3, C1 to C4, N1 to N5 in the consolidation record's section 5; S1 to S3 in slice 3's; S4 to S7 in slice 4's) stand.

  • S8. acos_c onto the arc-tangent kernel. Taken, so that asin_c and acos_c are one computation, each rounded once, and acos_c is correctly rounded for float and double like the slice's other functions. It changes 267 double results in a million on the x87 - every one to the correctly rounded value - and 23.8% of long double ones (237,788 to it, 88 from it); where long double is double, a quarter of double ones (section 4). The alternative keeps the old acos_c, within 2 ulps, beside an asin_c on the kernel, the two then computed two ways and rounded differently.

Review. Taken: for, asin and acos one computation, correctly rounded; against, three times the old cost (N6). The alternative keeps two methods rounding two ways. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

  • S9. A zero's sign by a compiler builtin. atan2 is the one function here whose result depends on the sign of a zero, which no comparison sees. Taken: __builtin_copysign (and f, l) on GCC and Clang - a constant expression in both, where __builtin_signbit is not in Clang 18 - and 1 / x elsewhere, which is right at run time but not a constant expression, so that atan2_c of a zero is not one there. The alternatives: bit_cast from C++20, which the C text cannot use; or treating -0 as +0 in constant evaluation, which would make the constant and the run time disagree.

Review. Taken: for, a constant expression on GCC and Clang; against, elsewhere (MSVC, which has never compiled the core) atan2_c of a zero is no constant expression. The alternatives: std::bit_cast from C++20; or -0 as +0, a second answer. Recommended: as taken, plus std::bit_cast from C++20 where the builtin is missing, so every C++20 compiler gets it. Taken in part. Ruled 2026.11.04: as recommended, at step 39 (6d). Flag: significant drawback on compilers without the builtin.

  • S10. Where the kernel lives. In elementary.h, beside the sine's, because asin and acos are there and tangent.h includes it; asin_c beside acos_c; atan2_c beside atan_c in tangent.h. The alternative, a header of the arc functions beneath both, would move acos out of elementary.h.
  • N3, sharpened again. atan2_c, asin_c and acos_c are correctly rounded for float and nearly so for double; glibc's atan2f differs from them at one float pair in six, asinf and acosf at about one float in 400 in [-1, 1], and the double atan2, asin and acos at 380, 740 and 380 arguments in a million - where the _c result is the correctly rounded one. linalg_elementary's rule gives two answers at those points.
  • N6. The arc-sine's cost. asin_c and acos_c take about 1.1 to 1.2 us, three times the old acos_c; a third of it is the long double square root, sqrt_c in pure arithmetic (370 ns). Where it is not constant-evaluated, a call could take the library's sqrtl instead - correctly rounded too, so with the same bits - through __builtin_is_constant_evaluated, which GCC and Clang (9 and up) offer in C++ at every level, or std::is_constant_evaluated from C++20; a C build, which evaluates nothing at compile time, would always call it. The same would serve every _c function that takes a root. Not done: it would be the first _c text that runs differently at run time and in constant evaluation, which is the owner's call.

Review. Taken: placement only; sound. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S11 and S12: slice 6, 2026.10.10

The record: _handoffs/math98_slice6_2026.10.10/README.md.

New with this slice; the earlier ones (M0 to M3, C1 to C4, N1 to N6, S1 to S10, in the consolidation record and slices 3 to 5) stand.

  • S11. A header of their own. hyperbolic.h, on exponential.h's kernel and elementary.h's exact arithmetic, as tangent.h stands on elementary.h's. The alternative was exponential.h's own sections, which would make every user of exp and log compile the hyperbolic functions too.

Review. Taken: for, users of exp and log do not compile the hyperbolic functions; against, one header more. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

  • S12. The series below 1/2, the exponential from it. At 1/2 the series' terms past the exact ones are under 2^-10 of the sum, and the difference E - 1/E loses under a bit; a lower crossing would make the difference lose more, a higher one the series' plain terms count for more. Below it, no double result was seen not correctly rounded; from it the error is the exponential's, as exp_c's is.
  • N3, again. sinh_c, cosh_c and tanh_c are correctly rounded for float and nearly so for double; glibc's differ from them at a quarter of the floats and doubles from 1/2 to 2, where the _c result is the correctly rounded one. linalg_elementary's rule gives two answers at those points.
  • N6, again. sinh_c and cosh_c cost about 500 ns, tanh_c up to a microsecond, where glibc's take 7 to 17 ns. The series divides at each nested term, which a table of reciprocals would avoid. Slice 5's way out
    • the library at run time, where a call is not constant-evaluated - is no way out here: it keeps the bits only where the library's are correctly rounded, as sqrtl is and glibc's hyperbolic functions are not.

Review. Taken: a measured crossing; for, no double result seen misrounded below it; against, measured, not proven. Recommended: as taken, with a worst-case search when 6a proves the family. Taken.

S13 to S16: slice 7, 2026.10.10

The record: _handoffs/math98_slice7_2026.10.10/README.md.

New with this slice; the earlier ones (M0 to M3, C1 to C4, N1 to N6, S1 to S12, in the consolidation record and slices 3 to 6) stand.

  • S13. exp2, log2 and log10 in exponential.h, on its kernels split so that five functions share them. They are the exponential and the logarithm in other bases, as C's <math.h> groups them, and add a few dozen lines a family. The alternative, a header of their own as hyperbolic.h is, would copy the series or reach into exponential.h's internals all the same.

Review. Taken: for, C's grouping and shared kernels; against, a larger header. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

  • S14. cbrt in a new power.h, where pow and hypot - which geometry and the calculus headers call - can go when they come. The alternative is elementary.h, beside sqrt_c, which every linear-algebra face includes.

Review. Taken: for, pow and hypot have a home off the linear algebra faces' include path; against, one header more. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

  • S15. Below C++11, the plain exp2, log2 and cbrt are the _c forms. C++98 has no std::exp2, std::log2 or std::cbrt, and ::exp2f and its kin are C99's, which a C++98 library need not declare. So the plain forms give the correctly rounded (or nearly) bits there, at the _c forms' cost.

Review. Taken: for, exp2, log2 and cbrt exist at C++98, correctly rounded; against, at C++98 the plain forms are the _c forms, 30 to 100 times slower, and give other bits than C++11's plain forms, the library's, where the library is not correctly rounded: two answers across levels, against principle 4. The alternative (calling C99's functions, which every real C++98 platform's <math.h> declares) keeps one answer per platform and the library's speed. Recommended: the library's functions wherever <math.h> declares them, the _c forms only where it does not. Ruled 2026.11.04 (the owner's, settled): as recommended, one answer per platform, at step 36 (6a). Flag: not the recommendation; two answers across levels.

  • S16. cbrt_c correctly rounded, by the exact settle, as sqrt_c is. The settle costs a few products where its approximation decides, and the exact sum is reached only for a root within 2^(4 - P) ulp of a midpoint, 2^-60 on an x87. The alternative, Newton's root rounded to the family, is faithful but not correctly rounded, and a constant would then differ from a correctly rounded library's at some arguments.
  • N3, again. glibc's cbrt differs from cbrt_c at about half of all doubles, log10f and log10 at a third of those from 1/2 to 2: linalg_elementary's rule gives two answers there. Its exp2 and log2 differ at fewer than one double in a thousand.
  • N6, again. exp2_c about 450 ns, log2_c and log10_c about 320, cbrt_c 620, where glibc's take 3 to 13 ns. The library at run time keeps the _c bits only where the library's are correctly rounded: glibc's exp2 and log2 are at all but about one double in a thousand, its log10 at seven in ten from 1/2 to 2, its cbrt at fewer than half of all doubles.

Review. Taken: for, correctly rounded by an exact settle, with no worst-case search; against, a few products more. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S17 to S20: slice 8, 2026.10.10

The record: _handoffs/math98_slice8_2026.10.10/README.md.

New with this slice; the earlier ones (M0 to M3, C1 to C4, N1 to N6, S1 to S16, in the consolidation record and slices 3 to 7) stand.

  • S17. pow_c as exp(y log x) carried past long double, the exact powers exactly. Correctly rounded wherever measured, not proven: a proof needs the worst cases of x^y, which for double are not known in full, and an exact settle like cbrt_c's has no cheap form for a transcendental power. The exact routes cover every result that can be exact or on a midpoint, so that no tie is left to the approximation. The alternative, exp_c(y log_c x) on the existing kernels, misrounds about 5 doubles in a million and 1.2% of x87 long doubles (measured on the prototype).

Review. Taken: for, correctly rounded wherever measured, exact cases exact; against, not proven, and 690 ns where glibc takes 15. The alternative misrounds 5 doubles in a million. Recommended: as taken, then a proof by CORE-MATH's method in 6a. Taken.

  • S18. The shared kernels made faster without a bit changed (section 2): an improvement every _c function shares. The alternative, a pow of its own Dekker product, would leave the rest 2 to 3 times slower than they need be.

Review. Taken: for, every _c function faster, no bit changed; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

  • S19. exp_scale breaks a tie in long double's subnormals by y_lo (section 3), which changes exp_c and exp2_c there: toward the correctly rounded value at 99.8% of the changes, away at 0.2%, where the fast kernel's error passes the true y_lo. The alternative, a scaling of pow's own, would leave exp_c's double rounding in place and add a second scaling to keep in step.

Review. Taken: for, 99.8% of the changes toward correct rounding; against, 0.2% away, in long double's subnormals. The alternative keeps a known double rounding. Recommended: as taken until 6a makes the family correctly rounded there. Taken.

  • S20. power.h includes exponential.h and tangent.h, the latter for d_internal_math_*_negative: pow(-0, -3) is -inf, and no comparison sees a zero's sign in a constant expression. The alternative is to move negative into float_order.h, beside the other order helpers, at the cost of touching a third header.
  • N3, again. glibc's powf, pow and powl differ from pow_c at 0.2%, 0.07% and 8% of the pairs measured; linalg_elementary's rule gives two answers there.
  • N6, again. pow_c about 690 ns a double, where glibc's pow takes 15: the _c forms are for constant evaluation and as a reference.

Review. Taken: for, no third header touched; against, power.h includes tangent.h for a sign helper that belongs with the order helpers. The alternative, the helper in float_order.h, is the better layering. Recommended: the alternative, at its small cost. Ruled 2026.11.04: the alternative, as recommended, at any step that touches power.h. Flag: not the recommendation (minor).

S21 to S24: slice 9, 2026.10.10

New with this slice; the earlier ones (M0 to M3, C1 to C4, N1 to N6, S1 to S20, in the consolidation record and slices 3 to 8) stand.

  • C5. The merge takes master's four later rulings (section 2.2). They are the owner's, made after the register lane's fifth batch, on entries that lane left open; the lane's own versions are what they were applied to. The alternative is the lane's versions, which would undo the owner.
  • S21. hypot_c correctly rounded by an exact settle, as sqrt_c and cbrt_c are: no worst-case search needed, the sign of an exact sum decides every bit. The alternative, a pair to some 2P bits rounded once, needs the root kept from a midpoint by more than the pair's error; for hypot that distance is bounded below only by some 2^-(3P) of a last place, past what a long double pair holds for double.

Review. Taken: for, correctly rounded with no worst-case search; against, cost. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

  • S22. A _c function returns its NaN argument, never x + y: Clang's constant evaluation takes no arithmetic that yields a NaN. atan2_c already did; pow_c now does, and hypot_c and fmod_c do.

Review. Taken: for, Clang's constant evaluation, and IEEE 754's advice to return an input NaN; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

  • S23. fmod in a header of its own, remainder.h, for C's remainder functions, rather than in power.h or float_order.h: neither is about remainders, and remainder and remquo can join it. It needs no long double kernel.

Review. Taken: placement after C's grouping; sound. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

  • S24. hypot_c's candidate by Newton's iteration, not sqrt_c: the settle makes the candidate's last bit irrelevant, and sqrt_c was half the cost.
  • N3, again. Through calculus.hpp and series.hpp the merge makes the C++20 run-time dispatch of expression.hpp's and calculus/elementary.hpp's kernels independent of include order (section 2.3). glibc's hypot differs from hypot_c at 0.28% of the pairs, fmod nowhere: under linalg_elementary's rule hypot gives two answers there, fmod one.

Review. Taken: for, half the cost, the settle making the candidate's last bit moot; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S25: chunk 2, 2026.10.11

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk2_2026.10.11/README.md.

New with this chunk; the earlier ones (M0 to M3, C1 to C5, N1 to N6, S1 to S24) stand.

  • S25. A NaN is in no order: advance keeps it, steps saturates. advance(NaN, n) is that NaN, as next_up(NaN) and C's nextafter are. steps with a NaN is D_MATH_UMAX_MAX, the count that says there is none to give: advance(a, steps(a, b)) == b still holds wherever steps does not saturate, and a test steps(a, b) <= k never takes a NaN for a neighbour. The alternative, 0, would make a NaN every value's neighbour.

Review. Taken: for, NaN in no order, as nextafter treats it, and a saturating count that no test mistakes for a neighbour; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S26 and S27: chunk 3, 2026.10.11

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk3_2026.10.11/README.md.

New with this chunk; the earlier ones (M0 to M3, C1 to C5, N1 to N6, S1 to S25) stand.

  • N5, ruled: the rewrite fixes the range. The owner, asked with the consolidation's recommendation named, said to continue; this chunk reads that as taking the recommendation. The C core's four are right everywhere; whether the C++ port keeps any wrap is now moot, since it will call these. Say so if the reading is wrong.
  • S26. floor, ceil, trunc and round in a header of their own, nearest_integer.h, after C's nearest integer functions (7.12.9), as remainder.h is after its remainder functions (S23). Not remainder.h, not float_order.h: neither is about integers. rint, nearbyint and lrint belong to the same group but read the rounding mode, which pure arithmetic cannot; they are left out.

Review. Taken: for, exact floor, ceil, trunc and round in pure arithmetic; against, the C core has no rint, nearbyint or lrint. Recommended: as taken, and those three from the library at run time, round-to-nearest-even in constant evaluation (which assumes the default mode), documented as such. Taken in part: the gap remains.

  • S27. The integer part by conversion to d_math_umax, a part at a time past 2^W. The alternatives: adding and subtracting 2^(P-1), which rounds to an integer only in the default rounding mode and, on the x87, only where excess precision does not keep the fraction; or building the integer a bit at a time, up to 112 steps for a quad. The conversion truncates exactly in any mode, costs a few ns, and is a constant expression.

Review. Taken: for, exact in every rounding mode, a constant expression, a few ns; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S28: chunk 4, 2026.10.11

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk4_2026.10.11/README.md.

New with this chunk; the earlier ones (M0 to M3, C1 to C5, N1 to N6, S1 to S27) stand.

  • S28. A zero remainder has x's sign, as IEEE 754 says, where glibc's double remainder sometimes gives the other. remainder_c follows the standard, and every other implementation measured here; the harness counts glibc's departures instead of failing them, and fails remainder_c if its zero has the wrong sign. The alternative, copying glibc, would make the C core's answer depend on the library it was measured against.

Review. Taken: for, IEEE 754's sign, independent of the library measured; against, differs from glibc's double remainder at some points. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S29 to S31: chunk 5, 2026.10.11

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk5_2026.10.11/README.md.

New with this chunk; the earlier ones (M0 to M3, C1 to C5, N1 to N6, S1 to S28) stand.

  • S29. A constant-expression kernel may answer twice, through a pointer. remquo_c is D_MATH_FN and keeps C's signature, (x, y, int*); math_common.h says so. A constant expression reads its quotient through an int of its own, as with C++23's std::remquo. The alternative was a struct returned by value, as C's div returns div_t: no rule to change, but a signature C has not, for remquo and later for frexp and modf.

Review. Taken: for, C's signature kept; against, constant evaluation needs a local int. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

  • S30. The quotient is |n| mod 8, 0 to 7, with x / y's sign; 0 where the result is NaN or y is infinite. C promises three bits and lets the library keep more. glibc on x86-64 gives 8 or -8 for some quotients whose last three bits are 0 - where its count carries to 8 - and on 32-bit x86 never, so only the last three bits agree anywhere, and those are what remquo_c gives. 0 to 7 indexes a table of eight.

Review. Taken: for, the three bits C promises, the same on every platform; against, fewer bits than some libraries give. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

  • S31. A null quo stores nothing, in both forms, the house's check of a parameter first. C's remquo has no such case; the library form hands it an int of its own.

Review. Taken: for, a null pointer is safe; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S32 and S33: chunk 6, 2026.10.11

New with this chunk; the earlier ones (M0 to M3, C1 to C5, N1 to N6, S1 to S31) stand.

  • S32. The harnesses' random numbers come from one generator in 32-bit halves, and are the numbers they were. The alternative, a 32-bit generator as t_nearest_integer.c has, would have changed every argument the six harnesses check, and every count their records give.

Review. Taken: for, every earlier count stays comparable; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S34 and S35: chunk 7, 2026.10.11

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk7_2026.10.11/README.md.

New with this chunk; the earlier ones (M0 to M3, C1 to C5, N1 to N6, S1 to S33) stand.

  • S34. Where two d_math_umax limbs cannot hold (2^(P+1))^2 and P is below 64 - a double where d_math_umax has 32 bits - sqrt_c settles its last bit in integers too, in digits of 16 bits; Dekker's product is left to long doubles of 64 bits or more. Integers give the same bits under every evaluation method and in constant evaluation. The other ways: Dekker's product with each step stored through a volatile (no constant expression, and a step a compiler keeps in a register is still wide); setting the x87's precision (no portable way in C99 or C++98, none in constant evaluation); limbs of half d_math_umax at every width (the digits serve only where it has 32 bits, whose half is 16).

Review. Taken: for, the same bits under every evaluation method and in constant evaluation; against, a slower path on 32-bit-word targets. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

  • S35. g++'s ISO-strict C++98 -m32 build on the x87 joins the matrix, for every harness: the configuration sqrt_c failed in. clang++'s stays out, as section 4 says.

Review. Taken: for, the configuration that failed is tested; against, clang's x87 build stays untested. Recommended: as taken, with clang's x87 in 6d. Taken.

S36 and S37: chunk 8, 2026.10.12

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk8_2026.10.12/README.md.

New with this chunk; the earlier ones (M0 to M3, C1 to C5, N1 to N6, S1 to S35) stand.

  • S36. The harnesses compare values as stored. A value a harness computes in float or double arithmetic and then compares, or hands to a kernel and to the library both, is stored through a volatile first, so that every build compares values of their type. GCC rounds at the assignment, as C says, and the store changes nothing there, nor on any build that evaluates in the type (the fingerprints); it is what makes an x87 build's verdict the library's and not the register allocator's.

Review. Taken: for, verdicts that are the library's, not the register allocator's; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

  • S37. clang on the x87 is left as it is. It keeps the x87's 64 bits through assignments, casts and arguments, which C says remove them, and the library's float and double kernels then give values that are not float or double arithmetic's: t_vec, t_quat and t_interval_float record them. The library cannot round a value to its type inside a constant expression - a volatile read is not one - and rounding every step of every kernel through memory would cost every target. clang's x87 builds stay out of the matrix (S35); with clang, a 32-bit x86 build should use SSE (-msse2 -mfpmath=sse), as the matrix's do.

Review. Taken: for, no cost on any other target; against, on clang's x87 the float and double kernels give values of no float or double arithmetic, so results differ by compiler there. Recommended: as taken, given that C allows no fix inside a constant expression; keep the SSE advice prominent. Taken. Ruled 2026.11.04: as taken, the SSE advice kept prominent. Flag: significant drawback on clang's x87, documented.

S38 and S39: chunk 9, 2026.10.12

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk9_2026.10.12/README.md.

New with this chunk; the earlier ones (M0 to M3, C1 to C5, N1 to N6, S1 to S37) stand.

  • S38. The plain roots store the C library's root only where their family is evaluated wider than its type (FLT_EVAL_METHOD, through math_common.h's D_MATH_*_EVAL_WIDE). Everywhere else the root is returned as it comes, and costs what it did. Storing always would cost a store and a load on every target, for builds - every one on SSE or x86-64 - whose roots were right: GCC and clang compute them there with sqrtss and sqrtsd.

Review. Taken: for, the store only where wide evaluation makes it needed; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

  • S39. Only the roots. The other plain functions return the C library's result as it comes, glibc's i386 asin and its 64 bits included: their word is "the C library's", not "_c's, bit for bit", and no kernel computes further with them.

Review. Taken: for, no cost; against, on x87 targets the plain functions can return values wider than their type, which then vary with register allocation. The alternative, storing every plain result where evaluation is wide, costs a store and a load on the x87 alone and makes every plain result a value of its type. Chunk 37's plain complex functions show it on the x87, within their bounds (S288). Recommended: the alternative, as S38 does for the roots. Ruled 2026.11.04: the alternative, as recommended, at step 39 (6d). Flag: not the recommendation.

S40: chunk 10, 2026.10.12

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk10_2026.10.12/README.md.

New with this chunk; the earlier ones (M0 to M3, C1 to C5, N1 to N6, S1 to S39) stand.

  • S40. sqrt_settle_digits decides the last bit by the residual G = S M - R^2 + R - 1, taken in the low digits - P + 9 bits or more -- modulo a power of 2. Its sign survives there because the loops keep R within four steps of the root, and t_elementary checks the settle over all of that reach. The other way, the two midpoints' squares at full width and moved by 8R a step, saved a fifth to two fifths as much.

Review. Taken: for, a cheaper settle, checked over its whole reach; against, a subtler proof. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S41 and S42: chunk 11, 2026.10.13

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk11_2026.10.13/README.md.

S41. The arc-tangent and the remainder are this side's - tangent.h's atan2 and atan2_c, remainder.h's fmod and fmod_c - not the package's section of elementary.h. They are the later and the more measured (slices 5 and 9; 51,859,520 and 15,300,041 checks a build, the 184-build matrix). The package's harness passes on them in all 12 builds and its oracles print the same, byte for byte, at three levels: the faces take trigonometry from the C library at run time at every level, and a remainder's _c and plain forms agree. Alternative: the package's, which would move every caller slices 5 and 9 made, and their checks, onto functions measured less.

Review. Taken: for, the more measured functions kept; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S42. infinity stays in exponential.h, where this side defines it, and isect.h includes exponential.h for it; the package's abs helper goes into elementary.h's internal helpers, where the package had it. Alternative: infinity moved into elementary.h beside abs - the base header, a tidier home - at the cost of changing a second measured header and the include comments of hyperbolic.h and power.h.

Review. Taken: for, no measured header touched; against, infinity lives in an unexpected header. Recommended: either; the alternative is tidier. Taken, a minor point.

S43 and S44: chunk 12, 2026.10.13

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk12_2026.10.13/README.md.

S43. The module's README and roadmap live in inc/djinterp/math/, as README.html and roadmap.html, the way inc/djinterp/ui/ keeps its own. Alternative: keep them in the lane's records and the project only, where a reader of the tree would not find them.

Review. Taken: for, documents beside the code they describe; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S44. master fast-forwards to this chunk's tip, and the lane keeps committing on consolidated. This is the owner's C1. master is where every lane, this one included, merges back. Keeping the lane on its own branch keeps each bundle's base known while other lanes merge into master. Alternatives:

  • commit on master directly from now on. That is simpler while this lane is the only one landing, but a bundle could then meet another lane's merge;
  • leave master where it is until the lanes reunite.

The roadmap's gates G1 to G9 are proposals, not decisions taken. Each states its recommendation and alternatives in roadmap.html. The lane takes each recommendation when its phase begins, unless the owner says otherwise.

S45 to S49: chunk 13, 2026.10.13

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk13_2026.10.13/README.md.

S45. Single-shift QR on the Hessenberg form, Wilkinson's shift. Real shifts only, one rotation per bulge step. The result type holds real eigenvalues, and a complex pair is flagged rather than found.

  • Alternative: Francis's double-shift QR (LAPACK's dlahqr). It converges on complex pairs as fast as on real ones, and could give them once complex numbers are a value kind (the roadmap's Later). Switching then changes only the kernel.

Review. Taken: for, one simple kernel, every real eigenvalue found; against, a complex pair was flagged, not found, so a real matrix with complex eigenvalues had no full answer, and the language's eig (chunk 36, S258) gives it as a domain error. The alternative, Francis's double-shift QR (LAPACK's), finds complex pairs as fast as real ones and is the standard method. Recommended: the alternative, each pair given as a complex number of the C core's (S267), as the owner ruled on 2026.10.27. Taken in chunk 38: Francis's double-shift QR after balancing finds every pair, each a complex number of the C core's (S292), the old function on its path (S296).

S46. _tol defaults to 0, meaning the type's epsilon, and the epsilon is also its floor. A caller can loosen the deflation test, not tighten it past what the type resolves. The old default, 1e-12, was below a float's resolution and about 4,500 epsilons for a double.

  • Alternative: keep 1e-12 as the default.

Review. Taken: for, a deflation test the type can resolve, epsilon as the floor, as LAPACK's is; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S47. converged() is false in three cases, and each still gives an answer for every entry:

  • a complex pair gives its real part twice, and the rest of the spectrum is still found;
  • a spent budget gives the diagonal of what is left unsolved;
  • a NaN or an infinity anywhere gives NaN for every eigenvalue. This holds even where the eigenvalues are finite, as for an infinity above the diagonal of a triangular matrix, which the old code solved. numpy's eigvals raises an error on such input.

Alternatives: stop at the first complex pair; give NaN for a complex pair's two entries.

Review. Taken: for, every entry answered, NaN where any input is not finite; against, a complex pair's real part given twice is a wrong value, flagged only by converged(). Recommended: S45's alternative, which removes the first case. Taken in chunk 38: the driver gives each pair (S292); the old function still gives a pair's real part twice, flagged, for its callers (S296).

S48. Both kernels scale the matrix by a power of two, exactly. 2^k A has 2^k times A's eigenvalues bit for bit (barring overflow and underflow), and the iteration works the same at the range's ends: section D converges from 1e-300 to 1e300.

  • Alternative: divide by the largest magnitude. That is inexact, so the 2^k property is lost.
  • Alternative: no scaling. That fails at the range's ends, as the old code did at 1e300.

Review. Taken: for, exact scaling, the 2^k property kept, the range's ends handled; against, scaling as a whole, not balancing. Recommended: as taken, plus LAPACK's balancing (dgebal: a diagonal similarity by powers of two), which also keeps bits exact and improves accuracy on badly scaled matrices. Taken in part.

S49. Eigenvectors by inverse iteration, from the eigenvalues. The shift is 4 epsilon of the eigenvalue's magnitude, or of the matrix's scale if larger, moved 16 times further while the shifted matrix is exactly singular. U x = e is solved first, then two full solves. The vector has unit length with its largest entry positive, and the column stays the identity's where there is nothing to find. Costs O(n^3) per vector.

  • Alternative: accumulate the QR steps' rotations and back-substitute in the Schur form (LAPACK's dtrevc). That gives independent vectors for a repeated eigenvalue (section 6) and could handle complex pairs, at the cost of a second kernel.

Review. Taken: for, one kernel, simple; against, dependent vectors for a repeated eigenvalue and no complex vectors. The alternative, Schur vectors and back-substitution (dtrevc), gives independent vectors and handles complex pairs. Recommended: the alternative, with S45's. Taken in chunk 38: the Schur vectors by back-substitution, complex for a pair (S292), the C++ face's general eigen among their callers (S296).

S50 to S52: chunk 14, 2026.10.14

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk14_2026.10.14/README.md.

S50. The runner lives in ci/, in the ladders' style: the same banner, JOBS, --save, and exit statuses 0, 1 and 2. It is the third of the tree's checks, next to the two ladders.

  • Alternative: tools/, beside the generator.
  • Alternative: a Makefile or CMake target. That is C4c's job, with MSVC.

Review. Taken: for, the tree's checks side by side, one style; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S51. The tests are found by their names on each run, not listed. A harness's C++14 and C++20 builds follow from its C++14 section. A new test therefore joins both tiers with no edit to the runner; section 4's four new failing tests were found that way.

  • Alternative: a list in the runner, as chunk 7's had. It went stale (13 of 17 harnesses, 7 of 26 face tests).

Review. Taken: for, no list to go stale; against, a test named against the pattern is missed silently. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S52. The full tier stops before it starts when a compiler or the 32-bit libraries are missing, and says what to install. It never skips a build, as the C++ ladder never skips a configuration, so a full run that passes always means all 1,896 builds passed.

  • Alternative: skip the builds that cannot run and report them. A pass would then depend on the machine.

Review. Taken: for, a pass means every build passed; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S53 and S54: chunk 15, 2026.10.14

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk15_2026.10.14/README.md.

S53. D_MAYBE_UNUSED leads the typedef. All three placements work with GCC and Clang. The leading one is also where a standard [[maybe_unused]] goes on a typedef, so the macro stays right if D_MAYBE_UNUSED is ever defined that way at these levels.

  • Alternative: after the declarator, the usual place for a GNU attribute on a typedef. That place would be a syntax error for [[maybe_unused]].

Review. Taken: for, right for a standard attribute too; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S54. The class-scope checks stay where they are. solve_requires_float, decomp_requires_float, the measure_*_check structs and stellate_check assert at class scope, for the functions that name them. They could now be D_STATIC_ASSERTs inside each function, but that is a change in five more headers for no change in behaviour.

  • Alternative: move them into the functions now, to finish the job in one chunk.

Review. Taken: for, no change in behaviour, five headers untouched; against, two styles of check remain. Recommended: as taken, and moved when those headers are next changed. Taken.

S55 to S59: chunk 16, 2026.10.14

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk16_2026.10.14/README.md.

S55. A kernel that can fail returns a status and writes through _out. The caller cannot use a result without seeing whether there is one. The status tells overflow, which 1b's value promotes, from a pole or 0/0, which are error values. Only D_RATIONAL_OK writes.

  • Alternative: return the rational, with failure marked in it, such as a zero denominator. One argument fewer, but every caller must test the result, and each kind of failure needs its own in-band mark.
  • Alternative: return a struct of the result and the status. The same information, at more length for C callers.

Review. Taken: for, a result cannot be used unchecked, and each failure is told apart; against, an out parameter. Recommended: as taken; it is C's idiom. Taken.

S56. Overflow is exact, by double width in d_math_umax. A kernel reports overflow only when the reduced result does not fit (section 2.3).

  • Alternative: report overflow as soon as an intermediate does not fit, by a checked multiply. Simpler, but then MAX/3 + MAX/6, and every sum whose cross term passes a word, would overflow though the result fits, and 1b would turn exact values into inexact ones for nothing. F11 and F14 are this alternative.
  • Alternative: __int128, where the compiler has one, for the double width. It is not ISO C99 or C++98: -pedantic-errors rejects it unless it is marked __extension__, 32-bit targets lack it, and MSVC has none. The two-word code would be needed anyway.

Review. Taken: for, overflow only where the result does not fit, so exact values stay exact; against, more code than a checked multiply. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S57. The harness's reference is 256 bits, in 16-bit limbs. The roadmap said 128 bits. But a check multiplies an exact result, below 2^127, by a term, below 2^64, so it needs 190 bits. And with 16-bit limbs the product of two limbs fits 32 bits, so the reference needs no 64-bit type and runs the same in ISO strict C++98 -m32.

  • Alternative: __int128, where the compiler has one, as t_interval.c uses it (marked __extension__). 32-bit targets lack it, so 6 of the 16 builds would have no reference, and it is too narrow for the checks anyway.

Review. Taken: for, a reference wide enough, in every build; against, slower than __int128. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S58. The harness checks the double-width helpers on their own. The kernels divide only by a gcd of two denominators, which is below 2^(w-1), so they never reach divmod_wide's carried bit (section 6). The helper stays general, for any divisor, and the harness checks it directly, as t_interval.c checks its lattice helpers.

  • Alternative: narrow divmod_wide to divisors below 2^(w-1) and drop the carry. Less code today, but the helper would be wrong for larger divisors with only a comment to say so, and rounding a rational to a float (1c) may want them.

Review. Taken: for, a helper right for every divisor, and checked; against, code no caller needs yet. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S59. The steps are numbered in the planned order, not the list's. P0 is listed in Phase 0, but it is step 12, because G7 opens Phase 2 with it. C6 and C4b wait on things outside the lane, so they are counted last, as steps 47 and 48, though either may come sooner. If one does, the steps are renumbered and the note says so.

  • Alternative: number the items as listed. Simpler, but the count would jump whenever P0, C6 or C4b were done out of turn.

Review. Taken: for, a count that does not jump; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S60 to S64: chunk 17, 2026.10.14

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk17_2026.10.14/README.md.

S60. A conversion rounds once, in integers, and then builds its result exactly. That is the only way to give the nearest value for every ratio, in every build, and in constant evaluation.

  • Alternative: floating-point division, (double)n / (double)d. It rounds up to three times: n and d each past 2^53, then the quotient. And on the x87, a quotient computed in extended precision is rounded again when it is stored.
  • Alternative: the C library's strtod and its kin, which round correctly. They need the decimal text, and they are not constant expressions.

Review. Taken: for, the nearest value for every ratio, in every build and in constant evaluation; against, slower than a division. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S61. Nearest, ties to even, whatever the environment's rounding mode. Under the roadmap's design, the value's context will carry a rounding mode. Until then, the conversions do not read the floating-point environment, and constant evaluation could not read it anyway.

  • Alternative: follow fegetround() at run time. Run time and constant evaluation could then give different bits for one call, which breaks the roadmap's principle 4, "one answer".

Review. Taken: for, one answer at run time and in constant evaluation; against, a caller's fesetround is ignored. Recommended: as taken, with the rounding mode carried in the context when a kind reads one, as planned. Taken.

S62. 1b takes two chunks. The conversions moved from 1c into 1b, because the value's promotion needs them. 1c keeps text to value and numeric_compare, and the count stays 48.

  • Alternative: make the conversions a step of their own, and renumber to 49.
  • Alternative: one large chunk for all of 1b. The owner asked for small chunks.

Review. Taken: process; sound. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S63. Two-word ratios convert to long double only. That is all the value needs: G2 sends an overflowing exact result to long double. To float, a two-word ratio could pass the type's range, 2^±128, and then needs subnormals and overflow handled.

  • Alternative: all three types, with that range handling. More code, with no caller.

Review. Taken: for, only what the value needs; against, a two-word ratio to float or double is missing. Recommended: as taken until a caller needs it. Taken.

S64. The harness's oracle uses the C library's nextafter and frexp. They are independent of the kernels, and of float_order.h, whose powers of two the kernels use to build their results.

  • Alternative: float_order.h's own next_up and next_down. Those are checked by t_float_order.c, but the oracle would then share a header with what it checks.

Review. Taken: for, an oracle independent of what it checks; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S65 to S72: chunk 18, 2026.10.14

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk18_2026.10.14/README.md.

S65. No union. Each kind's value has its own field, and the struct is 48 bytes on a 64-bit target (raw/x87_probe.txt) instead of about 32. In return every operation is a constant expression from C++14, as in the rest of the C core.

  • Alternative: a union, constant-evaluable only from C++20, where changing the active member became allowed.

Review. Taken: for, every operation a constant expression from C++14; against, 48 bytes a value instead of about 32 (64 on x86-64 since S112), which an exact matrix of values pays per element. Recommended: as taken; dense matrices (S239) carry the bulk data. Taken.

S66. One representation. A rational whose reduced denominator is 1 is an integer, and every field a kind does not use holds 0 (exact 0/1). Equality of kinds then means something, and 3/1 and 3 are never two different values.

  • Alternative: keep integer and rational apart by how a value was made. Then 1/3 + 2/3 would be the rational 1/1, and every reader would have to check for it.

Review. Taken: for, equality of kinds means equality of representation; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S67. A boolean or none in arithmetic is a domain error. The math language keeps truth values and numbers apart, as its relations and logic (2d) will.

  • Alternative: booleans as 0 and 1, as in C and Python. This makes true + 1 legal and x < y < z subtly wrong.

Review. Taken: for, numbers and truth kept apart, as the language's logic needs; against, unlike C and Python. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S68. The inexact flag marks every overflow into long double, even where the long double is exact (2^(w-1)). The flag says that the value has left the exact kinds, which is what G2 asks for. It is kept by every result computed from the value.

  • Alternative: set it only where the rounding lost something, as IEEE's inexact flag does. A value would then turn floating-point without notice, wherever the overflow happened to land on a representable number.

Review. Taken: for, the flag says the value left the exact kinds, always; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S69. Errors pass through, the first of two, with every flag. This is the same rule as for a NaN.

  • Alternative: an order among the errors, such as invalid above pole above domain. That needs a rule nobody has asked for, and makes a + b and b + a differ less obviously.

Review. Taken: for, the NaN rule, simple and predictable; against, which error comes first depends on operand order. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S70. No context parameter yet. The roadmap's design passes a context (precision, rounding mode, error policy). Nothing in this chunk would read one: rounding is to nearest (S61), errors are values, and there are no big floats. Its first reader adds it, while the only callers are the harness's.

  • Alternative: take a context now and ignore it, so that no signature changes later. Every call would pass a value that does nothing.

Review. Taken: for, no parameter that does nothing; against, signatures change when the first reader comes. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S71. rational.h's sums and products return their whole result, and its rounding reports its direction. These are internal, for the value layer, and rational.h's own kernels are unchanged (section 2.5).

  • Alternative: compute the exact result again in value.h. That would mean two copies of Knuth's forms.

Review. Taken: for, one copy of the exact forms; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S72. The exact reference is shared. math_tests_exact.h holds the 256-bit integers, the exact rationals and the nearest-value oracle for both t_rational.c and t_value.c. Its functions are static inline, as in math_tests_random.h, so a harness that does not use one is not warned about it.

  • Alternative: a second copy in t_value.c, about 800 lines.

Review. Taken: for, one reference, checked by two harnesses; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S73 to S81: chunk 19, 2026.10.14

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk19_2026.10.14/README.md.

S73. Simple Decimal Conversion. One algorithm, exact for every input, in integer arithmetic on decimal digits: no table of powers, nothing wider than a word, and constant-evaluable from C++14. It is slow at the far ends of a long double's range (section 2.3), which a literal reader can afford.

  • Alternative: Eisel and Lemire's algorithm, as in Go's and Rust's standard libraries and in fast_float, which GCC's std::from_chars uses. Its usual case is one 128-bit product, where this one works over every digit for every 60 bits of exponent, so it is far faster. But it needs a table of 128-bit powers of five, a 64 by 64 bit product into 128 bits, which the 32-bit ISO strict C++98 build, with no 64-bit integer at all, would have to assemble from four words, and a slow path for its hard cases anyway. It can come later as a fast path in front of this one, where measured demand exists.

Review. Taken: for, exact for every input, in every build, constant-evaluable; against, far slower than Eisel and Lemire's algorithm, the fastest correct method. Recommended: as taken, with Eisel-Lemire as a fast path where a 64-bit product exists, both correct, so no bit changes. Taken; the fast path is still to come.

S74. One buffer, for the widest type. Its capacity is the bound for a long double (11,516 digits on the x87), whatever the type converted to.

  • Alternative: a capacity per type (769 digits for a double), which saves stack for float and double, at the cost of three buffers or a size passed around; C++98 has no arrays of variable length.

Review. Taken: for, one buffer, simple; against, about 11.5 KB of stack a call for every type, which small stacks feel. Recommended: as taken on hosted targets; a per-type capacity where stack is scarce. Taken.

S75. The number and nothing else. The syntax is the decimal part of a C floating literal. No locale, no leading space, no hexadecimal, infinity or NaN. Its callers tokenize first, and a decimal number token is exactly this.

  • Alternative: strtod's syntax, which would make the result depend on the locale's decimal point and accept text that other tokens own.

Review. Taken: for, no locale, nothing other tokens own; against, no hexadecimal, infinity or NaN text. Recommended: as taken; the expression language names inf and nan as words. Taken.

S76. Always to nearest, ties to even, whatever the environment's rounding mode, as chunk 17's conversions do (S61). The result is then the same on every build and in constant evaluation.

  • Alternative: honour the rounding mode, as strtod does. The result would then depend on run-time state that constant evaluation cannot see.

Review. Taken: as S61. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S77. Underflow is a subnormal or zero result that is not exact, judged after rounding: a value that rounds up to the least normal value is not an underflow, and an exact subnormal is not one either.

  • Alternative: IEEE's tininess before rounding, under which a value just below the least normal one underflows even where it rounds to it. C leaves strtod's ERANGE here to the implementation.

Review. Taken: for, tininess after rounding, as x86 judges it; against, other platforms judge before. Recommended: as taken, documented. Taken.

S78. The point's place is counted exactly, then held. The digits before the point, or the zeros after it, are counted in size_t, which cannot overflow for a text in memory; the exponent's size is held at SIZE_MAX. The two are combined with their signs, and only the sum is held within 10^6 of 0.

  • Alternative: hold each within 10^6 on its own. A million zeros after the point with an exponent of 1,000,006, which is 1, would then read as 10^-1.

Review. Taken: for, the exponent's sum held, not its parts, so every text reads right; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S79. The harness's own reference. Integers of up to 54,400 bits, independent of decimal.h's algorithm and of the C library. A midpoint in a long double's lowest binade needs 38,000 bits; math_tests_exact.h has 256.

  • Alternative: glibc's strtod as the reference. It is correctly rounded, and the record's tools check decimal.h against it, but a C library elsewhere need not be, and the harness should mean the same on any.

Review. Taken: for, a reference that means the same on any platform; against, a large harness. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S80. A nudge past the buffer, or at its last digit, is checked against the same nudge within it, which the reference has checked. Both must round the same way, since both lie strictly between the same two midpoints, or on the same side of one value. The run takes 10.6 seconds.

  • Alternative: the reference for every text. It agrees on all 17,858 of them, but its numbers there reach 38,000 bits, and the run takes 67 seconds (raw/allref.txt).

Review. Taken: for, the same verdict in a sixth of the time, each nudge's side proved; against, an argument in place of a direct check. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S81. The smoke tier, not the full matrix. Nothing outside t_decimal.c includes decimal.h (raw/includers.txt), so no other test's build can change. t_decimal's 16 builds ran in full, and the smoke tier shows every other test's result unchanged, byte for byte, from chunk 18's full run.

  • Alternative: the full matrix, about an hour on two cores, which would show the same.

Review. Taken: for, the include closure shows no other build can change; against, the full matrix is skipped. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S82 to S89: chunk 20, 2026.10.15

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk20_2026.10.15/README.md.

S82. A header of its own. literal.h reads text into a value; value.h stays arithmetic on values, and decimal.h stays floating point. A file that includes value.h does not compile decimal.h too.

  • Alternative: d_math_value_from_text in value.h, one header fewer, which every user of the value would compile.

Review. Taken: for, users of the value do not compile the decimal reader; against, one header more. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S83. Three forms, and nothing between them. A fraction's terms are digits only, with no point, exponent or sign; a mixed number is separated by spaces, and its sign is the whole number's, in front. So 1.5/2 and 1/-2 are not literals: the literals at their start are 1.5 and 1, and a parser sees the division. Spaces only, not tabs or line breaks, and none around the literal: a tokenizer decides what surrounds a token.

  • Alternative: decimal terms, or signs on terms, which make a fraction a division of other literals; or 1+1/2 for a mixed number, which an expression reads as an addition.

Review. Taken: for, a literal that never swallows what a parser should see; against, 1.5/2 is a division, not a literal. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S84. Exact at any length. A decimal is judged by its value, not by how it is written: the 2s and 5s it shares with its power of ten are taken out of its digits, and zeros at either end do not count. So 0.1 followed by a thousand zeros is 1/10, and 1 followed by a thousand zeros and e-1000 is the integer 1. Only a decimal with more significant digits than any exact one has (63 or 31) is not worked on.

  • Alternative: read the digits into two words and give up past them, so that 0.10 written with forty zeros would be inexact and 0.1 exact.

Review. Taken: for, a decimal exact by its value, at any length; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S85. Not exact: the nearest long double, from the text, or a range error. A decimal that is not exact is read by decimal.h straight into a long double, rounded once (G3), and flagged inexact (G2). Past the largest long double it is a range error, not an infinity: the literal is an exact number the value cannot hold yet, not an IEEE result. Below the least subnormal it is 0 or the nearest subnormal, flagged, with its sign: that is the nearest long double, and no error.

  • Alternative: an infinity past the largest, as strtold gives; the exact tower would then hold an infinity that no exact operation made, and 1e5000 - 1e5000 would be a NaN, not an error.
  • Alternative: a range error below the least subnormal too, as strtold reports ERANGE. The flag already says the value is not exact.

Review. Taken: for, no infinity in the exact tower, and the nearest long double below the subnormals; against, a range error where strtold gives an infinity. Recommended: as taken until bignum holds the value exactly. Taken.

S86. Fractions in two words. A fraction's terms are read into two words, the width that rational.h reduces and rounds in; past that, a range error until bignum (P1). The zeros both terms end with, a common factor of a power of ten, are dropped first, so 10/20 with a thousand zeros on each term is 1/2. A mixed number w n/d is (w d + n)/d, whose numerator must fit too.

  • Alternative: a gcd on the decimal digits first, so that any fraction whose reduced terms fit is read. That is bignum's work.

Review. Taken: for, the terms rational.h works in; against, a fraction whose reduced terms fit but whose written terms do not is a range error. Recommended: as taken until bignum (P2, P3). Taken.

S87. Over 0 first. A fraction over 0 is a pole, or invalid for 0/0, whatever the size of the other terms: a numerator of a hundred digits over 0 is a pole, and 1 0/0 is invalid, as an exact division by 0 is in value.h.

  • Alternative: the range first, so that a long numerator over 0 is a range error. The literal has no value either way; the pole says why.

Review. Taken: for, the error that says why; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S88. The harness reads each literal itself, on the shared reference. t_literal.c has its own scan and reads the terms as math_tests_exact.h's 256-bit integers, the reference that t_rational.c and t_value.c already check the rounding with. Past those bits, a decimal is checked against decimal.h's reading, which chunk 19 checked. The record adds a check on Python's arbitrary-precision rationals, which reaches what 256 bits cannot: decimals of 400 and 11,500 digits, and the fractions nearest the midpoints between long doubles.

  • Alternative: a reference of 54,400 bits, as t_decimal.c has. The values literal.h computes have terms of at most two words, whose products 256 bits hold; the long decimals are decimal.h's, checked there.

Review. Taken: for, a check independent of literal.h, carried past 256 bits by an outside tool; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S89. The smoke tier, not the full matrix, as in S81. Nothing outside t_literal.c includes literal.h (raw/includers.txt). Its 16 builds ran in full, and the smoke tier shows every other test's result unchanged.

  • Alternative: the full matrix, about an hour on two cores, which would show the same.

Review. Taken: as S81. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S90 to S99: chunk 21, 2026.10.15

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk21_2026.10.15/README.md.

S90. The header's design, kept. integer_value, rational_value, parse_number, compare and the six predicates, as the old header declared them. Its internal helpers (big_add, big_mul, normalize and the rest) are gone: integer_value's members do their work. Added: numeric_text (S94), integer_value::digits and !=, and rational_value::is_number (S93). Changed: integer_value is made from d_math_umax, since strict C++98 -m32 has no 64-bit integer for std::uint64_t; its text constructor reads the digits at the start, as strtoul does; its subtraction gives 0 where the result would be negative. The class stays rational_value, as the old header named it: math::rational is constants.hpp's compile-time rational, from C++14.

  • Alternative: the old .cpp's design, integer_constant and decimal_constant with std::strong_ordering, which needs C++20 (the owner chose C++98).

Review. Taken: for, the old design's names and shape kept, built for C++98; against, none of weight. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S91. The arithmetic is the face's, on decimal digits, until bignum. Exact comparison at any length needs storage that grows with the input, and the C core's kernels allocate nothing. So integer_value keeps its digits in a std::string and works on them one at a time. Bignum (P1 to P4) brings binary limbs and faster products to the C core; this face can then stand on them.

  • Alternative: digit kernels in the C core now, on caller buffers, which P1's binary limbs would replace.

Review. Taken: for, exact comparison at any length now; against, decimal digits in a string, one at a time: slow, quadratic in length, and a second big-number arithmetic beside bignum's. Recommended: as taken until bignum (P2 to P4), then this face on its limbs, as planned. Taken.

S92. A power of ten held apart. A rational_value is n / d times 10^k, k of any size, so 1e100000000000000000000 holds a few digits, not 10^20; orders of magnitude settle a comparison before anything is multiplied, and cross products are formed only for numbers within a factor of 100.

  • Alternative: n / d alone, as the old header had it, which makes 10^k's digits. A number with a 20-digit exponent could not be read.

Review. Taken: for, numbers with huge exponents read and compared in a few digits; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.
