S93. Not a number is unordered, as a NaN is; nothing throws. A text that is not one literal, or a fraction over 0, compares as 2, value.h's D_MATH_VALUE_UNORDERED, with everything, itself included; every predicate but not_equal is false for it. parse_number returns it as a rational_value whose is_number is false. The framework is used with exceptions off (the C++ guide's error policy), and value.h already treats a NaN and an error this way.

  • Alternative: throw std::invalid_argument, as the old header's <stdexcept> suggested.
  • Alternative: order non-numbers before every number, a total order for sorting. equal("abc", "xyz") would then be true.

Review. Taken: for, nothing throws, as the framework runs with exceptions off, and a non-number behaves as a NaN does; against, sorting needs its own order. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S94. numeric_text in place of std::string_view. C++98 has no string_view, and re_std's begins at C++11. A pointer and a length, made implicitly from a C string, a std::string and, from C++17, a std::string_view, gives every function one signature at every level.

  • Alternative: an overload of each function for each kind of string, three or four apiece; or re_std's string_view, from C++11 only.

Review. Taken: for, one signature at every level; against, a type of the framework's own where C++17 has string_view. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S95. One syntax, literal.h's. parse_number asks d_math_literal_length whether the whole text is one literal, so the C core and the face read the same numbers: a text the value layer reads is one numeric_compare can compare.

  • Alternative: a syntax of numeric_compare's own. The old .cpp's skipped leading spaces and read no scientific notation or mixed numbers.

Review. Taken: for, the C core and the face read the same numbers; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S96. The .cpp stays, as a translation unit that includes the header. Its neighbours in src/djinterp/core/math/ are the same, and the zip of plain files can replace a file but not delete one.

  • Alternative: delete it, which the owner would then have to do by hand.

Review. Taken: for, a zip of files cannot delete; against, an almost empty unit. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S97. literal.h's scan struct renamed, a fix to chunk 20. Its struct d_internal_math_literal_scan and its function of the same name make g++'s -Wshadow fail in C++ ("hides constructor"), for any file that includes literal.h; the face tests build with -Wshadow. The struct is now d_internal_math_literal_parts; the function keeps its name, so chunk 20's planted faults still apply. raw/shadow_probe.txt shows chunk 20's literal.h failing and this one passing.

  • Alternative: rename the function, which chunk 20's tools name.

Review. Taken: a fix; sound. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S98. A cross-check in Python with methods of its own. The face test's references stop where d_math_umax and the C core's family do; past them, it checks the comparison against rewrites of each number and against itself. Python's Fraction, Decimal and integers check it from outside, up to 300 digits and exponents of 30.

  • Alternative: an exact reference in the face test, as t_literal.c has math_tests_exact.h. Its 256 bits stop well short of a 30-digit exponent.

Review. Taken: for, a check from outside, past the face test's references; against, it runs only in the record. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S99. The smoke tier, not the full matrix, as in S81 and S89. Only the face test includes numeric_compare.hpp, and only t_literal.c and numeric_compare.hpp include literal.h (raw/includers.txt). Their 64 and 16 builds ran in full, and the smoke tier shows every other test's result unchanged.

  • Alternative: the full matrix, about an hour on two cores.

Review. Taken: as S81. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S100 to S112: chunk 22, 2026.10.15

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk22_2026.10.15/README.md.

S100. The registry is the table each kind fills in, and each value carries it. A boxed value points to its kind's struct d_math_boxed_kind, so dispatch needs no lookup, and there is no list of kinds anywhere: no global state, as Phase 1's design asks, and nothing to register at start-up or to keep in step across translation units. A lookup by name, which the parser (2b) may want, can then be a list the caller owns.

  • Alternative: a registry object holding the kinds, numbered as they are added, and a value holding the number. Every operation would need the registry passed to it, so value.h's every signature would change, and values from two registries would not mix.
  • Alternative: one global registry, filled at start-up: global state, in a header-only C core, where a static array is one per translation unit.

Review. Taken: for, no global state, no lookup, values from any unit mixing; against, no list of kinds to enumerate. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S101. One scale of ranks, the inline numbers' included; a rank is its kind's own. The inline numbers stand at 100 to 500, so a boxed kind's rank says where it sits among them as well as among boxed kinds: an exact kind past the rational's, an inexact one past the long double's, a container past every number's. No two kinds share a rank, which makes the rank the kind's identity: in C, every translation unit that includes a kind's header has its own copy of its table, at its own address, so a kind knows its own values by their rank, not by the table's address.

  • Alternative: a kind's place given as "just above" an inline kind, and a separate order among boxed kinds: two fields where one does.
  • Alternative: identity by the table's address, which fails across translation units in C; or by its name, a string compare per operation.

Review. Taken: for, one scale says where a kind stands among all numbers, and gives it an identity across C's units; against, two independently written kinds can choose one rank and be taken for each other, with nothing to detect it. Recommended: as taken, with the ranks in use listed in value.h's banner and a rule for choosing new ones. Taken in part.

S102. The operand higher in the tower handles an operation, an inline number too. Where a boxed kind is the higher, its table does the work; where an inline number is, value.h converts the boxed operand to that number's kind through the kind's convert and carries on as with two inline numbers. So a wide integer meeting a double becomes a double correctly rounded, as an exact rational does, with no code in the kind. For a comparison the boxed number goes to the nearest long double, and convert reports which way it rounded: where the rounding equals the other operand, the direction is the order, so every comparison stays exact, as value.h's own cmp_exact does it for rationals.

  • Alternative: a boxed kind handles every operation with an inline number, which puts conversions to each floating-point type, and exact comparison with each, into every kind.
  • Alternative: Python's reflected operators, the first operand asked first and the second if it declines: two calls where one does, and results that depend on the order.

Review. Taken: for, no conversion code in any kind, comparisons kept exact; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S103. A result is the caller's; operands are lent. Values pass by value in C, so ownership is the caller's to keep: every result is to be given back with d_math_value_release (which does nothing more for an inline value, so a caller need not ask), no operation keeps or releases an operand, and d_math_value_retain makes a copy. release takes a pointer and leaves the value none, so a released value cannot be used by mistake. A kind keeps its boxes as it likes; the harness's kinds count their holders. math::value (1e) will do the retaining and releasing in its copies and destructor.

  • Alternative: operations that consume their operands, which makes a + a a double release.
  • Alternative: release by value, leaving the caller a copy of a freed box.

Review. Taken: for, C's value semantics with one owner, and a released value left none; against, a caller must release every result. Recommended: as taken; math::value does it in C++. Taken.

S104. Errors, booleans and none are settled before any kind is asked. An error passes through, a boolean or none in arithmetic is a domain error, and either beside a boxed value is unordered, exactly as with inline operands. No kind has to handle them, and none can handle them differently.

  • Alternative: hand them to the kind, so that, say, a vector of booleans could take true as an operand. Logic is 2d's, and can add its own operations then.

Review. Taken: for, every kind meets only numbers, and none can disagree on errors; against, a kind cannot give booleans a meaning of its own. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S105. A memory error. A kind that allocates must be able to fail, and value.h's errors are values, so D_MATH_VALUE_ERROR_MEMORY joins them. value.h itself never makes one.

  • Alternative: a range error, which means something else; or failing outside the value, which C would have to do with a global flag.

Review. Taken: for, failure as a value; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S106. One entry for the four operations, and one for the two unary ones, each taking an enum d_math_value_op. A new operation then adds an enumerator, not a table entry: a new entry would change every kind's table, and with -Wextra (missing field initializers) break every kind that had not added it. The four operations were already numbered inside value.h; the numbers are now public.

  • Alternative: one entry per operation, clearer to read in a table, and a change to every kind for each new one.

Review. Taken: for, a new operation changes no kind's table; against, a switch in every kind. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S107. text is in the table, and value.h does not call it yet. The design lists text among a kind's operations, and adding the entry later would change every kind's table (S106). But an inline value has no text of its own yet: how a float is written is the renderer's question (2b), so value.h has no text function to dispatch from.

  • Alternative: leave text out until 2b.

Review. Taken: for, no table change later; against, an entry nothing called then. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S108. t_value_boxed.c registers kinds of its own, which mirror inline values. A real kind (chunk 24) needs a reference of its own; three mirrors of inline values at ranks of their own (150, between the integer and the rational; 250, between the rational and the float; 600, past the long double) make value.h's inline arithmetic the reference for every boxed path, and a fourth, a pair, is a kind that is no number. Every check also counts the calls into the tables, so a result that comes out right by the wrong path is still caught.

  • Alternative: test the registry only with the wide integer, in chunk 24: the protocol and the kind would then be checked through each other.

Review. Taken: for, the inline arithmetic as the reference for every boxed path, and the path itself checked; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S109. Step 9 in three parts. The roadmap said two, the registry and the kind. The kind's arithmetic, wide integers from limbs with division and correct rounding to floating point, is a header and a harness of its own, as rational.h was before value.h; putting it beside its registration as a kind would make one chunk of some 3,000 lines. So: the registry (this chunk); c/math/wide.h, which is P1, step 40, done early (chunk 23); and the wide integer as a boxed kind (chunk 24), whose diff then shows that value.h did not change.

  • Alternative: the integer and its kind together in chunk 23, as the roadmap first had it.

Review. Taken: process; sound. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S110. The exact comparison of a rational and a floating-point value is shared. t_value.c's own copy moved into math_tests_exact.h, as S72 did with the rest of the reference; t_value_boxed.c finds its kinds' rounding directions with it.

  • Alternative: a second copy, about 70 lines, in the new harness.

Review. Taken: as S72. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S111. The smoke tier, not the full matrix, as in S99. Every test that includes value.h or math_tests_exact.h ran in full (raw/includers.txt): t_value, t_value_boxed, t_literal, t_rational and t_numeric_compare_face. The smoke tier shows every other test's result unchanged.

  • Alternative: the full matrix, about an hour and a half on two cores.

Review. Taken: as S81. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S112. Two pointers in every value. type and box are fields of their own, NULL in an inline value, as every unused field holds 0 (S66). A value grows from 48 bytes to 64 on x86-64, and from 40 to 48 at 32 bits (raw/sizes.txt). On x86-64 one pointer would cost as much: a long double aligns the struct to 16 bytes, so 48 plus 8 is 64 too.

  • Alternative: one pointer, to a box that starts with its kind's table: an indirection on every dispatch, a layout imposed on every kind's box, and 4 bytes saved at 32 bits.
  • Alternative: the pointers in place of the unused exact field, through a union, which constant evaluation cannot switch between before C++20 (S65).

Review. Taken: for, dispatch with no indirection and no layout imposed on a kind's box; against, 64 bytes a value on x86-64. Recommended: as taken; the size is the price of constant evaluation before C++20 (S65). Taken.

S113 to S126: chunk 23, 2026.10.17

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk23_2026.10.17/README.md.

S113. One width, 256 bits, in one type, struct d_math_wide. P1 asks for fixed-width integers from limbs and names no width. 256 bits holds the product of any two 128-bit values, and of two d_math_imax with room to spare; it is the next width in common use after 128, as in 256-bit hashes and the Ethereum virtual machine's word; and it keeps a value at 36 bytes, and its arithmetic, a gcd apart, under a hundred nanoseconds on x86-64 (section 2.5). The limb kernels take any length, so another width later is a thin type over them.

  • Alternative: 128 bits, two words: little past what rational.h already does in double width.
  • Alternative: the width set by a configuration macro, which changes the layout of every value for every user of the build.
  • Alternative: a family of widths, 128, 256 and 512, as types of their own: more code than one step needs, before any kind asks for them.

Review. Taken: for, one width that holds the products the value needs, and kernels of any length beneath it; against, a fixed width, until bignum. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S114. Sign and magnitude, not two's complement. The range is then symmetric, so negation and the absolute value never fail; 0 has one form; and the magnitude is a plain natural for the limb kernels, as the products, quotients and roundings want it, and as the boxed kind's ratios will.

  • Alternative: two's complement, -2^255 to 2^255 - 1, as C's integers: addition and subtraction need no sign test, but -(-2^255) overflows, products and quotients take magnitudes anyway, and the positive range is a bit shorter.

Review. Taken: for, a symmetric range, one zero, and magnitudes for the kernels; against, a sign test in addition. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S115. Limbs of half a word, chosen as math_common.h chooses the word. uint32_t where dstdint.h declares intmax_t, which is where the word is 64 bits, and uint16_t where it does not, where the word is 32. A limb times a limb plus two limbs fits a word, so no step needs a product of two words, which rational.h has to build from four half-products; the code is shorter, and as constant-evaluable as the rest. Two static assertions keep the limb at half a word.

  • Alternative: limbs of a word, the products split in halves as rational.h splits them: half the limbs, four multiplications and the carries for each product, and more code in every kernel.

Review. Taken: for, no double-word product, the same code at every width, constant evaluation; against, two to four times the work of word limbs where a 128-bit product exists. Recommended: as taken, with a word-limb path where unsigned __int128 exists, giving the same bits. Taken in part.

S116. Exact or nothing, rational.h's first four statuses by number, and two more. A kernel that fails stores nothing anywhere, divmod's two destinations included, so a caller may pass an operand's own storage as the destination and still have it after a refusal. FRACTION is a rational that is no integer, SYNTAX a text that is no integer.

  • Alternative: one status for every failure, which leaves a caller to work out which it was.

Review. Taken: for, nothing stored on failure, so a caller may reuse an operand's storage; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S117. Division truncates, as C's, and either result may be left out. The quotient goes toward zero and the remainder takes the dividend's sign, as / and % do in C99 and C++11, so a wide integer divides as a C programmer expects. Floor division (Python's) and the Euclidean remainder follow from the truncated pair in a line, by the caller. divmod with a NULL destination gives the other result alone, so there is no div or mod to keep in step.

  • Alternative: floor division, which C programmers would have to unlearn; or separate div and mod, each doing the whole division.

Review. Taken: for, C's division; against, the language's mod is floored, so the two layers differ, each as its users expect. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S118. The limb kernels serve any length, in GMP's mpn style, and Knuth's division takes its scratch from the caller. Chunk 24's ratios need the same kernels at twice the width, and bignum (P2) at any; with the lengths given and the scratch the caller's, they need neither allocation nor a longest length. The type's kernels give them arrays on the stack.

  • Alternative: kernels for 256 bits only, rewritten for 512 in chunk 24, and again for P2.
  • Alternative: division a bit at a time, as the harness's reference does: simpler, with a step for each bit of the quotient where Knuth's takes one for each limb.

Review. Taken: for, one kernel set for 256 bits, 512 and bignum, allocation-free; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S119. The conversions to floating point report the rounding's direction, through a pointer that may be NULL. value.h's convert entry needs it (S102): a boxed number compared with a float goes to the nearest long double, and where that ties, the direction is the order. A second answer through a pointer is how remquo gives its quotient, and remainder.h already takes a NULL one.

  • Alternative: rational.h's form, the value alone, with the direction from an internal function: the kind would repeat to_f's handling of an infinity.

Review. Taken: for, the direction exact comparison needs; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S120. A float past the largest is an infinity of the integer's sign, its direction away from 0. That is IEEE 754's round to nearest, and what decimal.h stores past the range. A double's and a long double's range hold every wide integer, which a static assertion checks, so to_f alone tests for it.

  • Alternative: an overflow status, as decimal.h returns. Its kernels return a status anyway, since a text may be no number; here a value with its direction says as much.

Review. Taken: for, IEEE 754's round to nearest; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S121. Rounding reuses rational.h's result and its builders. The kernel rounds an integer to P bits from its bits, the top P, the one below and whether anything is set lower, into d_internal_rational_rounded, and d_internal_rational_build_f, _d and _ld make the value, so wide.h has no floating-point code of its own. A ratio, as chunk 24 will have, rounds through the same kernel: its quotient carried two bits past P, with a remainder that is not 0 set into the lowest bit.

  • Alternative: a rounding of its own, long division a bit at a time as rational.h's, a step for each bit of the precision, where an integer needs no division at all.

Review. Taken: for, one rounding to trust; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S122. Text is decimal: an optional sign and digits, every character of the text. Leading zeros are allowed, -0 is 0, and a text that is both no integer and too large is SYNTAX: its form is found before its size. d_math_wide_to_text writes as snprintf writes, as interval.h's formatters do, and is D_MATH_FN_RT, math_common.h's rule for writers.

  • Alternative: hexadecimal, digit separators and spaces, which are a literal's business (literal.h), not an integer's.
  • Alternative: to_text as a constant expression into the evaluation's own buffer, which the C core's rule does not allow.

Review. Taken: for, an integer's text and nothing a literal owns; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S123. gcd by Euclid over the same division. One division algorithm to trust, and fast enough: about 6 microseconds for two random integers of 256 bits on x86-64 (section 2.5). The harness's reference finds its gcd the binary way, so the two do not share a mistake.

  • Alternative: Stein's binary gcd, shifts and subtractions only: a second algorithm in the unit.

Review. Taken: for, one division to trust; against, about 6 us, where Lehmer's gcd is several times faster. Recommended: as taken, Lehmer's in P4 where speed is asked. Taken.

S124. t_wide.c has a reference of its own. math_tests_exact.h's integers are 256 bits: short of a product of two operands (512), of the limb kernels' operands at twice the width, and of the oracle's values, which reach 2^256 and pass it. Its limb count cannot be raised for one harness without a second definition of the macro, which the macro lint refuses, nor for all without changing three other harnesses. The new reference, 640 bits, is written another way than wide.h.

  • Alternative: widen the shared reference to 640 bits: its users would run slower, and their checks would change for no gain of theirs.

Review. Taken: for, a reference wide enough, written another way; against, a second reference to keep. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S125. Coverage as a check of the record. gcov counts every line of wide.h that t_wide.c runs, at both limb widths, and the rarer paths of Knuth's division (raw/coverage.txt): every line runs. A line no check reaches is a line no fault planted there could show.

  • Alternative: rely on the planted faults alone, which sample lines.

Review. Taken: for, every line reached by some check; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S126. The smoke tier, not the full matrix, as in S99 and S111. Nothing but wide.h and t_wide.c changed in the code, and nothing includes wide.h but its harness (raw/includers.txt). t_wide's 16 builds ran in full; the smoke tier shows every other test's result unchanged.

  • Alternative: the full matrix, about an hour and a half on two cores.

Review. Taken: as S81. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S127 to S140: chunk 24, 2026.10.17

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk24_2026.10.17/README.md.

S127. The kind is a header of its own, c/math/value_wide.h, over value.h and wide.h, and neither changes. The registration needs both the integer's arithmetic and the value's table; a header beside them that includes both keeps wide.h free of the value layer and of malloc, and shows, by the diff, that value.h took no change (raw/unchanged.txt).

  • Alternative: the kind at the end of wide.h: a program that wants only wide integers would get the value layer and an allocator with them.
  • Alternative: the kind in value.h, the change 1d set out to show a new kind does not need.

Review. Taken: for, wide.h free of the value layer and the allocator, value.h unchanged; against, a header more. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S128. Rank 210. value.h places an exact kind that holds more than d_math_imax past the rational's 200 and below the float's 300. Of those, the wide integer holds the least, a fixed 256 bits; bignum's kinds (P2, P3) will hold every wide integer, so they belong above it, and 210 leaves them 220 to 290.

  • Alternative: 250, the middle, which gives no reason of its own and leaves less room above.
  • Alternative: just below the float's, which leaves bignum's kinds no room above the wide integer.

Review. Taken: for, room above for bignum's exact kinds; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S129. An integer result stays a wide integer, however small. An operation of the kind's gives its own kind where the result is an integer within 256 bits, as an operation of a float gives a float. Until bignum promotes it (P2), an inline integer that overflows becomes the nearest long double (G2), so a wide integer given back as an inline one where it fits would lose the exactness it was made for at the next overflow: a factorial begun from a wide 1 would turn inexact at 21!.

  • Alternative: an integer that fits d_math_imax given back inline, as Ruby and Scheme normalize their bignums. That suits a small kind whose overflow promotes, which d_math_imax's does not yet. P2 can revisit it, when overflow promotes to bigint.

Review. Taken: for, an exact value never turns inexact at the next overflow; against, small integers stay boxed, an allocation each, and arithmetic of 256 bits for numbers that fit a word. Recommended: as taken until bignum promotes overflow (P2); then normalize to the inline kind, as Python and Scheme do. Taken.

S130. No wide rational: a result that is no integer is an inline rational where it fits, and anything past both kinds the nearest long double, flagged inexact. A quotient of two wide integers, or a sum or product with a rational, is a rational; the kind has no rational of its own, so it keeps one of the family inline, and past the family does as G2 does for every exact result that does not fit (value.h, gate G2), as does an integer past 2^256 - 1.

  • Alternative: a range error past the kinds, which G2 already rejected for the inline kinds (1b).
  • Alternative: a wide rational kind, a pair of wide integers: a kind and harness of its own, and much of P3's work, ahead of it.

Review. Taken: for, no new kind ahead of its step; against, a rational of wide terms becomes the nearest long double, flagged inexact: exactness lost where a CAS keeps it. Recommended: as taken as an interim, big rationals in P3, as planned. Ruled 2026.11.04: the interim stands; big rationals at P3, step 45. Flag: significant drawback until P3.

S131. The long double is rounded once from the exact ratio, at twice the width, by wide.h's division. A result's terms run to 512 bits: the product of two wide integers, or a wide integer times a word. Its numerator is moved up until it has P + 2 bits more than its denominator, Knuth's division takes the quotient, a remainder that is not 0 is set into the quotient's lowest bit, and wide.h's rounding kernel rounds it to P bits, as S121 planned. The quotient's bit below the mantissa is its own, so the lowest bit is below it, and the remainder's place is kept exactly.

  • Alternative: rational.h's loop, a bit at a time, on terms of 512 bits: a step of 16-limb arithmetic for each bit of the precision.
  • Alternative: the terms each rounded to long double and divided: two roundings, sometimes an ulp off, and no direction.

Review. Taken: for, rounded once, with its direction; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S132. Every result is a box of its own. No result shares an operand's box, not even |x| of a positive x or x + 0. One allocation per result: a value made and given back takes 17.5 ns on x86-64 (section 2.5), against 239 ns for a sum. And one rule for ownership, which the harness checks on every operation.

  • Alternative: the operand's own box with a holder more where the value is the same: an allocation saved in a few cases, and a second rule to state and test.

Review. Taken: for, one ownership rule, checked on every operation; against, an allocation where a holder more would do. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S133. A lower boxed operand is read through its own table, as an exact number of the family, and only an exact kind is asked. value.h hands the kind any operand no higher than itself; the kind asks an exact one to convert to a rational, and takes what comes back only where the kind says it converted. Anything else, a kind that is no number, an inexact one, or an exact value past the family, gives a domain error, or no order, as value.h gives where an inline number cannot read a lower boxed operand.

  • Alternative: the operands' nearest long doubles, the result inexact: a guess presented as an answer, for a kind value.h's rank rules place above the long double in the first place.

Review. Taken: for, no inexact guess presented as an exact answer; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S134. Storage through two knobs of a configuration header of its own, and holders in a plain integer. config/c/math/cfg_value_wide.h defines D_CFG_MATH_VALUE_WIDE_MALLOC and _FREE, malloc and free unless set before the kind is first included, as fs's allocation hooks are (cfg_file_common.h), and as dconfig's rule wants a module's knobs: in its cfg_*.h, the module reading them only. A host with an arena takes every box with two definitions; the harness counts every box and refuses some. C99 has no atomics, so holders are a size_t, as the harness kinds' were (chunk 22, section 6).

  • Alternative: malloc and free called directly: no arena, and no way for a harness to refuse storage.
  • Alternative: the knobs' defaults in value_wide.h, which dconfig's localization rule forbids.
  • Alternative: one knob for all of math: no other kind allocates yet. When bignum's do, an aggregate default can stand above these, as dconfig's aggregates do.

Review. Taken: for, a host's arena, and a harness that refuses storage; against, holders in a plain size_t: a value copied in two threads at once races, so values cannot be shared across threads. Recommended: as taken for the knobs; holders atomic where C11 or C++11 atomics exist, behind a knob, plain elsewhere. The matrix kind (S237) holds its holders the same way, and the complex and quaternion kinds will (S271, S275): one helper for every kind's holders, in step 23, before those two are written. Taken in chunk 40: every kind's holders counted by value.h's d_math_boxed_hold and d_math_boxed_unhold, atomically by default wherever the compiler can, C89 and C++98 among them, behind the knob D_CFG_MATH_VALUE_ATOMIC_HOLDERS (S326).

S135. The table is a static object of a function, d_math_value_wide_kind, and a result is made under the table of the operand of the kind that handled it. In C each translation unit still has a copy of its own, as value.h expects (S101), and the header defines no object that a unit might not use. Taking the operand's table keeps the kind's code free of a forward declaration, and keeps a value made under one unit's copy under it.

  • Alternative: a static const object in the header.
  • Alternative: every result under the handling unit's own table.

Review. Taken: for, no object a unit might not use, and a value kept under the table it was made under; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S136. Text is the integer's decimal digits, as wide.h writes them, with no mark of the kind. How a value is written is the renderer's question (2b), as S107 left it for the inline kinds.

  • Alternative: a suffix such as w, which no reader accepts yet.

Review. Taken: for, the writing left to the renderer; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S137. The exact arithmetic, the order and the rounding are pure arithmetic, D_MATH_FN; the entries and the constructors, which allocate or read a box, D_MATH_FN_RT. math_common.h's rule, and the harness checks the pure part as constant expressions from C++14.

  • Alternative: everything D_MATH_FN_RT, as no constant expression can make a box: the pure part would then be untestable at compile time for no gain.

Review. Taken: for, the pure part checked as constant expressions; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S138. t_value_wide.c uses the shared reference, at 768 bits, through a size knob. math_tests_exact.h has exact rationals, Stein's gcd, the family test and an oracle for the value of a type nearest a ratio: what the kind's results need. It now takes D_TESTS_EXACT_LIMBS, 16 by default, so its four other includers compile as before, and t_value_wide.c sets 48. This also corrects S124, which said a second limb count would need a second definition that the macro lint refuses: the lint reads headers, and an #ifndef-guarded default with the count set by the .c harness is no second definition.

  • Alternative: t_wide.c's 640-bit naturals moved into a shared header: integers only, with no ratio or oracle for ratios.
  • Alternative: a third reference.

Review. Taken: for, one reference, widened where one harness needs it; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S139. Three kinds of the harness's own below the wide integer, and a counted copy of its table. "small" (exact, 150), "blur" (inexact, 130) and "token" (no number, 120) are the three cases of S133; a refusing small writes a value anyway, as value.h's contract allows, so a kind that read it regardless would be seen. A second copy of the wide integer's table, which counts the calls into it, stands for another translation unit's.

  • Alternative: the wide integer checked alone, as no other boxed kind exists yet: S133's three cases and S135's tables would go untested.

Review. Taken: for, every path of S133 and S135 checked; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S140. The smoke tier, not the full matrix, as in S99, S111 and S126. Every harness that includes a changed header ran in full: t_value_wide, and math_tests_exact.h's four other includers, t_rational, t_value, t_value_boxed and t_literal (raw/includers.txt). The smoke tier shows every other test's result unchanged.

  • Alternative: the full matrix, about an hour and a half on two cores.

Review. Taken: as S81. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S141 to S154: chunk 25, 2026.10.18

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk25_2026.10.18/README.md.

S141. Two classes: math::value, which holds any kind, and math::inline_value, which holds the inline kinds alone. A value that may hold a box must give it back when destroyed, and before C++20 a class whose destructor does anything is no literal type: one class could not both hold boxes and be a constant expression at C++14 and C++17, where value.h's inline operations already are. An operation on two inline values is inline, so inline_value's operations never leave it, and it converts to value implicitly where a box may come.

  • Alternative: math::value alone, a constant expression from C++20 only: value.h's constant expressions lost in C++ at C++14 and C++17.
  • Alternative: one class with no destructor, and holds given back by hand, as in C: the bookkeeping the face is for.

Review. Taken: for, constant expressions at C++14 and C++17 kept in C++; against, two classes to learn, and generic code that must accept both. Recommended: as taken; C++20 lets value be one class for both, which a later step can offer. Taken.

S142. A value holds its box as an int holds its number. A copy retains, a destructor releases, copy assignment copies and swaps, and from C++11 a move takes the hold and leaves none, noexcept. Boxes are never changed once made (S132), so values may share one: a copy needs no storage and cannot fail. Copy and swap keeps a value assigned itself whole, its only holder included; adopt and detach move a hold between C and C++ without a retain or release.

  • Alternative: copy assignment that releases the old value before retaining the new: a value assigned itself, its only holder, gives its box away first (F34 plants it).
  • Alternative: a deep copy of the box: storage for every copy, and a copy that can fail, to guard a box nothing changes.
  • Alternative: no move constructor: a std::vector that grows retains and releases every value it holds (F37 plants a move that may throw).

Review. Taken: for, cheap copies that cannot fail, self-assignment safe, noexcept moves; against, shared boxes need atomic holders for threads (S134). Recommended: as taken, with S134's atomic holders. Taken.

S143. Implicit construction from the arithmetic types, bool, the error codes and d_rational; a pointer refused. A value is a number, so v + 1, 1 + v and x == 0.5 read as they would with a double, and step 11's generic code can take literals. Each type has its own constructor, an exact match, so no call is ambiguous, and char and short promote to int. A pointer would convert to bool, and become a boolean; a deleted constructor template matches every pointer better, so a pointer does not convert. D_DELETED_FN deletes it from C++11; below, it is private and never defined, so a C++98 build stops at it as well.

  • Alternative: explicit constructors: v + math::value(1) everywhere, and no number type that generic code can mix with literals.
  • Alternative: one template constructor for the arithmetic types: needs enable_if or its C++98 equivalent, for what the overloads already do at every level.

Review. Taken: for, a value mixes with literals as a double does; against, implicit conversions can surprise, a long double taken as itself where a double was meant. Recommended: as taken; a number type must convert. Taken.

S144. d_math_value_from_uint in value.h, for the unsigned types. An unsigned integer past D_MATH_IMAX_MAX has no inline integer; G2 says it is the long double nearest it, flagged inexact. value.h builds that from the exact ratio with a function of its own, which the face could not reach without calling into the core's internals, so the core gains a public constructor, 13 lines that call that function, and C callers gain it too. t_value.c checks it against the exact reference at every 2^k - 1, 2^k and 2^k + 1 and 2,000 values drawn at random, and from C++14 as a constant expression.

  • Alternative: the face converting an unsigned past the family to long double itself: a second rounding path beside the core's, and the flag to set by hand.
  • Alternative: a range error past d_math_imax, which G2 decided against for every exact value.

Review. Taken: for, one rounding path, the core's, reachable by C callers too; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S145. The operators, abs and swap are hidden friends, found by argument-dependent lookup only. A free abs(const value&) in djinterp::math would hide ::abs and std::abs from every unqualified call in the namespace; a free compare would be an overload of numeric_compare.hpp's compare of two texts, so compare is a member. A hidden friend is found only where an argument is its class: 1 + v finds value's +, and 1 converts. inline_value does not convert to value's friends' other operand the other way, so an inline_value with a value has one candidate, value's, and the result is a value.

  • Alternative: free operators in the namespace: the hiding above, and every operator call in the namespace weighing a conversion to a value.
  • Alternative: member operators: 1 + v does not compile, as a member's left operand is never converted.

Review. Taken: for, no hiding of ::abs or std::abs, and 1 + v works; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S146. Each comparison is value.h's exact order; == is true only where it is EQUAL; <=> is a std::partial_ordering from C++20. value.h orders across kinds exactly, and leaves a NaN, an error and unlike kinds unordered. The operators follow IEEE 754's comparisons: every one false where the two are unordered, but !=, which is == negated, so an error is not equal to itself, as a NaN is not. partial_ordering's four values are value.h's four.

  • Alternative: a total order, errors and NaNs at one end, as a sort might like: a second order beside the core's. A sort can order by compare with a rule of its own for the unordered.
  • Alternative: two errors with the same code equal: an error is no number, and value.h leaves it unordered with everything.

Review. Taken: for, IEEE 754's rules, the core's exact order, partial_ordering; against, values cannot be sorted by < where NaNs or errors are among them. Recommended: as taken; a sort can order by compare. Taken.

S147. Conversion out is to(T& out), one overload per type, true where the value has one of that type, out as it was where not. Nothing throws, and a value may be no number, so a conversion must be able to say no; value.h's to_* functions say it the same way. One name for every type lets generic code write x.to(d).

  • Alternative: explicit operator double(): a double for none or an error, where there is none.
  • Alternative: std::optional<T> as<T>(): C++17 only.
  • Alternative: a name per type (to_double): what step 11's traits would then have to pick among.

Review. Taken: for, a conversion that can say no, one name for every type; against, an out parameter. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S148. inline_value is a constant expression from C++14, value from C++20, and value's destructor releases only a boxed value. D_CONSTEXPR_CPP14 and D_CONSTEXPR_CPP20 mark the two, as value.h's D_MATH_FN marks its operations from C++14. d_math_value_release tests its argument against NULL before the kind, which a constant expression cannot do with a member of a temporary, so value's destructor tests the kind first: no constant expression holds a box, as no constant expression can make one, so it never calls the release.

  • Alternative: value constant from C++14: not possible, as above (S141).
  • Alternative: value.h's release changed for C++20's sake, where a test in the face suffices.

Review. Taken: for, the most each level allows; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S149. The concept number, from C++20, in value.hpp until step 11. 1e named a number concept from C++20. It asks what generic code uses: copyable, + - * / and unary - giving the type, == and < giving a bool. bool is excluded by name, since true + true is the int 2, which converts back to bool, and value.h holds a boolean as no number. Step 11 moves it beside number_traits, in number_traits.hpp.

  • Alternative: the concept with the traits, in step 11: 1e's item names it, and its test shows both classes satisfy it.
  • Alternative: std::totally_ordered: its semantics require a total order, which a NaN and an error break.

Review. Taken: for, the concept 1e named, satisfied by both classes; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S150. expression.hpp's factory value(v) removed, and math.hpp includes value.hpp. The factory, a second name for constant(v), was a function template math::value, which hid the class wherever both were seen: with the umbrella, math::value x = 1; named the function. No code calls it: every file that includes expression.hpp, directly or not, is in the C++ ladder's directories, whose verdicts are chunk 24's (raw/includers.txt, raw/ladder_cpp_compare.txt). constant(v) does what it did.

  • Alternative: another name for the class: the roadmap, the C core and every document call it the value.
  • Alternative: the umbrella left without value.hpp: the clash only postponed to the first program that includes both.

Review. Taken: for, the class's name kept, the clash removed, nothing called it; against, a function removed from another module. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S151. The face test checks the face against the C core, and has no Python check of its own. The face adds no arithmetic. What it can get wrong is which call it makes, on which operands, with which holds, so the test runs every operation of the face beside value.h's on the same C values, over every pair of 57 operands, and counts holds through a counted table. The C core's harnesses check value.h against exact references and Python's fractions (chunks 18, 22 and 24).

  • Alternative: a Python driver through the face: value.h's answers checked a second time, and nothing of ownership.

Review. Taken: for, the face checked where it can go wrong, ownership included; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S152. Nothing committed; the record's checks run on copies of the working tree. The owner's instruction of 2026.10.18. Each run of verify_queue_c25.sh copies the tree, without .git and _handoffs, and checks the copy. files_zip_c25.py takes what the working tree has, git's files changed and new ones not ignored, against the base; zip_check_c25.sh compares a base with the zip over it to the working tree. Chunk 16's tools took two commits.

  • Alternative: commits on the lane's branch, as before: what the owner stopped.

Review. Taken: the owner's ruling. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S153. The smoke tier, not the full matrix, as in S99, S111, S126 and S140; every test that includes a changed header, directly or not, in full. include_closure_c25.py follows the includes (raw/includers.txt): value.h reaches six tests, t_value_face and the five of step 3, all run in full, 192 builds; value.hpp reaches t_value_face alone; expression.hpp, which lost a function no one called, reaches t_constants_face, in the smoke tier, and ten headers, all on the C++ ladder. The smoke tier shows every other test's result unchanged.

  • Alternative: the full matrix, about an hour and a half on two cores.

Review. Taken: for, every includer of a changed header run in full, the rest in the smoke tier; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S154. Each operation makes value.h's result in place. Two private constructor templates in each class take a type that names one of value.h's operations, with its operands, and initialize the value's member from the call, so the result is made where the value keeps it, with no copy between. The first version took the result through the constructor that adopts a C value, and g++ copied it once more: 19 instructions more in each sum at C++20, of either class and either kind, and 19 to 21 at ISO strict C++98, as callgrind counts them, a count no machine's speed changes (adopted_c25.sh, raw/adopted.txt). What the copy costs in time depends on the machine. On the queue's, at C++20, the first version's integer sum took 36.5 ns against value.h's 23.5 in the same run (raw/timing_adopting.txt), where in place it takes 22.0 against 19.6 (raw/timing.txt). On a later machine, the two versions run in turn, it took 48.0 ns against 45.9 in place, and the wide sum 245.2 against 228.1, though value.h's own wide sum, the same code in both builds, differed by 8.7 ns between them (section 8). In place, the face costs what value.h's call does on both. value's assignment forms still copy and swap, around a result made in place: they count 7 instructions more than the first version's at C++20 and 17 fewer at ISO strict C++98, and take the same time within 0.2 ns at C++20 and 3.7 ns less at ISO strict C++98 -m32. The templates stand at the top of each class, above the operators that use them: clang reads a template's body only where it stands, and an instantiation it made before then is no constant expression.

  • Alternative: the operation as a pointer to value.h's function, tried first: value.h's functions are always_inline outside testing builds, and at -O1 with the sanitizers g++ could not inline a call through the pointer, an error (raw/pointer_sanitizers.txt), though -O0 and -O2 built.
  • Alternative: the adopting constructor taking the C value by value: the parameter is made in place, then copied into the member in its turn.
  • Alternative: a private constructor per operation, each with a tag: twelve constructors where four do.

Review. Taken: for, the face costs what the C call costs; against, a subtler construction. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S155 to S166: chunk 26, 2026.10.18

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk26_2026.10.18/README.md.

S155. number_traits<T>, static functions in a class template, says what generic code needs of a number type. It is the one place a template asks for a type's zero, root or tolerance, and a type joins by specializing it, as a bignum would. Its flags take std::numeric_limits's names where they mean the same. Its members are functions, not constants: at C++98 a static constant in a class must be integral, and a value's zero is not.

  • Alternative: std::numeric_limits specialized for the value classes: it says nothing of roots or conversions, and most of its members, max(), epsilon(), mean nothing for a type that holds any kind.
  • Alternative: free functions found by argument-dependent lookup, zero(x), sqrt(x): the arithmetic types' would live in djinterp::math and hide ::sqrt, as S145 found for abs.

Review. Taken: for, one place for what generic code asks, open to any type; against, a trait class where C++20 has concepts. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S156. The primary template serves any type as the generic path always took one. Made from an int, through double, ordered by <, its tolerance 1e-12 cast to the type, is_specialized false. Every element type the linear-algebra headers took before still compiles, and computes as it did. An integral type it serves, a character type or bool, it calls exact and an integer, as the language does, so inverse refuses a matrix of char as it did (F3 plants the fault).

  • Alternative: no primary definition, so a type without traits stops the build: every user type that worked before would stop.
  • Alternative: traits of their own for char and the other character types: types no one computes with as numbers, which the language already says are integers.

Review. Taken: for, every element type that compiled before still computes as it did; against, a user's type without traits has its roots and tolerances taken through double silently, an accuracy loss a high-precision type would not expect. Recommended: as taken for compatibility, with is_specialized false reported where an exact or precise answer is asked (a static assertion in inverse and the roots, say). Taken in part.

S157. The arithmetic types' traits: an integer type exact, its root the double's truncated, its tolerance 0; a floating-point type inexact, its own correctly rounded root, its tolerance 1e-12 in the type. The integer types keep what the generic path computed. The floating-point types take the C core's root, as their C kernels already did: d_math_sqrt_c in a constant expression, from C++14, and the C library's at run time, which the C core keeps the same bit for bit.

  • Alternative: an integer's root rounded to nearest: a change to what the generic path gave (F4 plants it).
  • Alternative: a long double's root through double: two roundings, and a different answer (F7 plants it).

Review. Taken: for, the C core's correctly rounded roots for the floating-point types; against, two weaknesses: an integer's root was the double's truncated, off by one past 2^53, where an exact integer square root is cheap; and the tolerance was an absolute 1e-12 in every type, below a float's resolution (as S46 found for the eigenvalues) and blind to the operands' scale. Recommended: an exact integer square root, and a relative tolerance of a few epsilons of the type times the operands' scale, as S46 chose for the eigenvalues. Taken in chunk 38: the integer root exact, rounded down, and the tolerance four epsilons relative to the larger magnitude (S303, S304).

S158. The value classes' traits: inexact; the root exact for the square of a rational of the family, and the long double's otherwise. A value may hold a float or G2's long double, so its arithmetic is exact only where its operands are, and is_exact says no. A root is exact where it can be, so a vector of values has an exact norm and an exact unit vector wherever they are rationals; elsewhere the long double's root, as the floating-point types have theirs. An exact negative number has no root, a domain error, as the C library reports one for sqrt, a wide integer past the family among them, which reads as no rational; an inexact one's is a NaN. The tolerance is the double 1e-12: a value compares as a double does.

  • Alternative: is_exact true: generic code that trusts it would compare values holding doubles with no tolerance.
  • Alternative: every root through the long double: the norm of (3, 4) a long double 5, and no exact unit vector (F12 plants it).
  • Alternative: a negative number's root a NaN wherever it reads as no rational: a negative wide integer's root no error (F14 plants it).
  • Alternative: a tolerance of the integer 0: approximate comparisons of values that hold doubles would ask for equality (F16 plants it).

Review. Taken: for, exact roots wherever the answer is rational; against, the same absolute 1e-12 for every value, exact ones included, which compared exact rationals with a tolerance they do not need. Recommended: tolerance 0 where both operands are exact, and S157's relative tolerance otherwise. Taken in chunk 38: a pair's tolerance 0 where both are exact, else four of the larger epsilon times the larger magnitude (S303).

S159. common_number<A, B> is the type of a + b, from C++98. For two arithmetic types C++98 tells it through sizeof, which evaluates nothing, from the overload of a function a + b would call; from C++11 any other pair through decltype; the value classes say theirs at every level. Where there is none, there is no type, so a use stops the build where it is, not inside the template that used it.

  • Alternative: std::common_type: C++11, and the conditional operator's type, not the sum's: bool for two bools, whose sum is an int.
  • Alternative: decltype alone: nothing below C++11, where the lane's rungs start.

Review. Taken: for, the sum's type, from C++98; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S160. The concept number moves to number_traits.hpp, unchanged. As S149 planned: the concept and the traits are the static side together, and value.hpp includes them.

  • Alternative: the concept left in value.hpp: a template that asks for a number would include the value classes to name it.

Review. Taken: as planned. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S161. Linear algebra's generic path takes its constants, roots, conversions and tolerances from number_traits. The generic path is every element type's but the three floating-point types'. Its casts of 0, 1, 2 and 0.9995, and its roots through double, gave a value the right constants but an inexact root, and a type with no conversion from int or to double none at all. Through the traits, each type computes in its own way, and the types that had no traits before compute as they did.

  • Alternative: a second generic path for the value classes: two paths to keep in step.
  • Alternative: the default tolerance left as 1e-12 cast to the type: a type whose author chose another, as the test's rational does, would not get it (F22 plants it).

Review. Taken: for, each type computes in its own way, through one path; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S162. A vector or matrix made by default is zero in any number type. An arithmetic type's components are value-initialized, as before, a constant expression from C++11; any other type's are set to its traits' zero. A value made by default is none, so a vector of values made by default would otherwise hold none, and vector() would not be zeros(). From C++11 two default constructors, chosen by the element type, keep the arithmetic types' a constant expression from C++11; at C++98, where nothing is a constant expression, one constructor tests the type.

  • Alternative: one constructor that sets every type's zero: the arithmetic types' default construction a constant expression from C++14 only, a loop in its body.
  • Alternative: none by default for the value classes: every sum with a vector made by default none, an error component by component (F23 to F26 plant it).

Review. Taken: for, vector() zero in every type, a constant expression from C++11 for the arithmetic types; against, two constructors. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S163. A vector's components may be the element type's own values. The component constructors took arithmetic types alone. They now take the element type too, so a vector of values is written with values, a wide integer among them, and a vector of a user's rational with its rationals. Any other type stays out, so no component constructor is taken for a copy, or for construction from an array.

  • Alternative: any type that converts to the element type: a vector of values would take a vector of doubles as one component, and C++98 has no standard conversion test.

Review. Taken: for, vectors written in their own values; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S164. inverse takes any element type that number_traits does not call an integer type. It took floating-point types alone. A value's inverse is exact where its entries are rationals of the family, and so is a user's exact rational's; an integer matrix has no integer inverse in general, and stays refused, by static assertion.

  • Alternative: floating-point types and the value classes only: a user's exact type refused for no reason.

Review. Taken: for, exact inverses for exact types; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S165. D_MATH_FN_ENTRY, for a function a table points to: a header inline no compiler is told it must inline. A boxed kind's entries are reached through the kind's table, so a forced inline gains nothing there, and at gcc's -Og stops the build. In C the qualifier is a static D_ENV_COMPILER_INLINE, so each translation unit has its own copy, as D_INLINE gives; in C++ an inline function, so the copies are one.

  • Alternative: D_MATH_FN_RT never forced: every run-time kernel's inlining changed, for a fault in table entries alone.
  • Alternative: no change: no program that includes value_wide.h builds at -Og.

Review. Taken: for, tables build at every level; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S166. The full tier, in four parts, and every test's lines against its last full run. math_common.h is included by every C core header, so every test includes a changed header, and runs in full, as S153 has it. The tier runs as four parts, the harnesses and then the face tests ten at a time, so that the queue can stop and start between runs: a background command here may run two hours, and the harnesses alone took 85 minutes. The parts' results are joined, line for line, as one run's would be, and each test's lines are compared with its last full run in an earlier record, so that a count that moved shows, not only a failure.

  • Alternative: the smoke tier: the full tier is what S153 asks of a test that includes a changed header.
  • Alternative: one run of the whole tier: past the two hours on this machine.

Review. Taken: for, the full tier within two hours a part, counts compared line by line; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S189 to S200: chunk 29, 2026.10.23

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk29_2026.10.23/README.md.

S189. Numbers by decimal.h's grammar: digits, a point, an exponent, from a digit or a point before one, never a sign nor a fraction. A sign is an operator, and 2/3^2 must be a division, 2/9.

  • Alternative: literal.h's grammar, fractions and mixed numbers among it: 2/3^2 would be (2/3)^2 and 1 1/2 one number.

Review. Taken: for, 2/3^2 a division, as every language reads it; against, fractions not literals. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S190. The parser layer is a pass that gives each token its role before parse/expression's climber sees it, then a check of the brackets, then the climb; its errors are mapped to the statuses at their characters. The shared parser cannot see where a minus stands (chunk 12's probe), and counts tokens, not characters (chunk 27).

  • Alternative: a climber of the lane's own, with errors of its own: gate G4 builds the language on the shared parser.

Review. Taken: for, gate G4's shared parser kept; against, the parser layer now does most of the work itself (roles, groups, chains, errors by character), and the shared climber's limits leak: groups put back as atoms, a postfix operator with operands awkward to express, folds that stop on a term made by default. 3b's indexing went through it (S252), as a postfix operator of a table the parser layer adds, its indices put back after the climb. The alternative, a precedence parser of the language's own over the same term type, would be simpler and more capable, the term and its folds still shared. Recommended: the alternative, before Phase 4's rewrites and any further operator make the layer larger. Ruled 2026.11.04: decided on 2026.11.06 with lane F against expression core v2's design: core v2 if its parser holds postfix operators and calls, S209 and S252 then following it; otherwise the language's own precedence parser, as S190's review recommends. Flag: not the recommendation; a growing cost.

S191. G5's precedences as the table's, ten apart: relations 10, or 20, and 30, not 40, + - 50, * / mod 60, the unary - and + 70, ^ 80, the postfix 90; ^ to the right; + and * commutative and associative. The order is G5's, the roadmap's recommendation, which the lane takes as the owner has not said otherwise: so -x ^ 2 is -(x²). The numbers are the lane's: ten apart leaves room for a level between two.

  • Alternative: the levels 1 to 9: a level between two would renumber every level above it.

Review. Taken: for, G5's order, the unary minus below ^ as in mathematics, room between levels; against, none. Recommended: as taken (with S213). Taken.

S192. a mod b is the floored remainder, with b's sign, as Python's %, MATLAB's mod and Mathematica's Mod. It is what "mod" means in mathematics: -7 mod 3 is 2.

  • Alternative: C's fmod, the truncated remainder with a's sign, as MATLAB's rem: -7 mod 3 would be -1.

Review. Taken: for, "mod" as mathematics and Python mean it; against, differs from C's fmod. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S193. ^ and mod on values follow value.h's tower: errors pass, the first; a boolean or none is a domain error; exact where the result can be, ^ for whole exponents and for rational ones of whole roots, mod for exact operands; a negative exact base to a power not whole a domain error, exact 0 to a negative power a pole; otherwise the C core's function in the tower's kind, the operands' flags carried. value.h has neither, and the fold to a value is "exact where possible" (the roadmap).

  • Alternative: ^ exact for whole exponents alone: 4 ^ 0.5 would be the long double 2.

Review. Taken: for, exact wherever the result can be; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S194. The typed fold computes ^ and mod with the C core's plain forms, the C library's, as the roadmap's "typed and fast" asks.

  • Alternative: the _c forms, correctly rounded and the same in constant evaluation: slower, and the typed fold is no constant expression.

Review. Taken: for, the typed fold fast; against, its bits are the platform library's, so one term in double can differ by an ulp between platforms. Recommended: as taken, plus a reproducible mode, the _c forms in the typed fold, for callers who want the same bits everywhere. Taken in part.

S195. A number a fold made is written exactly where exact, as the shortest decimal that reads back in its type where floating, and by name where it has no literal. 0.1 as a double is written 0.1, and reads back as that double in double.

  • Alternative: a floating number's exact decimal expansion, which reads back as the same number as a value too: 0.1 as a double would take 55 digits.

Review. Taken: for, the shortest text that reads back, as Python and JavaScript write; against, a double read back as a value is the exact decimal, not the double (it reads back the same in its type). Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S196. Implied multiplication, where on, is a * between an operand's end and the next one's start, as tight as *; never between two numbers, nor before a call's bracket. 1/2x is (1/2) x, as in Mathematica.

  • Alternative: implied multiplication tighter than /, as some calculators have it: 1/2x would be 1/(2x).

Review. Taken: for, Mathematica's reading, 1/2x as (1/2) x; against, some calculators read 1/(2x). Recommended: as taken; implied multiplication is off by default. Taken.

S197. What a later step brings answers not_yet with its step, at its place where it has one; and, or and not are kept from variables now, so that a text means the same before 2d and after. A constant's name is another matter: pi and e are variables until 2c, which fills the constant table and so decides which names are constants.

  • Alternative: such names variables until their step: and would be a variable now and an operator after 2d.

Review. Taken: for, a text means the same before and after each step; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S198. The face test grows in place: each stub check of 2b's entries becomes a check of what it does; random terms round-trip; random whole terms check the typed folds against the exact one. S185 has each step turn its stubs' checks into checks of what it fills.

S200. Three statuses widened: wrong_arity for an operator applied to a number of operands it does not take, unknown_name for an operator of no kind, missing_operand for a term made by default. The folds meet such terms only where a caller made them, and must answer something.

S201 to S212: chunk 30, 2026.10.23

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk30_2026.10.23/README.md.

S201. A call is a matter of syntax: a name before an open round bracket is a call of the function of that name, whatever the name, and a fold looks the function up when it meets the call. The parser needs no function table, so a text means the same with any table; a name no table has is the fold's unknown_name, at the call. With implied multiplication on, x(y) is therefore a call, never x·y, as S196 has it; 2(y) and (x)(y) are products. A function's name alone, sin, is a variable.

  • Alternative: the parser looks each name up, and a name before a bracket that no function has is a product or an error: a text's meaning would change with the table, and an unknown function would be found before its arguments are parsed.

Review. Taken: for, a text's meaning independent of any table, undefined functions kept as symbols, as computer algebra systems keep them; against, a typo is found only at the fold. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S202. The constants are a table, like the functions, and a name it has is a constant at parse; their values are the 40-digit literals of constants::of<T>, written in function.hpp rather than taken from constants.hpp. From C++14, constants.hpp includes math/expression.hpp, whose x, y, z, t, r, rho, theta and phi stand in djinterp::math: with it, every test and caller that names a variable x meets -Wshadow errors, as the lane's first build did. The test checks each type's value against of<T>'s.

  • Alternative: include constants.hpp: one source of the digits, and those names beside this language's from C++14.

Review. Taken: for, no names leaked into the language from constants.hpp; against, two sources of the digits, checked against each other by the test. Recommended: as taken, and the cause removed: math/expression.hpp's variables x, y, z and the rest moved into a namespace of their own (chunk 30's finding). Taken in part.

S203. The standard functions are the C core's 25, each of the number of arguments C gives it and named as in C, with abs and factorial; log is the natural logarithm. They are what the C core has kernels for in every type, and G5 names log natural.

  • Alternative: more names (ln, lg, sign, min, max, gamma): each a decision of its own, and gamma a kernel the C core has not.

Review. Taken: for, every function a C core kernel in every type; against, a narrower set than other systems': no min, max, sign, gamma, erf, inverse hyperbolic functions, log1p or expm1. Chunk 37 put log1p, expm1 and the inverse hyperbolic functions in the C core, in both forms and every family (S286); the table has none of them yet. Recommended: those five in the table in step 23 (S271), and min, max and sign with them (exact, no kernel needed), sign(z) being z / |z| of a complex z; gamma, erf and the rest with their kernels; chunk 40 placed the eight in step 23's second part, chunk 41 (S324), which put them in the table (S338), each with its complex value but the next chunk's two (S335); gamma, erf and the rest wait for their kernels, which no step has yet. Ruled 2026.11.04: gamma, erf and the rest wait for their kernels, the lane to place them: at step 36 (6a), gamma, lgamma, erf and erfc, with the correctly rounded forms (S379). Flag: functionality gap, now gamma, erf and the rest alone.

S204. In a type, a function is the C core's plain form, as ^ is (S194); factorial(n) the product rounded as it is made, for a whole n ≥ 0, and a NaN for any other n. The typed folds are the fast ones.

  • Alternative: the _c forms, correctly rounded and the same in constant evaluation; and for the factorial, Γ(n + 1), which the C core has not.

Review. Taken: for, the fast plain forms; against, the factorial of a non-integer a NaN, where other systems give Gamma(n + 1). Recommended: as taken, Gamma with its kernel. Taken.

S205. As a value, a function is exact wherever its value is a rational number of the family; an exact argument outside its domain is a domain error, and the logarithm of 0 a pole; elsewhere its value is the C core's in the tower's kind, its arguments' flags carried. Exact where possible (the roadmap), and by Lindemann and Weierstrass the transcendental functions are rational at their one point each and nowhere else, so the rule loses no exact value. The errors are value.h's, as ^'s are (S193).

  • Alternative: exact only where the result is plainly exact (abs, the roundings, the remainders): sqrt(4) would be the long double 2.

Review. Taken: for, exact wherever the value is rational, which by Lindemann and Weierstrass loses no exact value; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S206. The factorial is postfix at G5's postfix level, 90, tighter than ^, and to the left. So 2 ^ 3! is 2⁶, -3! is -(3!), and n!! is (n!)!, not the double factorial.

  • Alternative: !! as the double factorial, a token of its own: a function the C core has not, and n!! would mean two things.

Review. Taken: for, one meaning for !; against, Mathematica reads n!! as the double factorial. Recommended: as taken, documented. Taken.

S207. With G5's bars on, |x| is the call abs(x): a bar opens where no operand ends before it or where a bar is not the innermost group open, and closes after an operand where one is. ||x| - 1| and |x| |y| read as written, and |x| round-trips as abs(x).

  • Alternative: bars as a bracket of the climber's: parse/expression's climber knows one kind of bracket, whose open and close differ, and a bar is both.

Review. Taken: for, bars read as written, round-tripping as abs; against, an option that changes the grammar. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S208. The renderer describes a call to parse/expression's renderer as an operator of its own, spelled as its function, through an adapter over the table. The renderer writes an operator it has no description of as ?(a, b), and the table describes kinds: every call is one kind, of many names.

  • Alternative: a renderer of the lane's own for calls: two renderers to keep in step, with the brackets each needs.

Review. Taken: for, one renderer; against, an adapter to keep. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S209. The parser layer reads each call and each pair of bars as one operand before the climb, an atom standing in its place, and puts it back after; of an argument's error and the climb's, the further left is given. The climber's leaves are atoms (gate G4 builds on it as it is, S190), and the first error in the text is the one a reader looks for.

  • Alternative: the climber taught calls: parse/expression is shared, and P0 (chunk 27) changed only what stopped it compiling.

Flag: follows from S190.

S210. ^'s and mod's arithmetic moves from evaluate.hpp to function.hpp, beside the functions'; the folds take functions from the standard tables alone. pow is ^, and fmod, remainder and mod are one remainder rounded three ways: one header holds what each computes.

  • Alternative: the arithmetic left in evaluate.hpp, which function.hpp would then include, and every caller of the tables the folds with it.

S211. The face test grows in place: the stub checks of calls and constants become checks of what they do; calls, constants and the factorial join the random round trip; a boxed kind of the test's own, not exact, checks the tower's kind for it; and each answer of the C core's a fold is held to is computed as the test runs, by the C library.
