The derivative of a function with no complex derivative, abs, conj, real, imag, arg or sign, is not_differentiable of a variable not declared, and the real derivative of a real one, as SymPy's and Mathematica's derivatives take a symbol to be complex.

  • Alternative: variables real unless declared, as the guarantee is written now: the derivative of |x| sign(x) with no declaration, but sqrt(x ^ 2) = |x| wrong at x = im once a rule of the reals is added.
  • Alternative: declared matrix variables alone (S251's review).

Review. Ruled: for, no rewrite ever wrong at a binding a value can have, and the real rules where a variable is declared so; S251's matrix variables one case of a general declaration; against, the derivative of abs of a real variable needs its declaration. Recommended: as ruled. Ruled; step 25.

S275. Quaternions are a boxed kind of the value, in step 24: Hamilton's, w + x im + y jm + z km with im jm = km, of rank 950, past the complex kind's and below the matrix's; a complex operand is taken in with its im the quaternion's first unit. Its parts, storage and exactness are as S266 makes them, dense as the C core's array of four in its order, [x, y, z, w], the order of glTF, Eigen and GLM, so that a renderer takes one as it is, and values otherwise. An exact quaternion whose jm and km parts are exactly 0 is complex, and real where its im part is too; an inexact one stays a quaternion. jm and km are constants beside im; quat(w, x, y, z) makes one of its parts and parts(q) gives them; with real, conj, abs and the bars, inv, sign, exp, log, sqrt and ^ of a real exponent, each its principal value: log q = log|q| + u atan2(|v|, w), v = |v| u its vector part, u = im where v is 0, so that log(-1) is pi im, as a complex number's is. A non-real exponent is a domain error, as its two orders give two values. For rotations, rotate(q, v) of a 3-vector, slerp(p, q, t), the rotation matrix of a unit quaternion and the quaternion of a rotation matrix; their names are settled in the step.

  • Alternative: i, j and k as constants, never again variables (S202); or quaternions a C++ type alone, never a value, so that no term holds one.
  • Alternative: the order [w, x, y, z], the mathematician's and Boost's: a copy for each call a renderer makes.

Review. Ruled: for, quaternions of every kind the tower has, a complex number one of them; the layout renderers pass, so the C core's array goes to them as it is; principal values that agree with the complex ones; against, two more names, jm and km, lost to variables, and an exact quaternion boxed. Recommended: as ruled. Ruled; step 24.

S276. The C core's quaternions, quat.h, keep their conventions and grow, for the kind and for rendering, in step 24: Hamilton's product, right-handed, a rotation active, v' = q v q, as glTF's, Eigen's and GLM's are, never JPL's; the array [x, y, z, w], 16 bytes in float, which a renderer can align, upload and vectorize as it is. Added are the inverse, the right and the left quotient, exp, log, sqrt and powers of a real exponent; the scaled norm (S256) in normalize; a rotation matrix's quaternion by Shepperd's method, the largest of four candidates; the quaternion turning one vector to another, the antiparallel case among them; nlerp, and slerp along the shorter arc, q or -q, accurate near angle 0. The product's four parts are each a compensated sum of four products, as accurate as one computed in twice the precision and rounded once; today's formulas stay as the _fast forms of the product, the rotation and normalize, the last by a reciprocal square root, for a renderer's inner loops, in the same layout and conventions (6e's rule, its use the owner's). A function with a sine or a cosine has its _c form, as now, and quaternion.hpp's faces take each.* Today's callers of the product and the rotation get other last bits, more accurate; the _fast forms give today's.

  • Alternative: today's formulas as the default, an accurate form beside them: a renderer's speed by default, but a part's error unbounded where products cancel.
  • Alternative: dual quaternions now, for rigid motions and skinning: a pair of quaternions, left until a renderer asks (S278).

Review. Ruled: for, the conventions of glTF, Eigen and GLM kept; each part of a product bounded by default; a renderer's speed by the _fast forms, of the same layout; the functions a renderer and the kind need, Shepperd's and a shorter-arc slerp among them; against, today's callers' bits change, and a compensated product is about three times the operations of today's. Recommended: as ruled. Ruled; step 24.

S277. Quaternions do not commute, in step 24: p / q is p inv(q), the quotient on the right, as MATLAB's / divides, and inv(q) p is written so. Simplification keeps a quaternion's factors in their order, as a matrix's (S251), and im among them in any product that holds jm, km or a quaternion, since im jm is km and jm im is -km. A matrix of quaternions has sums, products, the conjugate transpose A', and inv and solve by Gauss-Jordan in each product's order (S269's kernels); eig gives its standard right eigenvalues, complex numbers of imaginary part 0 or more, those of the complex matrix of twice its size that represents it (Zhang's), by S269's complex QR; and det is a domain error, as no determinant of quaternion matrices is both multiplicative and the complex one on complex matrices.

  • Alternative: eig of a quaternion matrix a domain error too: no answer where Zhang's gives one.
  • Alternative: p / q the quotient on the left, inv(q) p, MATLAB's \.

Review. Ruled: for, one quotient, the right one, as MATLAB's; the order of factors kept wherever it matters; Zhang's eigenvalues, the standard answer, on the complex QR step 22 brings; against, no determinant, as none is both multiplicative and the complex one. Recommended: as ruled. Ruled; step 24.

S278. The other kinds the lane may need, each by S266's rules: intervals, a boxed kind as the roadmap's design plans it, their endpoints of one kind, exact ones exact and floating-point ones rounded outward, their step the owner's to place; algebraic numbers, exact roots such as sqrt(2), so that eig and a polynomial's roots are exact throughout, after Phase 8's big integers, which their coefficients need; and dual quaternions, for rigid motions and skinning, a C core type and a C++ face when a renderer asks. Not kinds: octonions, which do not associate, so that a chain a b c would have no one value, and which nothing planned needs; Clifford algebras, one general kind harder to make accurate and fast than complex numbers and quaternions are each; and dual numbers, as Phase 4's derivatives are symbolic and exact. Each waits in the roadmap's "Later" until it is placed.

  • Alternative: one kind of Clifford algebras for complex numbers, quaternions, dual numbers and the rest; or kinds made as uses ask, with no rules in common.

Review. Ruled: for, every kind to come made by S266's rules, so that none needs a design of its own; the kinds no use asks for left out, each for a reason; against, intervals and algebraic numbers still without a step. Recommended: as ruled. Ruled; each placed when the owner places it.

S279 to S290: chunk 37, 2026.10.27

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk37_2026.10.27/README.md.

S279 to S290, chunk 37's, as decisions.html has them; each is reviewed there, none flagged, and the 22 outstanding decisions of S1 to S290 come first, by group and urgency, as chunk 36's made them (section 2.6). The text below is decisions_c37.md, which decisions_c37.py reads.

S279. Step 22 (3d) is made in three chunks, each delivered and checked whole: chunk 37 the C core's complex numbers, cplx.h, with C99's 22 functions and the five real functions they need (S280 to S288); chunk 38 the linear algebra S269 names, Francis's double-shift QR after balancing, the eigenvectors of the real Schur form, complex LU, inverse and solution, Hermitian Jacobi and complex QR, the C core's norms scaled (S256) and its elimination of [A | B] (S257), and number_traits written again (S157, S158); chunk 39 math::complex<T> (S270), im, jm and km answering not_yet (S271, S275), the stubs' step numbers made new (S265), and the guard chunk 36's faults asked of t_expr_face.cpp's empty matrix. Each part is a chunk the record can check whole, its references and its faults with it; the step's flags, S45, S47, S49, S256, S257, S157 and S158, close with chunk 38, which needs the complex type this chunk makes.

  • Alternative: step 22 in one chunk, the C core's largest change yet in one delivery, its parts' faults and references checked together.

Review. Taken: for, each part checked whole, its references and faults with it, and the step's flags closing in its second part, which needs the type the first makes; against, three deliveries for one step. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S280. The C core's complex numbers are struct d_cplx_f, struct d_cplx_d and struct d_cplx_ld, in c/math/cplx.h; their functions are d_cplx_<S>_<fn> and d_cplx_<S>_<fn>_c, d_cplx_d_exp and d_cplx_d_exp_c, and in C++ also one name for every family, d_cplx_<fn> and d_cplx_<fn>_c, chosen by the argument's type, as d_math_<fn> is for the real functions. Each struct's one member is part[2], as S267 has it, and none is typedef'd, as the C guide asks. Beside C99's 22 functions, creal to catanh as real to atanh, are make, neg, add, sub, mul, div and equal of complex numbers, and add_real, sub_real, real_sub, mul_real, div_real and real_div of a complex number and a real one. This refines S267's d_complex_d: the C core's other types are named short, d_vec_, d_mat_, d_quat_, and a header named complex.h would be found for C99's <complex.h> by a build with c/math/ on its include path.

  • Alternative: S267's names, d_complex_d in a complex.h: a prefix longer than the core's others, and a header that can shadow the standard's.
  • Alternative: d_complex_d in cplx.h: the type named apart from its header and its functions.

Review. Taken: for, the core's short prefixes kept, no header that can shadow the standard's, and one C++ name a template calls for every family; against, a name other than S267's, as ruled. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S281. The complex arithmetic comes once, pure, a constant expression from C++14, with no plain form. A product's part is a sum of two exact products, each split by Dekker's method, its low parts kept and the sum rounded once, in every build: never fma, which no constant expression has before C++23 and the x87's long double has only in software. A quotient is the conjugate product over the squared modulus, both carried as exact pairs, the operands first scaled by powers of 2 so that nothing on the way overflows or underflows, and then a residual's step: q of the high parts, the residual u - q n formed exactly of Dekker's product of q and n's high part, and q plus the residual over n, rounded once. Where an operand is infinite, Annex G's recovery (G.5.1): an infinity times a nonzero number is an infinity, and a nonzero number over a zero is one. An operation of a complex and a real number takes the real as itself, not as x + 0i (G.5): z x is a x + b x i, so that a zero's sign survives and no infinity meets a zero. This refines S268's arithmetic: its product, Kahan's, formed without fma, and its quotient by exact pairs and a residual's step rather than Baudin and Smith's robust division, whose correction step keeps an error of its own. Each part of either is within a step of the correctly rounded one; at every reference point, double's and long double's at their full width among them, each is the correctly rounded one (section 4), and a float or double part within half an ulp of the exact value and 2^-8 more, as rounding once gives it, which t_cplx.c measures. Without the residual's step - the quotient over n's high part corrected by its low part, as the first version of this chunk's was - a double part is 1.3745 ulps from the exact value at a reference point, and a long double part two steps off at a point of long double's full width.

  • Alternative: S268's as it reads, fma where the target has it and Dekker's split where it has not, and Baudin and Smith's division: other bits on other targets, and no constant expression with fma.

Review. Taken: for, the same bits in every build that evaluates in the type and in a constant expression, each part within a step of the correctly rounded one and measured correctly rounded at every reference point, and Annex G's infinities; against, a product of several times the operations fma's takes, a quotient of more still, and no fast form for a renderer's inner loops until one asks for it (S266). Recommended: as taken, a _fast product by fma when a use asks. Taken.

S282. Of C99's 22 functions, real, imag, conj and proj, exact, come once, pure, with the arithmetic (S281); each of the other 18 comes twice, as every real function of the C core. The _c form is one kernel of pure arithmetic in long double, on the core's _c real functions, rounded once to the family: a float or double part is then within a step of the correctly rounded one, as long double carries 11 bits past double's. The three families share each kernel, which a new qualifier of math_common.h, D_MATH_FN_KERNEL, compiles once in each translation unit: constexpr from C++14, as D_MATH_FN is, but never forced inline. The plain form is the same algorithms in the family's own type, on the C library's real functions, fast at run time, its bound wider by the library's errors and its own roundings (the header gives both forms' bounds). Forced inline into every caller, as the core's other kernels are, the shared kernels would be compiled into each: a translation unit calling each of the fifteen unary double _c functions once, exp to atanh, compiles in 52.0 s to 580,801 bytes of text, where shared it takes 3.4 s and 100,564 bytes, and one calling pow_c alone in 8.8 s, where shared it takes 4.5 (section 4). Where long double is double, as with MSVC, the _c forms keep their kernel's error, as the real ones do (S8).

  • Alternative: a kernel per family in its own type: three times the text, and a float or double _c part with its kernel's error, not one rounding.
  • Alternative: the shared kernels D_MATH_FN, forced inline as the rest of the core's are: the compile time and the size above.

Review. Taken: for, one algorithm for each function, rounded once for float and double, compiled once in a unit; against, long double arithmetic in a float's _c form, slower than float's own, and where long double is double the _c forms' bounds are the kernel's. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S283. The plain pow's general case, w not whole and z neither real nor imaginary, is the _c kernel's, rounded to the family: w log z formed as long double pairs, its exponential taken of the pair, the angle by a sum. The plain form is fast where pow is exact by other means: z^0, a whole w by repeated squaring, a real or an imaginary z by turns of its argument, and 0^w. S266 makes each operation as accurate as the best algorithm for it, and a plain form fast beside the _c one but no less right. C99's exp(w log z), in the family's own type, carries |w log z| times log's error into the angle: at t_cplx's 198 points of pow it puts 123 double results past the plain bound, 12 of them by more than a million steps, the farthest right to 6 digits; float's and long double's alike (section 4). No C library function forms w log z closer.

  • Alternative: C99's formula, as glibc's cpow computes: fast, and past the bound at most of the points.

Review. Taken: for, the plain pow as accurate as the _c one; against, its general case at the _c kernel's speed. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S284. The values at the cuts, the zeros, the infinities and the NaNs are Annex G's (G.6), as C17 corrects them by its DR 471: cacosh(±0 + i NaN) is NaN + iπ/2, the sign C17 leaves open taken +, and ctanh(±0 + i∞) and ctanh(±0 + i NaN) are ±0 + i NaN, where C11 gave NaN + i NaN for each. Where Annex G specifies a zero's sign, every form gives it, a modulus being |x| + 0, so that a -0 never passes for +0: csqrt's real part +0 on the cut, cacos's and cacosh's real parts +0, ccosh(±∞ ± 0i)'s imaginary part a zero, catanh(x ± i∞)'s real part a zero of x's sign. Where it leaves a sign open, the sign is the algorithm's. No floating-point exception is promised, as a constant expression cannot raise one; the plain forms raise what their arithmetic and the C library raise. C17's three values keep the identities Annex G's others keep, cacosh(z) = ±i cacos(z) and ctanh(iy) = i tan(y), which C11's broke.

  • Alternative: Annex G as C11 prints it, the three values NaN + i NaN.

Review. Taken: for, C17's values, every sign Annex G gives given; against, no floating-point exception, which Annex G specifies, for a program that tests the flags. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S285. Where C has _Complex (no __STDC_NO_COMPLEX__), cplx.h converts each family's struct to and from it, d_cplx_<S>_to_complex and d_cplx_<S>_from_complex, by a copy of the two parts, without including <complex.h>; nothing in the header needs them. C++ has none: its std::complex<T> is math::complex<T>'s to convert (S270, chunk 39).

  • Alternative: no conversion, a C program copying the parts itself; or <complex.h> included, which defines I and complex as macros.

Review. Taken: for, a C program passes values to and from the standard type, and the header needs none of <complex.h>'s macros; against, none in C++ until math::complex<T> (chunk 39). Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S286. The C core gains log1p and expm1, in exponential.h, and asinh, acosh and atanh, in hyperbolic.h, each in the three families and in both forms, as its other real functions: the _c form one long double kernel carrying its reduced argument and its sums as exact pairs, rounded once to the family, a constant expression from C++14; the plain form the C library's, and below C++11, which has none of the five, the _c form; with C++'s one names. Annex F's values at the ends: log1p(-1) is -inf and below it NaN, expm1(-inf) is -1, acosh below 1 NaN, atanh(±1) ±inf. expm1's kernel takes the series of e^r - 1 with its first three terms exact, r, r^2/2 and r^3/6 as pairs, and past ln 2 / 2 x = k ln 2 + r, so that e^x - 1 = (2^k - 1) + 2^k (e^r - 1), two terms summed exactly; the exponential's own core takes the same series, 1 + (e^r - 1). S203's review recommended the five for this step, as the complex functions need them (S267). At 20,000 arguments each, the long double _c forms of the five and of exp are within 0.5183 of an ulp of the exact values, where glibc's long double functions reach 0.9417 for log1p, 1.0367 for exp and from 2.2983 to 2.4921 for the others (section 4). The first kernels of this chunk took e^r with its series' rounding in the whole tail, and expm1 as 2^k e^r - 1, which loses e^r's leading bits near e^x = 1: expm1 was 0.8737 of an ulp off and exp 0.5596 (section 3).

  • Alternative: the complex functions on the C library's real five, as the plain forms are: no constant expression, and the library's errors in the _c forms.

Review. Taken: for, the five functions in the C core in every family and both forms, the complex functions' _c forms on them, and exp's and expm1's long double kernels within half an ulp but where the exact value lies near a midpoint, closer than the C library's; against, a C++ program below C++11 gets the _c form's speed from the plain one, and exp's _c results move where its old kernel rounded wrongly, each to the correctly rounded value. Recommended: as taken; the five in the table in step 23 (S203). Taken.

S287. Where a target evaluates float and double wider than their types, as a 32-bit x87 does (math_common.h's D_MATH_FLT_EVAL_WIDE and D_MATH_DBL_EVAL_WIDE), cplx.h's float and double families take their exact steps in long double, which the target rounds in its own type: a product's and a quotient's parts, and the plain kernels' |z|^2 - 1 and 1 - x^2 - y^2, as long double pairs, each result rounded once to the family. Elsewhere they take them in their own types. Dekker's split and the two-sum need each operation rounded to the family's type, which pure arithmetic cannot force where the target evaluates it wider: a value keeps the register's bits or loses them as the compiler spills it, and a cast rounds only in an ISO mode. The first version of this chunk's arithmetic, in the families' own types everywhere, was hundreds of steps off on the x87 at points of double's full width where the parts cancel (section 3).

  • Alternative: one operation in each statement, on the x87 as elsewhere: exact in an ISO mode, where an assignment rounds, but each rounding twice, to 64 bits and then to 53, and in a GNU mode, where nothing need round, no exactness at all.

Review. Taken: for, the arithmetic and the plain kernels' exact steps exact on the x87 in every mode, ISO and GNU, C and C++; against, long double operations in a float or double product there, and an x87's last bit of a product or a quotient that can differ from SSE's where the two round a hair apart, each within its bound. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S288. cplx.h's harness, t_cplx.c, checks every function against references made by mpmath, kept in the tree as generated headers, math_tests_cplx_refs.h, and for the five real functions math_tests_real_refs.h, both made by the record's refs_c37.py, whose --check keeps them in step. Each value is computed at 400 bits and again at doubled precision until two agree, as mpmath's error is in places absolute and a part far below 1 needs more bits; each part is compared with the family's correctly rounded one, in steps, against a bound for each function, family and form, the header's. The points: exact in float, shared by the families; each family's own at the ends of its range; every other shared point widened to double's and to long double's full width, as a point exact in float makes every product of two parts exact in double and leaves the exact steps' low parts at 0; and for log, at each of those widths, |z| just below 1 with both parts just below 1/√2, which a widened point may pass. A product's reference is its exact value, whole, so that a tie reads as a tie, and the product's and the quotient's float and double parts are measured in ulps of the exact value, too, against half an ulp and 2^-8 more (S281). Beside them, Annex G's values in a table, each checked with the symmetries G.6 gives it, a sign it leaves open accepted either way; G.6's definitions of casin, catan, ccos, csin and ctan by their hyperbolic kin on a grid of special and ordinary parts; exact values; the paths no reference reaches, so that every line of cplx.h runs; in C _Complex's conversions, in C++ the one names, and from C++14 each _c form a constant expression with the run time's bits. The five real functions' long double _c forms, and exp's, whose core takes expm1's series, are held to 0.53 of an ulp of the exact value at points of long double's full width, expm1's among them where its reduction's k is 1 or 2. The plain forms are held to their bounds in every build, not to one bit pattern: on the x87 a plain form's values keep or lose their extra bits by where the compiler inlines it, as S39 finds of the library's results. Seven harnesses' d_tests_same_ld compare zeros' signs as booleans, as glibc's signbit of a long double gives 1 for a folded constant and 512 at run time. The full-width points found what the float points could not: the x87's inexact products (S287), the quotient's two steps (S281), and log |z| wrong near |z| = 1 where both parts lie near 1/√2 (section 2); and mpmath's atanh at a fixed 400 bits loses a real part below 2^-400, which settling finds. The fault trial found what the points could not: a double quotient without its residual's step within its step at every point, which the half-ulp measure finds; the plain double log with b^2 - 1 one value right at every point, as no widened point of double's width had both parts below 1/√2 where b^2 - 1 is not one value; expm1's kernel without r's low part within its bound at every point, as few lay where k is 1 or 2; and exp's without its series' low part, which no point of long double's full width measured.

  • Alternative: references from the C library, or from another kernel of the lane's: neither independent of what it checks; points exact in float alone, which never round a product in double or long double.

Review. Taken: for, references independent of every kernel and of the C library, kept in the tree and made again by the record; points at every family's full width; every line of cplx.h run; the real kernels held to a fraction of an ulp; against, the plain forms' bits not checked across inlining contexts on the x87, as S39 leaves them, and a header of references grown to some 28,000 lines. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S289.   • Alternative: the full tier of the three harnesses alone: math_common.h changed, and every test includes it.

Review. Taken: as S264, with the full tier of every math test, as the change reaches every one, the x87's paths among the faults and the lines run, and the measurements the decisions cite. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S291 to S311: chunk 38, 2026.10.30

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk38_2026.10.30/README.md.

S291 to S311, chunk 38's, as decisions.html has them; each is reviewed there, S301, S306 and S311 flagged, all minor, and the 18 outstanding decisions of S1 to S311 come first, by group and urgency, as chunk 37's made them (section 2.6). The text below is decisions_c38.md, which decisions_c38.py reads.

S291. Step 22's second part is the C core's linear algebra of the complex type and the general eigenproblem: the real path in decomp.h beside its other factorizations, and complex matrices in a header of their own, c/math/cmat.h, its functions d_cmat_<S>_<fn> and, where a root is taken, d_cmat_<S>_<fn>_c, as decomp.h's are; C++'s one names for every family, d_cmat_<fn>, as cplx.h has them. cmat.h includes decomp.h for the real helpers it shares - the scaled norms, hypot, the scaling by powers of two - and cplx.h for the arithmetic, so that a program wanting real matrices alone includes neither complex header's kernels.

  • Alternative: the complex functions in decomp.h, d_decomp_<S>_c...: one header, but every includer of the real factorizations compiling the complex ones too.
  • Alternative: the complex functions in cplx.h: the scalar header grown by matrices, against the core's one subject to a header.

Review. Taken: for, a program of real matrices compiles no complex kernel, the complex header the real helpers once, one subject to a header; against, a third header for the eigenproblem's complex half. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S292. A general real matrix's eigenvalues and eigenvectors are LAPACK's dgeev path, each part a public function in the three families and both forms: d_decomp_<S>_balance (dgebal, job 'B') and _balance_back (dgebak); _hessenberg_q, Householder reflectors with Q accumulated (dgehd2, dorghr); _schur, Francis's implicit double-shift QR (dlahqr: Ahues and Kressner's deflation, exceptional shifts at every tenth step without a split, dlanv2's standard blocks of two); _schur_vectors, back-substitution through the quasi-triangular form with dlaln2's small solves and overflow guards (dtrevc, job 'B'); and the driver, d_decomp_<S>_eigen(a, n, values, vectors, work, perm, max_iter, tol), its eigenvalues struct d_cplx_<S> and its eigenvectors complex, column j the vector of value j. Each part is the algorithm LAPACK's reference implementation takes, so that a reader can check it line by line against a standard, and each is reachable alone: a caller who wants the Schur form, or no balancing, composes the parts. The values and vectors are the C core's complex numbers (S267), as the owner ruled.

  • Alternative: LAPACK's storage, wr and wi arrays and real vectors with a pair in two columns, as dgeev returns them: no complex type needed, every caller unpacking pairs.
  • Alternative: the driver alone, the parts internal: a smaller API, no Schur form for a caller who wants one.

Review. Taken: for, LAPACK's reference algorithms, each checkable against it and each public, the values and vectors the C core's complex numbers, as ruled; against, a complex result for a real matrix every caller takes, and n (n + 4) entries of scratch. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S293. The driver's eigenvalues are sorted ascending by real part, then imaginary: a complex pair exactly conjugate, side by side, the negative imaginary part first where no real eigenvalue shares the pair's real part, a real eigenvalue's imaginary part exactly +0. Each eigenvector is of unit 2-norm, its entry of largest modulus (the first among equals, by the rounded squares) real and positive; a real eigenvalue's vector real, its imaginary parts +0; the second of a pair the first's conjugate, bit for bit. One order and one normalization make the answer a function of the matrix alone, what the language's eig and the C++ face's general_eigen need to compare and to print, where LAPACK's order is its Schur form's, which a change of algorithm or of scaling may move.

  • Alternative: LAPACK's order and normalization, unit vectors with the largest component real (dgeev), as NumPy and MATLAB give them: the standard's own answer, its order the iteration's.
  • Alternative: vectors scaled to a largest entry of 1, as dtrevc leaves them: no root, a vector not of unit length.

Review. Taken: for, one answer for a matrix, whatever the iteration's order or the matrix's scale, a pair's vectors conjugate bit for bit; against, LAPACK's order lost (a caller wanting the Schur form's calls the parts), and a pair whose real part a real eigenvalue shares sorted around it. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S294. The driver always balances, as dgeev with 'B' does; the parts are public, so that a path without balancing is the parts composed without it. Balancing, a diagonal similarity by powers of two with the permutations that isolate eigenvalues, is exact and makes a badly scaled matrix's eigenvalues as accurate as its condition allows; on a few matrices whose small entries are not noise it can worsen an eigenvector's residual measured against the matrix as given, which is why MATLAB offers eig(A, 'nobalance'). The harnesses measure each vector's residual against the balanced matrix, of whose norm the backward error is a few epsilons, as LAPACK's is.

  • Alternative: a flag to the driver, MATLAB's 'nobalance': a parameter every caller passes for a case few meet.
  • Alternative: no balancing, as before: a badly scaled matrix's small eigenvalues lost to its large entries' rounding.

Review. Taken: for, a badly scaled matrix's eigenvalues as accurate as its condition allows, by an exact similarity, and the parts for a caller who wants none; against, the rare matrix whose small entries are not noise, where balancing worsens a vector's residual against the matrix as given, has no flag in the driver. Recommended: as taken, with a 'nobalance' option in the language when a step asks. Taken.

S295. Before balancing, the driver scales the matrix by a power of two to a largest magnitude in [1, 2), exactly, and its eigenvalues back, so that 2^k A's eigenvalues are 2^k times A's and its eigenvectors A's, bit for bit, barring overflow and underflow (S48's property, kept on the new path); a NaN or an infinity gives every eigenvalue and every vector's entry a NaN and returns false; the zero matrix has zero eigenvalues and the identity's columns. _max_iter bounds the double-shift steps in all; if it is spent, the eigenvalues found are given, the rest the diagonal's entries, every vector's entry a NaN, and the driver returns false. _tol is the deflation's tolerance, the family's epsilon where smaller. dgeev scales only a matrix whose norm lies outside its safe range; scaling every matrix costs n^2 exact products and keeps the 2^k property for all.

  • Alternative: dgeev's scaling, only past the safe range: the property only in range.
  • Alternative: LAPACK's budget, 30 steps per eigenvalue: no budget the caller sets, unlike d_decomp_<S>_eigenvalues of chunk 13.

Review. Taken: for, S48's 2^k property on the new path, the zero matrix and a non-finite matrix answered, a budget the caller sets; against, n^2 exact products per call. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S296. d_decomp_<S>_eigenvalues of chunk 13 is the driver's path, its eigenvalues the driver's real parts, bit for bit, false where one is complex, as before; and the C++ face's general_eigen takes the driver's eigenvalues and eigenvectors, its vectors now from the Schur form (S49) rather than inverse iteration. d_decomp_<S>_hessenberg and _eigenvector, the old parts, stay as they were for their callers. One path for every caller, and S45's and S49's flags closed where the face reaches them. A face vector's sign can differ from inverse iteration's: the largest entry is positive in both, but a vector whose two largest entries are equal in magnitude may take the other.

  • Alternative: the old path kept beside the new one: two answers for one matrix.

Review. Taken: for, one path, the old function's bits the driver's real parts, the face's vectors independent for a repeated eigenvalue; against, a face vector whose two largest entries tie in magnitude can change sign from chunk 37's. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S297. A complex matrix's LU, determinant, inverse and solution are zgetrf's and zgesv's: each column's pivot its entry of largest |re| + |im| at or below the diagonal, the first among equals, as izamax chooses it; every product and quotient cplx.h's, correctly rounded or within a step; d_cmat_<S>_determinant the parity times U's diagonal, _inverse Gauss-Jordan as mat.h's d_mat_<S>_inverse, and _solve the elimination of [A | B] with back-substitution, each column the bits of _lu and _lu_solve. Needing no root, each comes once, a constant expression from C++14. A singular matrix is one with a pivot exactly 0, as zgesv reports it: its determinant 0, its inverse and solution the zero matrix, false.

  • Alternative: the pivot of largest modulus: a root or a comparison of squares per entry, and not LAPACK's choice.

Review. Taken: for, LAPACK's pivot, no root, constant expressions, a solution of LU's bits; against, a nearly singular matrix's inverse of huge entries with no warning, as zgesv's, until a condition estimate (S257's cond). Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S298. A Hermitian matrix's eigenvalues and eigenvectors are found by the cyclic Jacobi method with complex rotations, d_cmat_<S>_hermitian_eigen and its _c form, mirroring decomp.h's d_decomp_<S>_sym_eigen: each off-diagonal pair's phase u = a_pq / |a_pq| taken out by the unitary diag(1, conj(u)), then the real rotation of the pair; the values ascending, the vectors a unitary matrix's columns. A real symmetric matrix gets sym_eigen's values bit for bit. Jacobi's method is accurate to each eigenvalue's own scale on graded matrices, where tridiagonal QR is accurate to the norm's, and is the method the real path already takes.

  • Alternative: Householder tridiagonalization and implicit QL (zheev): faster for large orders, LAPACK's own, accurate to the norm.

Review. Taken: for, each eigenvalue accurate to its own scale, the real path's values for a real symmetric matrix, constant expressions; against, Jacobi's sweeps take several times tridiagonal QR's operations at large orders. Recommended: as taken, with a tridiagonal path when a step asks for large Hermitian matrices. Taken.

S299. A general complex matrix's eigenvalues and eigenvectors are zgeev's path, as S292's are dgeev's: d_cmat_<S>_balance and _balance_back (zgebal, zgebak), _hessenberg_q (zgehd2, each reflector's beta real), _schur, single-shift complex QR (zlahqr: the subdiagonal made real first and kept real, Wilkinson's shift, the root nearer the corner, by cplx.h's square root, exceptional shifts), _schur_vectors, back-substitution with each divisor no smaller than ulp times |t_kk| and x scaled down where the next step could overflow, and the driver d_cmat_<S>_eigen, with S293's order, S294's balancing and S295's scaling and budget. A real matrix keeps the real path, whose pairs are exactly conjugate; the complex path is for complex matrices.

  • Alternative: ztrevc's triangular solves by zlatrs, scaled column by column: more robust to growth than the per-entry guard, at more code.
  • Alternative: every real matrix through the complex path: one path, its pairs conjugate only to rounding.

Review. Taken: for, zgeev's path, a real matrix's pairs exactly conjugate on its own path; against, the back-substitution's per-entry guard rather than zlatrs's scaling, and a complex eigenproblem of order 3 as a constant expression takes clang past its default million steps of constant evaluation (-fconstexpr-steps raises it; the harness's is of order 2). Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S300. The eigenvalue paths' kernels are compiled once in a translation unit and never forced inline: D_MATH_FN_KERNEL where a kernel is a constant expression from C++14, and a new qualifier, D_MATH_FN_KERNEL_RT, for a kernel that takes the C library's root and is not (inline in C++, static with the compiler's inline hint in C). In cmat.h, cplx.h's product and quotient are called through two such kernels, d_internal_cmat_<S>_mul and _div, where the matrix kernels would otherwise each take a copy of them at every call. Forced inline, every call would compile its own copy of each kernel it reaches, and the complex kernels each a copy of cplx.h's exact products at every use.

  • Alternative: D_MATH_FN, forced inline, as the scalar functions are: the call's cost saved, the compile's multiplied.

Review. Taken: for, each kernel compiled once in a unit, the complex products once; against, a call where a forced inline would have saved it, and a fifth qualifier. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S301. The C core's norms are scaled, as S256 recommended: d_vec_<S>_norm and its _c form, _distance, _normalize and _cos_angle take the root of the sum of squares where it lies in [sqrt(MIN / EPS), MAX], where no square overflowed or lost its precision to underflow, and elsewhere scale the vector by a power of two to a largest magnitude in [1, 2), take the norm, and scale it back. In range every bit is as it was; at the ends the norm is the one the type holds, exactly where it is exact. The language's wrapper is gone: linear_algebra's norms, unit vectors and angles, and the language's, are the C core's. A power of two commutes with rounding, so the scaled path's sum is the unscaled one's, bit for bit, wherever both are finite.

  • Alternative: always scaled, as dnrm2's running sum or Blue's algorithm: slower in range, its bits other than the plain sum's.
  • Alternative: hypot chains: a root per entry.

Review. Taken: for, S256's recommendation where it serves every face, every bit in range unchanged, the norms at the ends exact where they can be; against, project and reject still divide by an unscaled squared norm, an infinity or 0 at the range's ends. Recommended: as taken, with project and reject scaled the same way at the next step that touches vec.h. Ruled 2026.11.04: as recommended, at the next step that touches vec.h. Done in chunk 50 (S409): project and reject scaled at the range's ends, every bit in range kept.

S302. mat.h's d_mat_<S>_solve(x, scratch, a, b, n, m) is Gaussian elimination of [A | B] with partial pivoting and back-substitution, as dgesv: each column of B the bits of decomp.h's d_decomp_<S>_lu and _lu_solve, false and the zero matrix for a singular A. The generic kernels' solve is the same elimination in the number type's own arithmetic, and linear_algebra's solve(A, b) and solve(A, B) take the kernels' for every number type but an integer type - the C families' LU's bits, an exact type's exact solution - where they took LU and required a floating-point type. A singular A gives the zero solution, as inverse gives the zero matrix. S257's recommendation: an exact solve no longer inv(A) B, at about a third of the products, and the C++ face's solve open to values, rationals and quaternions. For the C families, a singular matrix's solution was LU's quotients by 0, infinities and NaNs; it is now zero, as the inverse's was.

  • Alternative: Gauss-Jordan of [A | B], the inverse's elimination: the same exact answer, half again the products, and other bits than LU's.
  • Alternative: Bareiss's fraction-free elimination for exact types: no quotient until the end, larger intermediate numbers.

Review. Taken: for, S257's elimination in every family, an exact solve at about a third of the inverse's products, the face's solve open to every number type, a dense one LU's bits; against, a C family's singular solve now zeros, not LU's infinities and NaNs, a change for a caller who tested for those. Recommended: as taken; cond when a step asks (S257). Taken.

S303. number_traits is written again (S157, S158): the field's traits, is_exact, is_integer, is_field, is_commutative, zero() and one(); the order's, is_ordered and abs; magnitude(x) of magnitude_type, what generic code pivots by and scales a tolerance by, |x| for an ordered type and, for one without an order, a nonnegative number of an ordered type; epsilon(), the type's precision, and epsilon(x), the precision x carries, 0 for an exact value; tolerance(), four epsilons, the relative tolerance of a comparison given none, 0 for an exact type; and tolerance(a, b), that times the larger magnitude with the larger epsilon of the two, 0 where both are exact. An integer type's root is exact, rounded down. The value classes' epsilon is their kind's, 0 for an exact value, their tolerance for a pair 0 where both are exact. The tolerance relative and of the type, as S46 chose for the eigenvalues; the traits ready for complex numbers and quaternions, whose magnitude is a modulus and whose products do not commute.

  • Alternative: Eigen's NumTraits, dummy_precision() 1e-12 for double and isApprox relative to the smaller norm: a looser default, one per type.
  • Alternative: Julia's isapprox, rtol = sqrt(eps): eight digits of a double's sixteen.

Review. Taken: for, S157's and S158's recommendations, the traits ready for complex numbers and quaternions, a tolerance of the type's own precision relative to scale; against, four epsilons is strict for the results of long computations compared with no tolerance, where a caller must give one. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S304. The C++ face's comparisons given no tolerance - equals, approx_equal, is_symmetric, is_identity, is_unit, is_orthogonal - take internal::linalg_tolerance: the type's tolerance(x, y) of x, the entry of largest magnitude among both operands' (1 among them where the comparison is with the identity or a unit's norm), and y, the entry that carries the largest epsilon; a comparison given a tolerance takes it as an absolute one, as before. Relative to the operands' scale by default, four epsilons of a floating-point type, 0 where every entry is exact, and a type's author's choice where its traits make one; an explicit tolerance keeps its meaning for every caller who passed one.

  • Alternative: an explicit tolerance relative too: one meaning, every caller's explicit tolerance changed.

Review. Taken: for, a default relative to the operands, exact where they are, a type's own choice honored, every explicit tolerance unchanged; against, two meanings, relative by default and absolute when given. Recommended: as taken, a relative form by name when a caller asks. Taken.

S305. The generic kernels keep each product in its order - a row operation a product on the left, a quotient on the left as 1 / d times x and on the right as x times 1 / d where products do not commute - and pivot by magnitude, so that a quaternion matrix's inverse and solution are right, and an inexact type's elimination pivots by size. The face's tests plant a quaternion type of exact rationals: A A^-1 and A^-1 A the identity, A X = B, exactly; with either quotient's side wrong, each misses.

  • Alternative: x / d everywhere, as before: right only where products commute.

Review. Taken: for, quaternion matrices solved and inverted exactly, an inexact type pivoted by size; against, a quotient where products do not commute is two operations, 1 / d and a product. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S306. The Jacobi rotations' tangent - d_decomp_<S>_sym_eigen's, the one-sided Jacobi SVD's and d_cmat_<S>_hermitian_eigen's - is 1 / (2 theta), the off-diagonal entry over the diagonal's difference, where theta passes 2^(EMAX / 2) and theta^2 would overflow; below, as before, bit for bit. This chunk's constant expressions found it: at a threshold of 0 a pair all but annihilated gives theta past the range's half, theta^2 overflows, at run time to t = 0, no rotation, and in a constant expression to an error; the root equals 1 / (2 theta) within a part in 2^EMAX there.

  • Alternative: the tangent by hypot of the difference and twice the entry: no overflow at all, other bits everywhere.
  • Alternative: the matrix scaled by a power of two first, as the driver is: no entry past MAX / 2, its difference and twice its entries finite, but theta's ratio unchanged.

Review. Taken: for, constant expressions at a threshold of 0, the rotation made where it was skipped, every other bit as before; against, the Jacobi functions take the matrix unscaled, so that an entry past MAX / 2 overflows twice itself, or two of opposite signs their difference, an error in a constant expression. Recommended: as taken, with the matrix scaled by a power of two first, as the driver is, at the next step that touches the Jacobi functions, the bits in range unchanged. Ruled 2026.11.04: as recommended, at the next step that touches the Jacobi functions. Flag: minor: no scaling before the Jacobi sweeps.

S307. t_decomp.c and the new t_cmat.c check the paths against references made by mpmath at 80 digits, math_tests_eig_refs.h and math_tests_cmat_refs.h (tools/refs_c38.py): every matrix exact in float, so that each family takes it exactly; each eigenvalue within 32 epsilons of the family times |A|_F times its condition number; each eigenvector's residual within 32 epsilons of the balanced matrix's norm; the Hessenberg and Schur forms' similarities and their Q and Z's orthogonality within 8 n epsilons; a system's solution, inverse and determinant within 2 n epsilons times its condition number of the exact ones. t_vec.c checks the norms at the range's ends, t_mat.c the solution written out bit for bit, and t_number_traits_face.cpp the traits, the quaternions and the generic pivot. The bounds are a few times the largest measured in every build (section 4).

  • Alternative: residuals alone, with no references: a backward-stable method's property, not its answer's accuracy.

Review. Taken: for, every eigenvalue against an independent reference to its condition, every family on the same exact matrix, the parts checked alone and together, bounds a few times the measured; against, references for orders up to 12, and two headers of references. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S308. The C++ face's complex type, math::complex<T>, and with it the language's complex eigenvalues (S258) and the C++ face's general eigen of a matrix with a complex pair, come in chunk 39 as S279 planned; this chunk's paths give them every value they need. The face's general eigen gives a real matrix's real eigenvalues as before, a pair still flagged there until the face has a complex type to give it in.

  • Alternative: the face's pairs as two rows of reals now: a shape withdrawn by S258's review.

Review. Taken: as S279 planned; against, until chunk 39 the face flags a pair as before. Recommended: as taken. Taken; chunk 39 gives every pair as complex numbers (S317).

S309. In decomp.h's section of the general eigenproblem and cmat.h's sections of Hermitian and general matrices, gcc's -Warray-bounds is set aside, #pragma GCC diagnostic push and ignored, and restored after each section by pop; clang and other compilers are untouched. gcc 13 at -O2, compiling a caller whose matrix is a constant it can fold - a 2-by-2 rotation in main, the record's example - takes the kernels' loops for n of 3 and more for reachable and warns of indices past the array: eight false warnings, each an error under -Werror. Every index in these sections lies within the caller's n by n, as the harnesses and the sanitizers check.

  • Alternative: the warnings left, a caller adding -Wno-array-bounds: every caller with -Werror stopped by the library's headers.
  • Alternative: the kernels written so that gcc's value ranges can bound each index: a rewrite of LAPACK's loops for one compiler's analysis.

Review. Taken: for, a caller's -Werror build of gcc at -O2 not stopped by false warnings from the library's headers; against, a caller who passes an array too small for its n loses gcc's warning inside these sections, where the sanitizers would still find it. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S310. The checks, as S289's: the generator's --check and the references' --check; the full tier of every math test, as the change reaches every harness through decomp.h and the face's traits; the smoke tier against chunk 37's lines; the fault trial; the sanitizers, the lines run, the ladders, the lints and the levels, as chunk 37's record ran them.

  • Alternative: the full tier of the four harnesses and the faces the change names: fewer builds, a header every test includes left unchecked elsewhere.

Review. Taken: as S289, with the full tier of every math test, as the change reaches every one, and the faults of the new paths. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S311. The balancing scales the matrix in place, as dgebal and zgebal do: each step divides a row and multiplies a column by a power of two, so that the balanced matrix is D^-1 P^T A P D exactly unless an entry passes below the least normal number on the way and loses bits, as LAPACK lets it; decomp.h's comment on the balancing says so, and the harnesses check it exactly where no entry does and, across the range, to each entry's own magnitude. The guards that keep the factors within [MIN / EPS, EPS / MIN] keep each shrunk row's or column's largest entry above 2 MIN / EPS, so that an entry passing below the least normal is then below half an epsilon of that largest; later steps can raise it, its row's largest at the end, with few of its bits. The harnesses' guards found it, balancing matrices of entries from 2^-(EMAX-4) to 2^(EMAX-4), in float and long double; LAPACK's balancing, and MATLAB's and NumPy's through it, have the same property.

  • Alternative: the factors found as now, each norm taken of the entries times the factors' ratios rather than of the entries scaled, and applied once at the end, each entry a_ij d_j / d_i rounded once: D^-1 P^T A P D exactly wherever it is representable, and at every matrix where no entry passes below the least normal the same factors and bits as now; a norm of products, and an exact scaling by 2^k, which the C core lacks, in both headers.
  • Alternative: the factors bounded so that no entry can leave the normal range: matrices of a wide range balanced less, their eigenvalues less accurate.

Review. Taken: for, LAPACK's algorithm line by line, as S292 chose, and the eigenvalues' accuracy against |A| unchanged; against, at matrices whose entries span most of the range, an entry of the balanced matrix can keep few of its bits and be its row's largest, so that the balanced matrix is not the exact similarity the earlier text claimed. Recommended: the first alternative, the factors applied once at the end, at the next step that touches the balancing: exact wherever representable, the same bits wherever no entry underflows. Ruled 2026.11.04: the first alternative, the factors applied once at the end, as recommended. Flag: minor: the balancing exact only where no entry passes below the least normal.

S312 to S323: chunk 39, 2026.10.31

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk39_2026.10.31/README.md.

S312 to S323, chunk 39's, as decisions.html has them; each is reviewed there, S322 flagged, minor; S308's review notes its close; and the 19 outstanding decisions of S1 to S323 come first, by group and urgency, as chunk 38's made them (section 2.6). The text below is decisions_c39.md, which decisions_c39.py reads.

S312. Step 22's third part is the C++ face of the C core's complex numbers and what it completes, as S279 planned: math::complex<T> in a header of its own, math/complex.hpp, with its number_traits; vectors and matrices of it; general_eigen's eigenvalues and eigenvectors as complex numbers (S308); and in the language im, jm and km answering not_yet (S271, S275), the stubs' new step numbers (S265) and the empty matrix's guard (chunk 36). complex.hpp stands beside value.hpp and quaternion.hpp and includes cplx.h alone of the C core; linear algebra names the type by a declaration, so that a program of real vectors compiles no complex code.

  • Alternative: the type in value.hpp or number_traits.hpp: every includer of the value or the traits compiling cplx.h's 11,042 lines.

Review. Taken: for, step 22's last part as S279 planned, and a header of its own, so that real code compiles no complex code; against, a third delivery for one step. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S313. Every operation of math::complex<T> is cplx.h's: the arithmetic, which comes once; real, imag, conj and proj, exact; and abs, arg and C99's 16 other functions in their _c forms, at every level and in every build, never the plain forms, so that a result has the same bits on every compiler and target, in a constant expression or not (G6). Each operation is a friend defined in the class, found by argument-dependent lookup alone, as math::value's operators are; pow with a real operand is pow of it plus 0i, as C's <tgmath.h> takes it; and there is no _fast form until a use asks for one (S266). The real number_root takes the C library's root below C++14 because the two are the same bits, both correctly rounded; the complex plain forms are not: their bounds in cplx.h's banner run to 6 steps where the _c forms' run from 1 to 4, and the C library's real functions beneath them are each library's. The test finds, at its 2,000 points of each family, points where the plain forms give other bits, and checks the face against the _c form at every one.

  • Alternative: the plain forms at run time and the _c forms in constant evaluation, as std::is_constant_evaluated allows from C++20: faster at run time, and two answers for one call.
  • Alternative: function templates at namespace scope, found by math::exp(z) too: a qualified call as std::exp(z) is written, and every unqualified call in djinterp::math meeting templates that match any type.

Review. Taken: for, one answer (G6) on every compiler, target and level, in a constant expression or not, which std::complex cannot give; the operations found as math::value's are; against, the _c forms' long double kernels are slower than the plain forms on a fast C library, and a qualified math::exp(z) does not compile, a caller writing exp(z). The alternatives give two answers, or templates that every unqualified call in the namespace meets. Recommended: as taken, a _fast form where a use measures the need (S266). Taken.

S314. To number_traits, math::complex<T> is a commutative field, inexact and unordered: its magnitude, a T, is |re| + |im|, LAPACK's cabs1, by which izamax and cmat.h pivot, needing no root; its epsilon T's, its tolerance four of them, and a pair's that times the larger magnitude. It has no abs of its own type, no root and no conversion through double, so that generic code that would order its numbers, take their root or pass them through double - a vector's norm, its length, an angle - is refused at compile time rather than computing a sum of squares that is no norm. It is no number, the C++20 concept, which asks for <. common_number of it and T, either way round, or of two, is the complex type at every level.

  • Alternative: the modulus as the magnitude: a root for every pivot, and pivots other than cmat.h's where the two order two entries differently, so other bits.
  • Alternative: abs the modulus and sqrt the principal root in the traits: the generic norm compiling as the root of the sum of z_i^2, a complex number, silently no norm.

Review. Taken: for, LAPACK's magnitude, so that the pivots and the bits are cmat.h's, and no root per pivot; generic code that would compute a false norm refused at compile time; against, |re| + |im| is up to sqrt 2 times the modulus, so that a tolerance it scales is a little looser than the modulus would make it. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S315. Vectors and matrices of math::complex<T> take the generic kernels, whose every operation is the type's own and so cplx.h's. Their determinant, for this type alone (linalg_by_lu), is LU with partial pivoting by magnitude, cmat.h's d_cmat_lu and d_cmat_lu_det step for step, the sign of the row swaps 1 or 0 - 1, -1 + 0i, as the product's first factor; their inverse and solution, Gauss-Jordan and the elimination of [A | B] (S302, S305), are cmat.h's step for step already. So the face's determinant, inverse and solution of a complex matrix are cmat.h's bit for bit, and constant expressions from C++14. The test checks the three against cmat.h's on random matrices of 1 to 5 rows, singular and tied ones among them, in every build. Every other type the generic path takes keeps Bareiss's determinant, exact where the type is.

  • Alternative: a kernel family of its own, forwarding to cmat.h through the arrays' pointers reinterpreted: one source of the algorithm, but pointer arithmetic over the members of complex<T>, which the language does not define, no constant expression before C++20's transient allocations, and cmat.h's 14,117 lines compiled by every includer of linalg_common.hpp.
  • Alternative: Bareiss's determinant for complex matrices too: a row swapped in only at an exact zero and each step divided by the previous pivot, unstable in floating point, and other bits than the C core's.

Review. Taken: for, cmat.h's bits through the face, constant expressions from C++14, and no pointer arithmetic the language leaves undefined; against, the LU's steps written twice, in the C core and in the generic kernels, kept equal by the test's checks of every build rather than by one source. The alternatives cost a constant expression and defined behaviour, or the C core's bits and stability. Recommended: as taken; the parity checks are the guard, and the fault trial plants what would break them. Taken.

S316. A tolerance given to the faces' comparisons - matrix::equals, is_identity and is_symmetric, vector::equals and is_unit, approx_equal, is_invertible, is_singular and is_orthogonal - is of the traits' magnitude_type, as the kernels already take it, which for every type that had one is the element type itself; is_invertible asks that the determinant's magnitude pass it, |det| for a real type. The free approx_equal's tolerance is no longer deduced, so that approx_equal(a, b, 1e-6) of float vectors now compiles.

  • Alternative: the element type, as before: a complex tolerance, whose magnitude the kernels would have to take, and no build for complex elements.

Review. Taken: for, a tolerance of the type a magnitude is, so that complex and quaternion matrices compare, and approx_equal of float vectors with a double tolerance compiles; against, none for the types that had a tolerance, whose magnitude type is their own. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S317. general_eigen<T, N> gives every eigenvalue and eigenvector of a real matrix as math::complex<T> (S308): eigenvalues() a vector<complex<T>, N>, eigenvectors() a matrix<complex<T>, N, N>, and eigenvalue(i) and eigenvector(i) of them, the driver's values and vectors as decomp.h gives them, a pair's exactly conjugate, the real parts eigenvalues_qr's bit for bit. converged() is false only where not every eigenvalue was found, every vector then NaNs, as the driver gives them; is_real() says whether every eigenvalue is real. eigenvalues_qr keeps its real eigenvalues, a pair's real part twice and flagged. Where the face gave the identity's columns for a matrix whose vectors were not found, it gives the driver's NaNs, which no caller can take for eigenvectors.

  • Alternative: a second class, complex_eigen, beside the real one: two answers for one matrix, the real one's pair still flagged.
  • Alternative: real eigenvalues and a vector of their imaginary parts, as LAPACK's wr and wi: the shape S258's review withdrew.
  • Alternative: the identity's columns where no vector was found, as before: a matrix a caller who does not ask converged() takes for eigenvectors.

Review. Taken: for, every eigenvalue and vector of a real matrix in one call, as S308 planned, the driver's answer whole, NaNs that no caller can take for eigenvectors; against, a caller of the real face now takes complex values, writing .real() where it wanted a T, and a caller who tested the identity's columns of a failed iteration finds NaNs. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S318. im, jm and km are entries of the language's constant table from step 22, as S271 and S275 ask: the parser reads each as a constant, never a variable, and every fold answers not_yet for each, its message naming the step that gives it a value, "im: not yet (step 23, 3e)" and "jm: not yet (step 24, 3f)", "km" as "jm". An entry carries its message in not_yet, empty for every constant with a value, and expr_constant_of asks it first. No text binds im now and reads otherwise when step 23 gives it a value.

  • Alternative: the names unknown until their steps: im a variable now and a constant later, one text read two ways.
  • Alternative: the names as functions of the table answering not_yet: im a call, not a constant, and 3 + 4im no product.

Review. Taken: for, the names held for their kinds from now, so that no text changes its reading at steps 23 and 24, S197's rule for a name the language reserves; against, a variable named im, jm or km is lost now rather than at its step. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S319. The stubs of Phase 4 name their steps as S265 numbered them: expr_derivative and expr_gradient step 25 (4a), expr_integrate and expr_derivative_at step 26 (4b), expr_sum, expr_product, expr_limit and expr_taylor step 27 (4c), and expr_antiderivative step 28 (4d), in their messages, their comments and the banners; t_expr_face.cpp checks the new messages.

  • Alternative: the old numbers until Phase 4: messages naming steps the roadmap gives to other items.

Review. Taken: for, messages that name the roadmap's steps; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S320. t_expr_face.cpp's empty matrix asks first that [] parses, before it reads the term the parse gave, as chunk 36's ten faults that stopped the test by a signal asked (its section 5). Chunk 36's F743, [] of one empty element, planted again, fails a check (section 4).

  • Alternative: as it was: each such fault caught by a signal, with no check saying where.

Review. Taken: for, a fault that breaks [] fails a check that says where, rather than stopping the test; against, none. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S321. The type is math::complex<T>, as S270 names it, beside the lane's math::value, math::quaternion and linalg::vector and matrix, and the standard's std::complex, which it stands beside. Its parts are read as std::complex's are, real() and imag(), and set by real(x) and imag(y); c_value() lends the C core's struct, as math::value's lends the value; to_std() gives a std::complex<T>, by name at every level, as C++98 has no explicit conversion operator; and a complex<U> of another part type converts explicitly, part by part. The C++ guide names a project type D and PascalCase, DComplex, which no type of the lane's C++ faces is.

  • Alternative: DComplex<T>, as the guide asks: the guide's name, and one type of the lane's named apart from the others and from the standard's.
  • Alternative: an explicit conversion operator to std::complex<T> from C++11 and the member below it: two spellings by level.

Review. Ruled (S270): for, the name the owner gave, the lane's types' spelling and the standard's; against, the C++ guide's D and PascalCase, which no type of the lane's faces has, so that the lane's C++ types are named apart from the guide's rule. Renaming them all, DValue, DQuaternion, DMatrix and DComplex, is the owner's to ask for. Recommended: as ruled. Ruled.

S322. The face's dot of complex vectors stays the bilinear sum of a_i b_i, the matrix product's row by column and NumPy's dot; norms, lengths, angles and unit vectors of complex vectors are refused at compile time (S314); and is_orthogonal asks A^T A = I, an orthogonal complex matrix, not a unitary one. A conjugating dot, the 2-norm of a complex vector and the Frobenius norm of a complex matrix, and is_unitary, come with step 23, as the language's dot conjugates and its norm takes a complex matrix (S273).

  • Alternative: dot conjugating its first operand now, as Eigen's does and the inner product is written: the face's dot two different sums by type, and the product's row by column no longer dot.

Review. Taken: for, one dot for every element type, the product's row by column; what would be wrong refused at compile time; against, no inner product and no 2-norm of complex vectors in the C++ face until step 23, a gap a user of Eigen would notice at once. Recommended: as taken now, and at step 23 the conjugating vdot (Eigen's dot, NumPy's vdot), the 2-norm and the Frobenius norm of complex vectors and matrices, and is_unitary, with the language's (S273), in step 23's third part, chunk 43 (S324). Taken in chunk 43: vdot, the norms, frobenius_norm and is_unitary (S354).

S323. The checks: t_complex_face.cpp, new, at every level from C++98; the full tier of every face test, as the traits, the kernels and the faces' tolerances reach every one; the smoke tier against chunk 38's lines; the fault trial; the sanitizers on the four tests the change names; the lines run of the new code; the C++ ladder over the math lane; the lints; and the levels, as chunk 38's record ran them. The C core is unchanged, and its harnesses' full tier and the C ladder are not run again.

  • Alternative: the full tier of the four tests the change names: fewer builds, and a header every face includes unchecked elsewhere.
