S404 to S408: chunk 49, 2026.11.14

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk49_2026.11.14/README.md.

S404. The C core's sums of products and of terms are compensated by default: vec.h's dot, cross and sum, and mat.h's multiply, multiply_vector, trace and power (by multiply), each a sum rounded once from Ogita, Rump and Oishi's Dot2 or Sum2 - each product exact as a pair by Dekker's product, the terms summed by two-sums, the errors summed apart and added last - as accurate as the sum computed in twice the precision and rounded once. The pairs are double's where double is evaluated in its own type, for the float and double families - a float's products exact in one multiplication - and long double's otherwise and for the long double family, as quat.h's products took them (S276). Where an entry leaves the range where Dekker's products are exact, its operand is scaled by a power of two and the sum scaled back, rounded once, a subnormal result's midpoint decided by the low part; where an operand has a NaN or an infinity, the sum is the formula as written; an exact zero is +0, as the formula from +0 makes it, and a part of cross that is 0 because its two products are equal is the formula's, of the sign the arithmetic gives it. A product's entries are tested once for the range, and each entry summed with no test of its own where both operands lie in it.

  • Alternative: the formulas as they were, compensated forms under new names (dot_accurate, ...): no bits change and no cost; accuracy only where a caller knows to ask for it, against S276's choice for the quaternion's product.
  • Alternative: Kahan's or Neumaier's compensated summation of the rounded products: cheaper, but each product's own rounding stays, so a dot is no better than its products where they cancel.
  • Alternative: an exact accumulator (Kulisch's, or Rump's AccSum): every sum correctly rounded, at several times Dot2's cost and much more code.
  • Alternative: products by fused multiply-add at run time: Dot2 at a fraction of the cost where the target has one, but no constant expression and not the same bits on every target.

Review. Taken: for, every dot, sum, cross product, matrix product and trace of the C core as accurate as computed in twice the precision and rounded once - in this chunk's checks every float result and every sum and cross part correctly rounded, and every double and long double dot the rounding of a value within Dot2's bound, 86 in 100 of them correctly rounded where the formula as written rounds 14 or 15 in 100 so (raw/compensated.txt) - the same bits on every target where the family is evaluated in its own type, and a constant expression from C++14 as before. Against: the cost, measured for double at 6 times the formula's for a dot of 3 terms, 22 for 16 and 13 for 256, 11 for a 4 x 4 product and 12 for a cross product (raw/timing.txt); and every result of these kernels may change in its last bits, the tests written against the formulas moved to their _fast forms. The alternatives: the formulas kept as the defaults; Kahan's summation; an exact accumulator; fused multiply-adds. Recommended: as taken, as S276 took it for the quaternion's product: a default that is right, and a _fast form for a measured inner loop. Taken.

S405. Each kernel S404 compensates keeps its formula as written, bit for bit, under a _fast name: dot_fast, cross_fast, sum_fast, multiply_fast, multiply_vector_fast, power_fast, trace_fast and solve_fast (S406), in the C core, the kernels and both faces, fixed and dynamic. The norms keep their sums as written: norm_squared is dot_fast(a, a), as the norms S256 and S301 scaled use it, a sum of squares, which cannot cancel, within (n - 1) u of its value relatively, and every norm keeps its bits. project, reject, cos_angle and angle divide the compensated dot, the part of them that can cancel; quat.h's slerp takes its arc's sign from it. quat.h's rotate_fast and isect.h's ray tests take cross_fast and dot_fast: the first is the quaternion's fast form, as written by its contract, and the second tests within an epsilon, in a renderer's inner loop, which the compensated kernels would make several times slower for no answer it can use. The generic kernel, for exact and other number types, has one formula, and its _fast forms are it.

  • Alternative: no _fast forms: one formula each, the compensated, and a caller who needs the old speed or bits writes the loop.
  • Alternative: the norms compensated too: each norm within about half an ulp, at Dot2's cost in normalize, the renderer's commonest call, and every norm's bits changed for a gain of at most (n - 1) u.
  • Alternative: isect.h's tests compensated: their products more accurate, their epsilon the same, and their answers in an inner loop several times slower.

Review. Taken: for, every caller's old bits and speed one name away, the norms' bits and S256's and S301's guarantees untouched, and the kernels that divide a dot more accurate where the dot cancels; quat.h's fast rotation and isect.h's tests as fast as they were. Against: eight more names in the C core and the faces, and norm_squared(a) and dot(a, a) no longer the same bits. The alternatives: no _fast forms; the norms compensated; isect.h compensated. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S406. d_mat_<S>_solve refines its solution with extra precision, as LAPACK's dgerfsx does (Demmel, Hida, Kahan, Li, Mukherjee and Riedy, 2006): each column first as the elimination gives it, A's LU factors kept with their interchanges, then corrected from its residual b - A x, computed by Dot2 with x carried as a pair, x and its tail, the correction solved by the factors and added to the pair, until every entry's correction lies below half its unit in the last place, or neither the largest correction nor the largest relative to its entry halves (the last not added), or D_CFG_MATH_MAT_REFINE_STEPS steps have run; each entry is then x and its tail rounded once. For a system well away from singular, cond(A) u well below 1, each entry is the solution correctly rounded but where it lies within some cond(A) u^2 of a midpoint. The signature takes the factors' room, _scratch of n * n + 2 n entries and _pivots of n, so that a call written for the old one fails to compile rather than overrun a buffer of n * n; d_mat_<S>_solve_fast is the elimination alone, the old signature and its bits. The faces' solve(A, b) and solve(A, B) are the refined solve, solve_fast the elimination; the generic kernel's solve is its elimination, which needs no refinement for an exact type and has no wider sum to refine with for another, and the language's solves, which take the generic kernel, call it as solve_fast, their bits as they were.

  • Alternative: the old signature, the pivots kept in _scratch as values of the family's type: no call to change, and a caller's buffer of n * n overrun without a word.
  • Alternative: refinement without the tail, x alone: the classical refinement with an extra-precise residual, the solution to about an ulp, not to its rounding.
  • Alternative: LAPACK's whole procedure, with its normwise and componentwise error bounds returned: what a caller needs to trust an ill-conditioned answer, and an interface the faces do not have.
  • Alternative: the elimination in long double, rounded once: no refinement loop, and nothing gained for the long double family or where long double is double.

Review. Taken: for, the solution of a system well away from singular correctly rounded entry by entry - every entry of the 65 reference systems in this chunk's checks, 281, the 64 well conditioned and the one that is not, where the elimination alone gave 41 (raw/compensated.txt)

  • by the algorithm LAPACK and its analysis stand behind, its old bits under solve_fast, and no old call compiling into an overrun. Against: a C caller of d_mat_<S>_solve must change its call; and the cost, measured for double at 10 times the elimination's for order 3 and 7 for order 8 (raw/timing.txt). The alternatives: the old signature; no tail; LAPACK's error bounds; the elimination in long double. Recommended: as taken; error bounds, should a caller ask for them, in a form of their own. Taken.

S407. The refinement's bound is a knob, D_CFG_MATH_MAT_REFINE_STEPS, in a new configuration header, cfg_mat.h: 10 by default, LAPACK's ITHRESH, 1 or more. A system cond(A) u from singular gains about -log2 (cond(A) u) bits a step, so most converge in one or two; the bound matters only where the corrections neither converge nor stop shrinking quickly.

  • Alternative: 10, written into the kernel: one less header, and no way to trade the last steps for speed without editing the C core.
  • Alternative: an argument of solve: per call, and one more argument on every call for what is almost never changed.

Review. Taken: for, the configuration's convention, the knobs in cfg_*.h, one default a host can change without editing the C core; against, one more header. The alternatives: a constant in the kernel; an argument. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S408. The complex dense kernels stay as they were in this chunk: cmat.h's d_cmat_<S>_solve unrefined, and the products of matrices of math::complex<T> summed in order by the generic kernel, each product compensated by cplx.h's multiplication. The real kernels came first, being what the faces, the geometry and the renderer call; the complex ones need a Dot2 over pairs of real products for each part of each entry, and cmat.h's solve the same refinement with a complex residual.

  • Alternative: both now: every dense kernel compensated in one chunk, and this chunk's delivery later by the size of cmat.h's changes and their tests.

Review. Taken: for, this chunk delivered on the kernels every face calls; against, d_cmat_<S>_solve and the language's complex matrices as accurate as before, a complex solve no longer the real one's equal in accuracy. The alternative: both in this chunk. Recommended: cmat.h's solve refined and the complex products compensated in the next chunk, before 0.8. Done in chunk 50 (S410 to S412): cmat.h's products compensated and its solve refined, the generic path's and the language's complex products with them.

S409 to S413: chunk 50, 2026.11.15

S409. project and reject scale their operands at the range's ends, as S301 was ruled on 2026.11.04: in range - the squared norm d of the vector projected onto from MIN/EPS to MAX, the compensated dot p 0 or its ratio to d from MIN/EPS to MAX - each component p/d times the other's, as before, every bit kept; for the zero vector, or an operand with a NaN or an infinity, the formula as written; elsewhere each operand scaled by a power of two, its largest entry to [1, 2), the scaled dot by Dot2 over the scaled squared norm summed as norm_squared sums, each component that ratio times the scaled entry, scaled back by the first operand's power. Scaling by a power of two commutes with rounding in the normal range, so a projection whose result is in range is computed in range wherever its operands lie: a vector onto itself at either end is itself, where the formula made it NaN or 0.

  • Alternative: one path, always scaled: simpler, and the in-range bits changed, against the ruling's "every bit in range kept".
  • Alternative: the squared norm compensated too, by Sum2 of the squares: a more accurate ratio, and norm_squared's bits, which S405 kept as written, no longer the projection's.
  • Alternative: the ratio in long double: nothing gained where long double is double, nor for the long double family.

Review. Taken: for, the ruling of 2026.11.04 carried out: every bit in range kept, t_vec.c's 200,000 random pairs per family still the formula's, and 2,000 pairs per family moved past the range by powers of two each giving the in-range bits moved, every one checked, where chunk 49's formula fails 4,449 of those checks (raw/s301_probe.txt); a vector onto itself at either end itself, where the formula made it NaN or 0. S410. cmat.h's complex dense kernels are compensated and refined: d_cmat_<S>_multiply, new, rounds each part of each entry once from Dot2's pair - the real part's products re re and -im im, the imaginary part's re im and im re, each exact as a pair - and d_cmat_<S>_solve refines its solution as mat.h's does (S406), the residual's parts by Dot2 with the solution carried as a pair, part by part, until every part's correction lies below half its unit in the last place, neither the largest correction nor the largest relative halves, or D_CFG_MATH_MAT_REFINE_STEPS steps have run. The sums go through an accumulator vec.h now keeps, dj_internal_vec_<S>_acc, Dot2's running pair in the pairs the family sums in, for callers whose terms are not strided arrays of one family: added to by a product or a term, rounded once, scaled as the family's dot scales; with C++ overloads of one name for the three families. As vec.h's dot: operands past the range scaled by their largest part's power, a NaN or an infinity the formula as written, every product 0 the constant's parts plus 0, +0 for either zero. The solve takes the factors' room, (x, scratch, pivots, a, b, n, m), scratch n * n + 4 n, so that an old call fails to compile rather than overrun; d_cmat_<S>_multiply_fast is cplx.h's products summed in order, and d_cmat_<S>_solve_fast the old elimination, its signature and bits.

  • Alternative: each part as two real dots, re re and im im apart, then subtracted: vec.h's kernels as they are, two roundings a part, and the cancellation between the two dots unprotected.
  • Alternative: Gauss's product of three multiplications: fewer products, and an error that grows where the parts differ in size.
  • Alternative: the parts copied into real arrays, interleaved, and vec.h's dot called: the same bits (the checks show it), a copy per entry, and no form the generic path can call on math::complex<T>'s elements.
  • Alternative: the old signature, the refinement inside n * n of scratch: no room for the factors, the pair and the correction.

S414 to S423: chunk 51, 2026.11.16

The record: _handoffs/math98_chunk51_2026.11.16/README.md.

Chunk 51's decisions, S414 to S423: the first of the double family's rewrite, under the owner's rulings of 2026.11.16 at 09:26 on the decisions of 2026.11.15 - the double family on pairs of doubles, exp and log first, each in three phases with proven bounds (1, B); the eight rules (2, A); exp and log now, then 0.8's items, core v2 in December, then the rest of the family (3, B); the x87 refused for the family's functions (4, B); the lane's own algorithms, proven with Gappa and Sollya, where the lane had recommended CORE-MATH's (5, B); a public pair type (6, A); and kernels immune to fusing (7, B). His rulings on 0.8's items are in the reviews they rule: S413 (8, B), S391 (9a, A; 9b, C) and S403 (10, A), each "Ruled 2026.11.16", each outstanding until done before 0.8. Parse's M3 (11, A) is no decision of the lane's: math reads the opener's position from the note in the next diagnostic record, as its note of 11-13c answered, and its note of 11-16 says so. Chunk 50's decisions, S409 to S413, stand: his rulings of 11-16 followed its delivery.

S414. Every exact product is immune to a compiler that fuses a multiply and an add (ruling 7, B): wherever the target has a fused multiply-add for the family, an exact product takes its low part with an explicit one, fma(a, b, -high), exact, so the bits Dekker's product gives; where it has none, Dekker's product, which no compiler can fuse there; and in a C++ constant evaluation Dekker's product too, an explicit fused multiply-add being no constant expression for every compiler, the same bits. Found while preparing the decisions of 11-15: gcc fuses by default in its GNU modes wherever the target has the instruction, every ARM64 and x86 with a -march that has it, and a fused split * a - a breaks Veltkamp's split; built with gcc -std=gnu11 -O2 -march=x86-64-v3, chunk 50's compensated double dot was correctly rounded on 2,403 of 20,000 cancelling dots, its worst error 6.4e13 ulps. math_common.h's new section 5 says when the builtin may be called - D_MATH_{FLT,DBL,LDBL}_FMA and _FMA_OF, from gcc's __FP_FAST_FMA macros and x86's and ARM's own; in C, in C++ before C++14, where no kernel is a constant expression, and from C++14 where D_MATH_CONSTANT_EVALUATED keeps it out of a constant evaluation - and D_MATH_RUN_TIME() guards each call. The four exact products the generator writes take it - vec.h's, cplx.h's for each family, elementary.h's long double product and float square, and dd.h's (S417) -- and no error-free transform anywhere takes a product that is not exact. Every generated header opens with clang's #pragma float_control(push) and #pragma STDC FP_CONTRACT OFF and closes with the pop (D_MATH_PRAGMA_NO_CONTRACT, clang from 11), so that clang fuses nothing in them but under -ffp-contract=fast; gcc ignores the pragma, and there the exact products are safe by the above and the plain formulas, the _fast forms among them, follow the build (S415). check_math_tests.sh gains a third tier, fused, which builds every test as gcc and clang fuse: GNU C11, GNU C++14 and C++20 for the harnesses with a C++14 section, GNU C++17 for the faces, with the host's fused multiply-add (-march=x86-64-v3).

  • Alternative: fusing forbidden in the headers, by the standard pragma and gcc's #pragma GCC optimize("fp-contract=off"): gcc ignores the standard pragma, documents its own as not for production code, and stops inlining a function it marks, in every build.
  • Alternative: the needs list's X3 as written, -ffp-contract=off asked of every user and a self-test to report fusing: a build that misses the note is silently wrong, and the flag stops harmless fusing in the user's own code.
  • Alternative: Dekker's product everywhere, the build refused where it fuses: no macro says that gcc fuses - __FP_FAST_FMA says that the target can, which an ISO build with the instruction does not use - so the refusal would take every ARM64 build with the ones it must.

Review. Taken: for, every compensated kernel's exact products exact in every build, fused or not, with nothing asked of a user's flags; where the target has the instruction, an exact product two operations instead of some seventeen; clang told not to fuse in the headers, so that its plain formulas keep their ISO bits but under -ffp-contract=fast. Against: two forms of every exact product, both to be tested - the fused tier tests the explicit one, every other tier and every constant evaluation Dekker's

  • and, where gcc fuses, the plain formulas' and the _fast forms' last bits following the build (S415). The alternatives: fusing forbidden in the headers, which gcc does not honour in a header without losing inlining; -ffp-contract=off asked of every user, silently wrong where missed; Dekker's everywhere with a refusal where the build fuses, which would refuse every ARM64 build. Recommended: as taken. Ruled 2026.11.16: as recommended (decision 7, B).

S415. The double family's contract (ruling 2, A): one result, the correctly rounded one, on every platform, under the ruling's eight rules -- no long double inside; no C library calls; no fused multiply-add made by the compiler that changes a result, with a start-up self-test that says if one did; plain double arithmetic, so no x87; no dependence on a C library at all (wasm32); correctly rounded; within 2 to 3 times st_math's speed; and C's standard answers outside the domain. The roadmap's principle 4 and gate G6 say so. It holds for each of the family's functions as its rewrite lands, exp_c in this chunk (S416), and for dd.h's exact sums and products, exact in every build, its other results within their proven bounds, fused or not (S417). The plain formulas - every kernel written as its formula, the _fast forms among them - follow the build: where it fuses, their last bits may differ from an ISO build's, as ruling 7 has it, and d_math_formulas_fuse() says whether the translation unit's build fuses (S419). The float and long double families keep their contract until their turn, float from the double kernels (ruling 1); the plain double forms, d_math_d_exp among them, call the C library until ruling 12, due in early December.

  • Alternative: correct rounding alone, without the speed and no-long-double rules (the ruling's B): the same bits eventually, at 10 to 100 times the speed simterp needs each tick, so that the terps keep st_math beside it.
  • Alternative: the terps keep st_math (the ruling's C): two math libraries in one project, one whose pow is 120 ulps off.
  • Alternative: the plain formulas held to one result too, fusing forbidden: not possible for gcc in a header (S414).

Review. Taken: for, one answer on every platform and compiler, the best one, each function meeting the eight rules as its rewrite lands, so that the terps can retire st_math and share one library. Against: the largest block of work left, by the decisions' estimate 15 to 20 chunks; a 32-bit x87 build without SSE2 refused (S418); the plain formulas outside the contract where the build fuses; and until ruling 12 the plain double forms the C library's, a second answer beside each _c form wherever the library's is not correctly rounded. The alternatives: correct rounding without the speed and no-long-double rules; the terps keeping st_math. Recommended: as taken, and each plain double form the correctly rounded function once it is within about twice the C library's speed, as decision 12 recommends. Ruled 2026.11.16: as recommended (decision 2, A).

S416. d_math_d_exp_c is the exponential correctly rounded, in double arithmetic alone, by three phases each with a proven bound, the lane's own algorithm (rulings 1, B, and 5, B): x reduced to (128 e + j) ln2/128 + r, the integer nearest x 128/ln2 found by the shift 1.5 2^52, |k| < 2^18, ln2/128 in three parts L1 + L2 + L3, L1 and L2 of 35 bits so that k L1 and k L2 are exact, r1 + r2 exact but for 2^-113.9 and |r1| < 0.00271; then e^x = 2^e 2^(j/128) e^r, 2^(j/128) from a table of 128 pairs of doubles, each within 2^-107 of its value. Phase 1, in doubles, q of degree 3 by Sollya and T r1 split so that its high product is exact: within 2^-63.5 relatively, tested against 2^-63, k = 0 by its own bound; phase 2, in dd.h's pairs, P of degree 6, its first two coefficients pairs: within 2^-101.2 absolutely of a result below 2.01, tested against 2^-100; phase 3, in fixed point, 16 limbs of 16 bits in unsigned long, so that no 64-bit integer is needed: x exact, k ln2/128 from 240-bit digits, e^r by its series, times 2^(j/128) from 224-bit digits, within 51.4 units of 2^-224, 2^-218.3 relatively, then rounded at 53 bits or at the least subnormal. Each phase gives its result where the interval its bound allows rounds one way, as Ziv's method has it. The bounds are proven in the record's proofs/: Sollya's supnorm for the polynomials, Gappa for each phase's rounding errors from the code's own operations, every product a compiler may fuse modelled both ways, and exact rational arithmetic for phase 3. The hardest case known, Lefevre and Muller's 0x1.9e9cbbfd6080bp-31, lies 2^-110.6 relatively from a rounding boundary, far outside phase 3's bound. NaN gives NaN, x past 6243314768165359/2^43 (709.78) +inf, x below -6554261109157969/2^43 (-745.13) +0, and -2^-53 <= x < 2^-53 gives 1 + x; a result below the least normal is rounded to the subnormals at each phase's test, from the pair with its error. A constant expression from C++14 through all three phases. Measured (raw/timing.txt, gcc -O2): 9.9 ns a call over [-745, 709.7] against the C library's 6.7, 9.0 over [-1, 1] against 3.8; 57.8 ns where phase 2 decides; 3,950 ns at the hardest known argument, where phase 3 does; 22 to 27 times faster than chunk 50's exp_c on the long double kernel; phase 1 decides 99.86% of arguments over each range and phase 2 the rest, of ten million each, phase 3 none. The plain d_math_d_exp stays the C library's (S415).

  • Alternative: CORE-MATH's exp, ported with its proofs (decision 5's A, the lane's recommendation): proofs reviewed by specialists and the hard cases searched, and the licence notice in each ported file; the owner ruled the lane's own.
  • Alternative: chunk 50's long double kernel with a rounding test and an exact fallback (decision 1's A): correctly rounded too, at 149 to 597 ns a call, and slower where long double is 64 bits or 128 in software.
  • Alternative: phase 3 straight after phase 1: one bound fewer to prove, and the 0.14% that phase 1 leaves would pay phase 3's 3.95 us, some 5.5 ns a call on average, where phase 2's 57.8 ns costs some 0.08.
  • Alternative: phase 3 in triple doubles: a third floating-point bound to prove, where fixed point's is counted exactly and needs no 64-bit integer.
  • Alternative: a table of 256 pairs, or 64: a shorter phase 1 polynomial for twice the memory, or a longer one for half.

Review. Taken: for, exp correctly rounded on every platform in double arithmetic alone, each phase's bound proven from the code's own operations, fused or not, the hardest case known far inside phase 3's bound; 9.9 ns a call, 1.5 times the C library's over the range and 2.4 times over [-1, 1], and 22 to 27 times faster than chunk 50's exp_c; a constant expression from C++14 through all three phases; no 64-bit integer needed. Against: phase 3's 3.95 us at the arguments that need it, none of ten million random ones; a 2 KB table; proofs the lane wrote, which no specialist has reviewed, their risk held by Gappa's and Sollya's certificates, run again in this session (raw/proofs.txt), and by 2,398 references with every phase checked alone; st_math's own speed not measured here, where the needs list puts its functions at 9 to 36 ns a call; the plain form the C library's until ruling 12. Ruled 2026.11.16 (the owner's): the lane's own algorithms, decision 5's B, where the lane had recommended CORE-MATH's, A.

S417. dd.h is a public pair type (ruling 6, A): struct dj_math_dd {high, low}, normalized, with d_math_dd_of, _value, _neg, the exact _two_sum, _fast_two_sum and _two_product, and _add_d 2u^2, _add 3u^2, _sub_d, _sub, _mul_d 3u^2 (2u^2 fused) and _mul 7u^2 (5u^2 fused) by Joldes, Muller and Popescu (ACM TOMS 44(2), 2017) with their proven bounds, and _div_d 12u^2, _div 38u^2 and _sqrt 32u^2, the lane's, proven with Gappa; u^2 = 2^-106, relatively, where no operation overflows or underflows. The products become the paper's fused variants where the compiler fuses, the bits following the build and the bounds holding either way; the quotients and the square root are written so that no inexact product reaches an error-free transform (S414). Every function is pure double arithmetic, a constant expression from C++14; where double is evaluated wider, it refuses to compile (S418). t_dd.c, new, checks every result exactly in integers of 768 bits, 200,000 random arguments per operation, edges and normalization: the largest errors, in u^2, add_d 1.571, add 1.743, mul_d 2.375 (fused 1.702), mul 3.605 (3.500), div_d 2.333, div 6.118 (5.046), sqrt 2.808. Measured: two_product 2.1 ns, add 2.7, mul 2.3, div 6.3, sqrt 72.0, its square root elementary.h's correctly rounded d_math_d_sqrt_c.

  • Alternative: the pairs kept internal (decision 6's B): no names to freeze, and every user who needs them writes their own.
  • Alternative: every operation correctly rounded to the pair (decision 6's C): several times slower, for little a user gains.
  • Alternative: the paper's own quotients with their published bounds: tighter on paper, with products whose errors an error-free transform takes, which a compiler that fuses breaks.

Review. Taken: for, one tested pair type for every user, each operation within a bound proven for the code as written, fused or not, the exact sums and product exact in every build, constant expressions from C++14; t_dd.c's exact checks found every error within its bound. Against: names frozen once 0.8 ships them; the quotients' bounds, 12 and 38 u^2, and the root's, 32, proven but loose - the worst errors found are 2.3, 6.1 and 2.8 u^2 - which a function that leans on them must budget for; sqrt 72 ns, its first step d_math_d_sqrt_c, the correctly rounded root in pure arithmetic. The alternatives: the pairs internal; every operation correctly rounded to the pair; the paper's quotients. Recommended: as taken; tighter quotients, and the root by the instruction at run time, where a function needs them. Ruled 2026.11.16: as recommended (decision 6, A).

S418. The double family refuses, at compile time, a build whose double arithmetic is not plain double (ruling 4, B): where D_MATH_DBL_EVAL_WIDE and the compiler has the error attribute, each of the family's functions begins with D_MATH_DBL_REFUSE(x), a call, under if (x == x), to a function whose attribute stops the build with "double is evaluated wider than double: build with -msse2 -mfpmath=sse for the double family's correctly rounded functions"; so a translation unit that never calls one builds, and the float and long double families build as before. The condition keeps a constant evaluation of a NaN from reaching the call and clang from taking the function to have no constant value. exp_c, dd.h and d_math_self_test refuse; expm1_c's far path, which calls exp_c elsewhere, takes the long double kernel there, chunk 50's, with its bound, so that +inf and arguments past the overflow never reach the split, whose k would not fit an int (found by the handoff's zip check, t_exponential at -O0 on the x87: expm1(+inf)); the harnesses leave the family out with a note, and t_constants_face.cpp guards its double exp_c. Where the compiler has no such attribute, the functions document the bound they lose. S37 and S39 (step 39) stay, for the other families.

  • Alternative: the x87 supported as now, the drift documented (decision 4's A): silently wrong bits on those builds.
  • Alternative: a slow path through memory for the x87 (decision 4's C): several times slower, and fragile where clang keeps extra bits through a store (S37).
  • Alternative: #error in the headers where double is evaluated wider: every translation unit that includes exponential.h refused, its float users with the rest.

Review. Taken: for, no silently wrong answer from the double family on any build: an x87 build that uses it stops, the fix in the message, and one that does not builds as before, the float and long double families with it. Against: a 32-bit x86 build must add two flags to use the double family, and a processor without SSE2 cannot; a compiler without the error attribute gets documentation, not a refusal; expm1_c's far path keeps chunk 50's long double bound on the x87, not the family's. The alternatives: the drift documented; a slow path through memory; #error in the headers. Recommended: as taken. Ruled 2026.11.16: as recommended (decision 4, B).

S419. self_test.h, new: d_math_self_test() gives 0 where the double family's guarantees hold in the translation unit's build, as known results show, and otherwise D_MATH_SELF_TEST_PRODUCTS (1u), an exact product or sum that was not, D_MATH_SELF_TEST_EXP (2u), an exponential not correctly rounded, or both: three exact products and their sums against their parts, and exp_c at four arguments, one each phase decides, the hardest known among them, each read through a volatile; and d_math_formulas_fuse() says whether the build fuses a multiply and an add written as two statements: (1 + 2^-30)(1 - 2^-30) - 1, its factors volatile, is 0 unless fused. A header library cannot see its build: -ffast-math, a compiler that fuses where it should not, a target that rounds otherwise. So a program that needs the same bits calls the self-test once at start-up, in each translation unit whose build may differ, and stops where it is not 0. t_exponential.c holds it to 0 in every build of the tiers; it gives 3 under -ffast-math, by gcc and clang alike; and d_math_formulas_fuse is true for gcc in its GNU modes with the instruction and for clang under -ffp-contract=fast, false in ISO modes and in clang's GNU modes, which the headers' pragma governs.

  • Alternative: a test run on its own at start-up: C has no static constructor, and a header cannot run code no caller calls.
  • Alternative: every function tested at many points: a longer start-up for little more, as an exact product or sum that fails shows the build, and exp's four points take each phase.

S424. d_math_d_log_c is the natural logarithm correctly rounded, in double arithmetic alone, by three phases each with a proven bound, as exp_c is (S416; rulings 1, B, and 5, B): x = m 2^e, m in [1, 2), from its bits where the language can read them and by float_order.h's binade search elsewhere, a subnormal first lifted by 2^54; i, the integer nearest 128 (m - 1), picks c = 1 + i/128, and from i = 53, c > sqrt(2), m and c are halved and e raised by 1; inv the double nearest 1/c, 1 at i = 0 and 128, and L = -log(inv) from a table of 129 triples, its pair within 2^-107.07 of L relatively; r = m inv - 1 exactly as a pair, |r| < 2^-8, by an exact product, Sterbenz's lemma and Fast2Sum. Phase 1, in doubles: e LN2H exact, LN2H of 42 bits, L's high part and r taken by three Fast2Sums, r^2/2 split exactly, q of degree 5 by Sollya: within 2^-67.17 of log(x) relatively, tested against 2^-64; phase 2, in dd.h's pairs, P of degree 10, its first five coefficients pairs, ln2 in three parts, the first two of 42 bits: within 2^-101.48, tested against 2^-100; phase 3, in exp_c's fixed point and with its operations: e ln2 from exp_c's 240-bit digits of ln2/128, |L| from 224-bit digits, log1p(r) by its series, the positive parts and the negative ones apart: within 36.1 units of 2^-224, 2^-165.83 relatively, then rounded at 53 bits. Each phase gives its result where the interval its bound allows rounds one way, as Ziv's method has it. The bounds are proven in the record's proofs/: Sollya's supnorm for the polynomials, against their Taylor polynomials of degree 40; Gappa for phases 1 and 2 in each of three cases - e not 0; e = 0 and i neither 0 nor 128, where L and log1p(r) may partly cancel; e = 0 and i 0 or 128, where r = x - 1 - every value divided by |log(x)|, the magnitudes each case allows proven by log_consts.py against mpmath at 600 bits, every product a compiler may fuse modelled both ways; exact rational arithmetic for phase 3. log_gappa.py checks in exact arithmetic that each phase's test is sound: the proven bound and the rounding of lo -+ err within err. The hardest case known, Lefevre and Muller's 0x1.62a88613629b6p+678, lies 2^-118.03 relatively from a 54-bit number, far outside phase 3's bound. NaN and +inf give themselves, +-0 gives -inf, a negative x NaN, and 1 +0. A constant expression from C++14 through all three phases. The generator makes the table and the digits from integers alone, by ln's series of atanh, checking that each rounding is the same at both ends of an interval that holds the exact value; log_consts.py checks them again against mpmath. Measured (raw/timing.txt, gcc -O2): 18.2 ns a call over every binade against the C library's 3.9, 19.5 over [1/2, 2] against 4.0, 16.6 near 1 against 3.5, 17.6 over [1, 1e6] against 3.8; 117 ns where phase 2 decides; 2,940 ns where phase 3 does, at 1 + 6 2^-52; 8.0 to 9.6 times faster than the long double kernel's log_c; phase 1 decides 99.92% to 99.93% of ten million arguments in each of the four ranges, phase 2 the rest, phase 3 none. The plain d_math_d_log stays the C library's (S415).

  • Alternative: inverses of 8 bits, so that r = m inv - 1 is one double, exact by one fused multiply-add or by a split of m at 45 bits, as CORE-MATH's logarithm has it: no exact product of two full doubles and no low part of r, perhaps 3 to 5 ns a call less, for a longer polynomial, |r| then up to 2^-7.2; a design and proofs of their own.
  • Alternative: the long double kernel with a rounding test: correctly rounded too, at 148 to 175 ns a call, and slower where long double is 64 bits or 128 in software.
  • Alternative: phase 1 with r^2/2 rounded, not split: its rounding, 2^-61 of log(x) where L and log1p(r) nearly cancel, is past the test's 2^-64.
  • Alternative: a table of 256 triples: |r| < 2^-9 and q of degree 4, for a table of 6 KB.

S428 to S431: chunk 53, 2026.11.20

S428. The language's dense solves of real matrices, in float, double and long double, are mat.h's d_mat_solve, and of complex matrices cmat.h's d_cmat_solve: A's LU with partial pivoting, each of B's columns solved by it and then refined with extra precision as LAPACK's dgerfsx and zgerfsx refine - the residual by Dot2, the solution carried as a pair, until the corrections converge or stop halving - each part then rounded once, correctly rounded where A is far enough from singular, as la::solve's are (S406, S410), as ruled (8, B). Before, the real solve was decomp.h's LU and its substitutions, and the complex d_cmat_solve_fast, unrefined (S413). Exact solves keep the exact elimination (S257) and matrices of quaternions the generic one; a singular A is a pole, as it was. Measured (raw/s428_solve.txt), over 20,000 random 4 by 4 systems of doubles, entries uniform in [-1, 1], against the exact solution in rational arithmetic: unrefined, 23.03% of the solution's entries correctly rounded and 219 systems wholly; refined, every entry of every system; at about nine times the time, 1,201 ns a solve against 127 (gcc -O2, the C core's kernels alone, the machine otherwise idle). t_expr_face.cpp's and t_expr_complex_matrix_face.cpp's solves are checked against d_mat_solve's and d_cmat_solve's bits.

  • Alternative: the unrefined solve kept beside it as a second function of the language, solve_fast: speed for a user who asks, and one more name for 0.8 to fix, where an interpreter's user rarely feels a microsecond.

Review. Taken: for, the language's dense solutions what the faces give, each part correctly rounded where the system is well conditioned -- every entry of 20,000 random 4 by 4 systems, where the unrefined elimination rounded 23% correctly - with one kernel for the language and la::solve, so that one answer holds across them; a singular matrix a pole as before. Against: about nine times the unrefined solve's time on a small system, and the scratch of n^2 + 2n entries, 4n for a complex one; for an ill-conditioned system refinement stops where it no longer halves its corrections, so the gain is the conditioning's to allow. The alternative: solve_fast beside it. Recommended: as taken. Ruled 2026.11.16 (decision 8, B).

S429. rotquat(R) is the quaternion of the rotation nearest R, as ruled (9b, C): where R's elements are exact and R is exactly a rotation's matrix -- R^T R = I and det(R) = 1 in exact arithmetic - Shepperd's method as before, exact where the root of its largest candidate is rational and otherwise quat.h's from_matrix3 in long double (S391); every other R, dense or exact, by Bar-Itzhack's method (J. Guidance, Control and Dynamics 23(6), 2000): the eigenvector of the largest eigenvalue of the symmetric 4 by 4 matrix K that R's sums and differences make, K q = 3 q where R is q's rotation, by decomp.h's cyclic Jacobi to a tolerance of the type's epsilon times K's norm, normalized, w >= 0, in the elements' widest floating-point type, a long double where all are exact. A domain error where det(R) is not above 0, a reflection or no rotation, and where R lies farther than 1/2 from the rotation found, in the Frobenius norm, far from any rotation (a rotation's matrix is of norm sqrt(3)): s I is taken for |s - 1| up to 1/(2 sqrt(3)), about 0.289; NaNs for a NaN or infinite element. The docs - the README, rotation.hpp's banner, the function table and comparison.html - list MATLAB's equivalents, rotmat(q, 'point') and quaternion(R, 'rotmat', 'point'), with the Robotics toolbox's quat2rotm and rotm2quat, and SciPy's, Rotation.as_matrix and Rotation.from_matrix, as ruled (9a, A). Bar-Itzhack's eigenvector is the Frobenius-nearest rotation's quaternion, the polar factor's, with no SVD and no sign to fix; the gap between K's largest eigenvalue and the next is 4 for an exact rotation, so the eigenvector is accurate to some epsilons. A dense rotation's matrix now gives Bar-Itzhack's bits rather than from_matrix3's: t_expr_quaternion_matrix_face.cpp checks them against the test's own computation bit for bit and within 32 epsilons of the generating quaternion, in float, double and long double; a drifted 30-degree turn in double; an exact shear, [1, 1/1000, 0; 0, 1, 0; 0, 0, 1], in long double, the rotation by -atan(1/2000) about z within 8 epsilons; 1.1 I the identity's; reflections exact and dense, the zero matrix, 2 I and 1.3 I domain errors; and a NaN NaNs.

  • Alternative: a strict threshold, a few hundred epsilons: a matrix that is no rotation's refused early; and refused too where products of many rotations have drifted, the case the ruling's "far from any rotation" spares, against the lane's promise of 2026.11.15 of a generous one.
  • Alternative: the polar factor by an SVD, then Shepperd's method: the same rotation by two kernels and a determinant's sign to correct, where K's eigenvector gives it in one.
  • Alternative: Markley's method, SciPy's: the best-conditioned of four candidate columns, faster, and not the nearest rotation's in general.

Review. Taken: for, rotquat meaningful for every matrix a user is likely to have - a rotation's, exact or rounded, or one drifted through many products - by the nearest rotation, the answer the ruling asked, in one kernel the C core has, its accuracy set by K's eigenvalue gap of 4; an exact rotation's exact answer kept; a reflection or a matrix far from any rotation an error instead of a quaternion that means nothing; MATLAB's and SciPy's names in the docs, so a user arriving from either finds it. Against: a dense rotation's quaternion no longer from_matrix3's bits, so rotquat and quat.h's kernel differ in the last bits where they agreed; a cyclic Jacobi on a 4 by 4 per call, dearer than Shepperd's few operations; 1/2 is the lane's choice of "far", generous as promised, and an exact matrix that is no rotation's but near one now gives a long double, not an error. The alternatives: a strict threshold; the polar factor by an SVD; Markley's method. Recommended: as taken. Ruled 2026.11.16 (decisions 9a, A, and 9b, C).

S430. expression.hpp, the compile-time core, compiles from C++11 and is empty below it (N1), its kernels the C core's (N3), its placeholders in a namespace of their own (S403; ruling 10, A): step 29 (5a). From C++11 its leaves, nodes and operators, squared, abs and dot are constant expressions - each function one return, each return type spelled, a variable's argument picked by a small recursive template where std::get is not yet constexpr; exp, sin, cos, tan and sqrt are d_math_exp_c, d_math_sin_c, d_math_cos_c, d_math_tan_c and d_math_sqrt_c at compile time and at run time alike, constant expressions from C++14, where the C core's are; abs is exact, of -0 +0; x, y, z, t, r, rho, theta and phi are in djinterp::math::variables, brought in by using namespace djinterp::math::variables;, and var<I, T> stays in djinterp::math, from C++14. S432. log_c's reduction is cheaper and gives the same bits: the table's 127 inverses that are not 1 are rounded to 26 significant bits, within 2^-26 of 1/c, where they were doubles within 2^-53, and from i = 53 the table holds the inverse halved, so that m is never halved and e is raised by an integer test; m inv is exact as a pair by Dekker's product with m alone split, the inverse whole where its split would leave it so, or by a fused multiply-add where the target has one; and every positive normal x is taken in one test, x >= DBL_MIN and x <= DBL_MAX, the rest -- NaN, infinities, zeros, negatives, subnormals, the last lifted by 2^54 as before - after it; log(1) is +0 as the phases give it, with no test of its own. Every bound chunk 52 proved holds again (S424): |r| below 2^-8 at every i, at most 2^-8.011 from i = 1, as the slack between a c's neighbourhood and 2^-8 takes an inverse's error of 2^-27; the table's pairs within 2^-107.06 of -log(inv); each stored inverse of at most 26 bits, so that the product's split of m alone is exact (proofs/log_consts.py). The halving was the slowest part, a branch on i that a random argument's m mispredicts half the time; with the product's two splits, it was eleven of chunk 52's 20 ns a call. Measured (raw/timing.txt, raw/ identical.txt): with S433, 151 instructions a call where chunk 52's took 206 and the C library's log 66, and no mispredicted branch where chunk 52's had half of one; 12.2 ns a call where chunk 52's took 22.2, the C library's 5.3, over arguments from e^-690 to e^690 (gcc -O2), 1.70 to 1.85 times as fast as chunk 52's in each of nine builds and sets, 1.95 to 3.03 times the library's time, the machine otherwise idle, its times varying by a fifth from run to run where their ratio to chunk 52's does not; and its results chunk 52's, bit for bit, at 26,214,412 arguments, by gcc with and without a fused multiply-add.

  • Alternative: inverses of 8 bits, as S424's review named, so that r is one exact double: |r| then passes 2^-8, every polynomial and bound of phases 1 to 3 to be made and proven again, for a saving of the low part's few operations, where 26 bits give the product's saving with every bound kept.
  • Alternative: a table of 256, |r| below 2^-9: phase 1's polynomial of degree 5, at a table of 6 KB and every bound proven again.

S433. log_c's first phase sums rh^2 q(rh), q of degree 6 with q(0) = -1/2 (Sollya's, within 2^-65.05 of (log1p(r) - r)/r^2), where it split rh^2 exactly into a third exact sum; and it decides where the result is within 2^-90 of itself plus 11 2^-55 rh^2 of the logarithm, where it tested against 2^-64 of the result: its error is of the order of 2^-53 rh^2, from the rounding of rh^2 q, and of 2^-93 of the result besides, so that the test grows with rh as the error does. Proven in each of S424's three cases (proofs/): Gappa bounds the error's two parts, Z, of every part but rh^2 q's, within 2^-93.18 of |log(x)|, and W, of rh^2 q's roundings, within 2^-51.00 of it, checking as a rewriting that the computed sum less the exact one is Z + rh^2 q W; exact arithmetic checks the test sound at both ends of rh^2/|log(x)|'s interval in each case, the condition being linear in it. Phase 1 is within 2^-66.45 of the logarithm where e is not 0 and 2^-59.99 at worst, where rh^2 is largest against it. Over two million arguments of each set (raw/identical.txt), it leaves undecided 0.39% of those within 2^-7 of 1, more than chunk 52's 0.07%, the price of the cheaper sum; 0.01% of those over [0.5, 2], where chunk 52's left 0.07%; and none from e^-690 to e^690, where chunk 52's left 0.0002%. t_exponential.c's near-midpoint references, which chunk 52's phase 1 left all to phase 2, it decides 245 of 256, its error far below 2^-64 where rh is small. The rest of the phase is chunk 52's: e LN2H and L's high part, then rh, by Fast2Sum, rl (1 - rh), the low parts summed, rh^2 q added last. A test that chose its room by whether e is 0, a first form, gcc compiled to a branch whichever way it was written, mispredicted half the time over [0.5, 2] (cachegrind); the uniform test has no branch.

  • Alternative: chunk 52's phase 1 with S432's reduction: its bounds kept as proven, at some 15 ns a call, where S433 gives 10.
  • Alternative: the room chosen by e's being 0, 2^-66 or 2^-60 of the result: a select that compilers make a branch of, and 1.2% undecided near 1.

Review. Taken: for, phase 1 some 4 ns cheaper, its test no longer a fixed fraction but the error's own shape, so that it decides far more where rh is small and as much as before where it is large, with no branch; proven by splitting its error in two and checking the test at both ends of the one quantity it grows with; the near-midpoint references now mostly decided in phase 1. S451. A form is a constant expression from C++11 where it is one return: each derivative's tree, the higher derivatives and the gradient, the 5-point rule, the Taylor and Maclaurin evaluations, the arithmetic sequence and sums, and the scalar helpers abs to degrees, factorial by halving its product and gcd and lcm by Euclid's steps, each as deep as its type allows. A form that loops as many times as an argument says (the trapezoid, Simpson and Romberg rules and integrate, sum, partial_sum, geometric_sum, the geometric, power and Fibonacci terms and the partial sums that loop, binomial), reads a std::array (horner, numeric_partial) or calls exp, sin, cos, tan, sqrt, log or atan is a constant expression from C++14, and at C++11 the same code at run time. So every form keeps its bits at every level: a program compiled before and after the step gives the same results, form by form, at C++14 and C++20, where double is evaluated as double, log and atan apart (S453). The 5-point rule's one return adds its points in the loop's order, from 0.0, and gives the loop's bits at run time, fused or not; on a 32-bit x87 its sum is kept wider, as the loop's was not. numeric_partial reads its point through std::array's const operator[], a constant expression from C++14, where its copy and += needed C++17's.

  • Alternative: every loop a recursion, so that every form is a constant expression from C++11: a recursion as deep as an argument at run time, which a large count overflows, or a pairwise sum, whose bits differ from the loop's.
  • Alternative: every form from C++14 and C++11 given the declarations only: less than C++11 can express, against N1.

S452. On a 32-bit x87, where double is evaluated wider, log refuses to compile, as exp does (S418, S426). The header's constants are constants::of<double>'s, one spelling at every level, where the variable templates are C++14's.

S456 to S462: chunk 59, 2026.11.23

S456. reify(e), in expr/reify.hpp, makes a compile-time expression a term of the run-time language, expr_term, node for node in its shape, nothing rewritten: its constants numbers, its variables names, + - * / and unary - the language's operators, exp, sin, cos, tan, sqrt, abs, log, atan and dot calls of the functions of those names, the relations, and, or and not the language's, a piecewise a call of piecewise and a vector a vector literal, and from C++14 constants.hpp's rational an exact fraction. What the language has no term for, a parametric or an implicit function, is refused at compile time, with a message. The header is empty below C++11; math.hpp includes it, and expr/expr.hpp does not. The term prints by expr_render, simplifies by expr_simplify, substitutes and evaluates by name, and math::expr wraps it. Step 32 is done with it: the run-time derivative the step names is step 25's (4a), whose expr_derivative will take the term when it comes; until then a compile-time derivative, derivative<I>(e), is reified and simplified (S458, S461).

  • Alternative: a member, e.reify(), on every node: each node's header then includes the language's, which the compile-time core does not need.
  • Alternative: the compile-time core rebuilt on the parse module's static terms (expression_static.hpp), reified by its own reify<OpId, Atom>: another lane's module under the core, for one bridge.

Review. Taken: for, a compile-time expression printed, simplified, substituted and evaluated by name in the language, its shape kept, what has no term refused at compile time with a message; the compile-time core free of the language, the language's handle free of the core. Against: the run-time derivative the step names waits for step 25 (4a); until then a compile-time derivative is reified. The alternatives: a member on every node, each node's header pulling in the language; or the core rebuilt on the parse module's static terms. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S457. A name is anything a std::string is made from. A name the constant table holds, pi, e, im, jm, km, true or false, evaluates as the variable it is in the term, but its text reads back as the constant.

  • Alternative: a run-time list of names, with an error where it is short: every call's result an expr_result, for a check the compiler can make.
  • Alternative: x4, x5 and so on for indices past 3: names the caller never wrote.

Against: a name the constant table holds reads back from the text as the constant. The alternatives: a run-time list with an error; invented names past index 3. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S458. A constant whose value is an integer, of magnitude below 2^53 and not -0, is that exact integer, as the language reads 2 from its text; any other float, double or long double is its value in its type; an integer type's constant is exact, a bool the constant true or false, and a vec3 a vector literal. So expr_simplify collects a reified derivative's coefficients, the rules' 0.0 and 1.0 exact as it needs them: 1 * sin(x) + x * (cos(x) * 1) simplifies to sin(x) + x * cos(x). A term's text reads back as the same term's value, and in a typed fold the integer converts to the same double. -0, and integers from 2^53 on, keep their double, whose sign or size an exact integer would not keep.

  • Alternative: every floating constant its double: the simplifier, which collects exact coefficients only, leaves a derivative as the rules built it.

Review. Taken: for, a derivative's coefficients exact, so that the simplifier collects them; the term's text reading back as the term's value; the same double in a typed fold. Against: an integral double's kind exact in the term, where C++ held a double; -0 and integers from 2^53 keep their double. The alternative: every floating constant its double, a derivative left as the rules built it. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S459. reify rewrites nothing, and refuses what it cannot carry: a piecewise's last branch is the call's otherwise, its condition dropped, as the compile-time face evaluates it, never testing it; and arithmetic in an integer type, whose division truncates and whose overflow wraps in C++, as the language's exact integers do not, is refused at compile time, with the message to make a constant a double. A last branch that is otherwise(d) loses nothing; one that is when(c, d) is the otherwise it is in C++.

  • Alternative: the last branch's condition kept: a NaN in the term where C++ gives the branch's value.
  • Alternative: integer arithmetic reified as the language's: constant(1) / constant(2) 0 in C++ and 1/2 in the term.

Review. Taken: for, the term the expression's value wherever C++ gives one, nothing rewritten, integer arithmetic refused where C++ and the language differ. Against: a last branch's condition, never tested in C++, not in the term's text. The alternatives: the condition kept, a NaN where C++ gives a value; integer arithmetic reified, 0 in C++ and 1/2 in the term. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S460.   • Alternative: the language's functions the C core's kernels for reified terms alone: two answers in one language, before ruling 12 gives one.

Against: exp, sin, cos, tan, log and atan the C library's plain functions, one ulp from the C core's kernels at 1 to 56 of 20,000 arguments each, and at each the kernel the correctly rounded value. The alternative: the C core's kernels for reified terms alone, two answers in one language. Recommended: as taken, the plain functions made the correctly rounded kernels as ruling 12 decides. Taken in part: ruling 12, made 2026.11.24, switched the plain exp in chunk 63, and a reified term's exp is now the expression's, bit for bit (S478, S480); the plain log switched in chunk 66, and a reified term's log is the expression's too (S496, S498). **S463. The lazy type is linear_algebra/lazy.hpp, from C++11 and empty below it: lazy(x) wraps a vector, a matrix or a dynamic_matrix, and the operators on what it returns build an expression and compute nothing: + and

  • of two expressions of one shape, unary -, a scalar times an expression on either side, an expression of a vector divided by a scalar, as vector's / is, hadamard, the entry-wise product, the matrix product of a matrix by a matrix or by a vector, and transpose. eval(e), or e.eval(), computes it into a new value of the type eager arithmetic would give, and assign(dst, e), or e.assign_to(dst), into dst, each element once, with no temporary for each step. linalg.hpp includes it.** Nothing is lazy unless lazy is written: eager arithmetic is as it was, and an expression of lazy operands is a type of its own until it is evaluated.
  • Alternative: every operator of the types lazy, as Eigen's are: against decision 2, and a change to what every caller's auto holds.
  • Alternative: a lazy wrapper holding each operand by copy: a copy of each dynamic matrix, for an economy of copies.

Review. Taken: for, laziness where it is asked for and nowhere else, eager arithmetic unchanged, each element once with no temporary a step, the results of the types eager arithmetic gives. Against: a second spelling of the same arithmetic, lazy(a) + lazy(b) beside a + b. The alternatives: every operator lazy, against decision 2; operands held by copy, a copy of each dynamic matrix. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S464. A lazy element is computed by the same operations, in the same order, as the eager loops compute it, so that its bits are the eager ones: a sum, a difference, a negation, a scaling and a division element by element, an entry-wise product likewise; and a matrix product and a transpose by the eager operations themselves, once, before the elements, each product's entry the compensated dot of S404. Where the build may fuse a multiply and an add, gcc and clang on a target with a fused multiply-add, each product of the element loop is fenced, so that the next sum takes it rounded, as the eager loops, each storing its result, always do; where float and double are evaluated wider than their types, a 32-bit x87, every element is fenced, rounded to its type. With the fence taken out of a build that fuses, gnu++17 with -march=x86-64-v3, gcc's lazy elements differ from its eager ones at 54,931 of 262,000, clang's at none, its default fusing within one expression only (raw/measure/fence_out.txt); with it, at none. The fence costs a store and a load a product where the target fuses, and nothing elsewhere. On clang's x87 the eager fixed-size operations themselves keep the wider value across their steps, as S37 documents, and so differ from the lazy ones there, which round each step as gcc's eager ones do.

  • Alternative: a matrix product's entries computed in the element loop, each the dot of a row and a column of operands that may be expressions themselves: those recomputed for every entry.
  • Alternative: no fence, the lazy bits following the build where it fuses (S415): against principle 6, a lazy result another number than the eager one in the same build.

Review. Taken: for, the eager bits by construction, element by element, products and transposes the eager operations' own; the fence measured needed, gcc's lazy elements otherwise a fifth of them other numbers in a fusing build. Against: the fence a store and a load a product where the target fuses, which keeps the loop from vectorizing there; on clang's x87 the eager fixed-size steps, not the lazy ones, keep the wider value (S37). The alternatives: products entry by entry in the loop, operands recomputed; no fence, two answers in one build. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S465. The leaves hold their operands by reference, so an expression lives no longer than they do, and lazy of a temporary is refused at compile time; both operands of + - and a product are lazy, a concrete operand wrapped explicitly, lazy(b), and a scalar is any arithmetic type, converted to the element type as eager arithmetic converts it. Shapes are checked as eager arithmetic checks them: by the types for the fixed sizes, by assert for dynamic_matrix, a product's and a transpose's shape taken from their operands before anything is computed.

  • Alternative: a concrete operand lifted to a leaf on its own: a temporary's reference kept by an expression an auto holds past its full expression.

Review. Taken: for, no copy of an operand, no dangling temporary, nothing lazy unasked, shapes checked as eagerly as eager arithmetic checks them. Against: an expression's lifetime its operands', as with any reference. The alternative: concrete operands lifted on their own, a temporary's reference kept past its expression. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S466. assign(dst, e) writes each element of e into dst in place, dst taking e's shape, as an eager assignment would: where e reads dst, it reads it only at the element being written, element-wise, or before the first is written, in a product or a transpose, so that no temporary is needed. assign(a, lazy(a) * k + lazy(b)) and assign(m, lazy(m) * lazy(n) + lazy(m) * 0.5) give the eager a * k + b and m * n + m * 0.5.

  • Alternative: every assignment through a temporary, then copied: an allocation for each, which assign exists to save.

Review. Taken: for, assignment in place without a temporary, safe where the expression reads the destination, the destination taking its shape. Against: none measured. The alternative: every assignment through a temporary. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

A face test, t_lazy_face.cpp, checks the lazy type at every rung: 1 check below C++11, 12 from C++11, 7 on clang's 32-bit x87, where the fixed-size checks are left out (S37, S464); 64 of 64 builds of the full tier and 4 of 4 of the fused tier pass. It checks the types and shapes, and at 300 draws each element of vectors of double, float, long double and complex numbers, fixed-size matrices with products and a transpose, and dynamic matrices with products, a transpose and assign, against the eager arithmetic, bit for bit.

  • Alternative: the lazy checks in each type's own face test: the opt-in type spread over tests that need no C++11.

Against: on clang's x87 the fixed-size checks left out, as the eager side differs there. The alternative: the checks spread over each type's own test. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S474. Each section says one thing. HOW TO USE: how a caller reaches the header, included from C or C++ or through the header or face that includes it, and the names it gives, spelled as its family spells them. OPTIONS: what a run, or a caller, chooses, and how (an argument, a form chosen by name, such as _c or _fast); where nothing is chosen at run time, one line says so: "None: nothing in this header is chosen at run time." CONFIGURATION: the build's part, each D_CFG_ knob the header reads, with its configuration header, its default and any bound on it, the language levels it compiles at, as its own LEVELS paragraph has them, and any flag it needs, the x87's -msse2 -mfpmath=sse; where it has no knob of its own, it says so and what it compiles at. An umbrella says that each header it includes keeps its own level and knobs, as its banner says. The form is the guides': each heading over a row of dashes as long as it, after a blank line, the text indented three on its first line and one after, to 79 columns, the sections under the description and before the two blank lines above path:. The 91 hand-written headers that lacked the sections have them, 8 of the C core, the four configuration headers and 79 C++ headers, written by hand and each name in them checked against its code: the four knobs as their headers define them (D_CFG_MATH_MAT_REFINE_STEPS, 10; D_CFG_MATH_VALUE_ATOMIC_HOLDERS, 1; D_CFG_MATH_VALUE_WIDE_MALLOC and _FREE; D_CFG_MATH_EXPR_PROGRAM_STACK, 32), each included by dconfig.h and by the headers that read it. A header's revised: is 2026.11.24.

Against: 155 banners longer by about ten lines each, and their text a second place to keep right when a header changes. S483 to S488: chunk 64, 2026.11.24

S483. Ruling 13's helpers are a new header of the C core, c/modules/math/scalar.h, generated from one text for the three families (scripts/gen_c_math/scalar.py), with the C++ overloads that name each for every family: abs, copysign and is_finite; min and max, IEEE 754-2019's minimum and maximum, a NaN in either giving that NaN and -0 less than +0, as the expression language's are (S338); min_num and max_num, its minimumNumber and maximumNumber, which pass a NaN over for the other number, NaN only where both are; clamp, _x held to [_lo, _hi], max then min; lerp, as C++20's std::lerp is; and ldexp and frexp. The names are the C core's, d_math_d_min with _f and _ld beside it and d_math_min in C++. It includes float_order.h, whose exact powers of two ldexp_c and frexp_c use. S484. A helper the C library has comes twice, as nearest_integer.h's do: abs, copysign, is_finite, ldexp and frexp have a _c form, pure arithmetic, a constant expression from C++14, and a plain form, the C library's fabs, copysign, isfinite, ldexp and frexp; each is exact, so the two agree bit for bit. Those C99 lacks, min, max, min_num, max_num, clamp and lerp, come once, pure arithmetic, a constant expression from C++14. C++ before C++11 has no copysign or isfinite, so there those plain forms are the _c forms.

  • Alternative: one form each, pure arithmetic: ldexp_c scales by steps and frexp_c searches binades, several times the library's time, where the library's answer is the same bits.
  • Alternative: the plain min and max on C23's fminimum and fmaximum where a library has them: absent from C99 and from most libraries before 2022, and a NaN they return is quieted by arithmetic whose payload differs by platform.

Review. Taken: for, the library's speed where the library's answer is exact, the pure-arithmetic form for constant evaluation, the two the same bits, as nearest_integer.h's are; one form where C99 has none. Against: two names for five of the eleven. The alternatives: one form each, the library's time lost for ldexp and frexp; C23's functions, absent from most libraries and quieting a NaN by arithmetic. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S485. The edges, each to the bit. min and max give the first NaN itself, not one an operation quieted, so that the bits are the same on every platform, as the language's min does; a zero's sign, and a NaN's, is read by the copysign builtin GCC and Clang evaluate in a constant expression, as tangent.h's negative reads it, and elsewhere by 1 / _x, no constant expression there for a zero, a NaN counting as positive. clamp is max then min: a NaN in any of the three gives NaN, and a _lo above _hi gives _hi. lerp is Herring's (P0811), which std::lerp follows: exactly _a at 0 and _b at 1, monotonic, never past an end for _t in [0, 1], ends of opposite signs taking two products that cannot cancel; a NaN in any gives the first. ldexp_c scales down by steps of 2^(bottom + P), so that the one rounding that counts is the last, and frexp_c takes float_order.h's binade, a subnormal first scaled into the normal range; a zero, an infinity and NaN give themselves and exponent 0. is_finite_c is two comparisons, a constant expression at an infinity and at NaN, where _x - _x is not.

  • Alternative: a quieted NaN from min and max, as C23's are: a payload that differs by platform where both are NaN.
  • Alternative: clamp with _lo above _hi left undefined, as std::clamp has it: a precondition C cannot check, where one defined answer costs nothing.
  • Alternative: lerp as _a + _t (_b - _a) alone: it misses _b at 1 and overshoots near it.

Review. Taken: for, every edge defined to the bit: a NaN kept as it came, a zero's sign read as tangent.h reads it, clamp defined past its precondition, lerp as C++20's, ldexp rounded once. Against: away from GCC and Clang, a zero's sign at compile time cannot be read, so min and max of two zeros are no constant expression there. The alternatives: a quieted NaN, its payload the platform's; clamp undefined past _lo above _hi; lerp as the plain formula, past its end. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S486. t_scalar checks each helper to the bit, every family: abs_c, copysign_c, is_finite_c, ldexp_c and frexp_c, and the plain forms, against the C library at a million random patterns of float and of double, ldexp at scales from below the least subnormal to past overflow, and long double at two hundred thousand values; min, max, min_num and max_num against a reference written from IEEE 754-2019's words and, where glibc has them (2.35 on), against C23's fminimum, fmaximum, fminimum_num and fmaximum_num; clamp against max then min; lerp's promises at a million triples and, from C++20 where the target has no fused multiply-add, std::lerp bit for bit; C's special values, the C++ overloads, and from C++14 the constant evaluation. Its 16 builds of the full tier and 6 of the fused tier pass, 19.6 million checks each, 20.6 million where std::lerp is compared. **S495. log_c's speed step, its results the same bits: where the language reads a double's bits - in C with a 64-bit integer, by a union, and in C++ by __builtin_bit_cast, every build of the tiers - the reduction takes them alone: e from the exponent field, i, the integer nearest 128 (m - 1), as (f

Elsewhere the reduction is chunk 65's, by the binade's search. The reduction gives the same e, i, rh and rl as before at every argument, so each phase computes the same values and decides where it decided: mh inv and (m - mh) inv are exact, mh being of 27 significant bits and m - mh of 26, inv of 26; mh inv less its rounding high is a multiple of 2^-53 below 2^-25, exact; and that plus (m - mh) inv is m inv - high, a multiple of 2^-79 at most 2^-53, exact too. log_c takes 7.0 ns a call where chunk 65's took 10.2 (gcc -O2, every binade, by pointer): 1.6 to 2.6 times glibc's time across gcc, clang and g++, by pointer and inline, over every binade, [1/2, 2], within 2^-7 of 1 and [1, 1e6], where it took 2.4 to 3.6.** By compiler: 2.2 to 2.6 by gcc and g++ but on [1/2, 2], 1.9 to 2.0 there, and 1.6 to 2.3 by clang, glibc's log running here with fused multiply-adds; two runs, five of their 96 lines left out, each with a time more than a quarter off the other run's (chunk 66's record). Review. Taken: for, the rule kept, the cut at a section's heading, the exponential and the logarithm each in a part with its helpers. Against: a new file for the owner's tree, and the zip's list of exponential's parts longer by one. The alternatives: a small part for log_fast alone; the rule broken. Recommended: as taken. Taken.

S503. The tests: t_exponential's new d_tests_double_log_fast checks, at ten million random arguments - random positive patterns, the subnormals among them, 1 + d for d of every size from 2^-4 down to 2^-53, [1/2, 2] and [1, 1e6] - that log_fast is within an ulp of log_c, and, where long double holds 64 bits, within 0.52 of an ulp of the exact value as logl gives it near 1 and 0.53 elsewhere, to 2^-9 of an ulp, logl's own error; within an ulp of log_c at the 129 doubles about each of nine seams - the two ends of the range near 1, 0x1.6p-1 and 0x1.6p0, where k changes, 0x1.6p1023, where k reaches 1024, a row's edge below 1 and above, the least normal and the largest double; and log_c's value, bit for bit, at zero, the subnormals, the negatives, the infinities, NaN and 1. On the x87, the plain log and log_fast are the library's. Its 16 builds of the full tier and 6 of the fused tier pass, 264.7 million checks each, 174.8 million on the x87. The path without the bits was run once by hand, every check passing. tools/timing_c67.c measures log_c and log_fast against glibc by gcc and clang, plain and fused, and g++ (raw/timing_c67_1.txt and _2.txt).

  • Alternative: log_fast checked within an ulp of log_c alone: the bound proven but not measured.

Review. Taken: for, each path's bound measured as well as proven, where long double can measure it, about every seam the two paths and the table have, and log_c's answers where log_fast hands over; the x87's library forms checked. Against: the measure leans on logl, 2^-9 of an ulp of slack, and only where long double holds 64 bits; the path without the bits checked by hand, not by a tier. The alternative: log_fast against log_c alone. Recommended: as taken. Taken.
